Data balance is bounded (`-dlimit=10` = max 10 chunks per run, ~10 GiB) to prevent runaway IO. Both use `ProtectSystem = false` (balance ioctl needs raw filesystem access) + `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`.

**GC guard — NEVER gate reclamation on a % of device-unallocated (fixed 2026-08-21):** `btrfs-gc-guard` (ExecStartPre on `nix-gc` + `nix-build-cleanup`) originally blocked GC when `UNALLOC_PCT < 10` — on a 723 GiB device that demands 72 GiB of chunk-level unalloc, a level GC itself can NEVER restore (deleting files frees extents WITHIN allocated chunks; only balance returns chunks). Result: a 5-night deadlock (2026-08-17..21, root 96%, GC failing nightly while ~20 GiB sat idle, 44 generations accumulating). Correct gates (current): absolute `UNALLOC_BYTES < 5 GiB` floor (same figure the balance jobs use) + `META_PCT > 90` hard block (the real crash precursor). General rule: space-recovery automation must be gated on the resource it actually consumes (a few GiB of metadata churn room), never on a threshold the recovery action cannot itself satisfy.

**Manual `btrfs balance` at 0% unalloc froze the box (2026-08-24 crash #3):** root sat at 0% chunk-unalloc (gc-guard warned; both scheduled balance jobs correctly self-skipped), then a manual `sudo btrfs balance start -dusage=10 -musage=10` with zram 85% full + ClickHouse at 100% CPU drove IO PSI to 99% and froze the kernel DEAD in 2.5 min (journal stops mid-entry 06:20:52, no panic, WDT never fired — the scheduler-livelock class). Chunk-headroom recovery runbook (now embedded in gc-guard + both balance skip messages): (1) `rm /btrfs-emergency-reserve` to free extents FIRST, (2) wait for QUIET (zram <80%, low IO PSI), (3) `sudo ionice -c 3 btrfs balance start -dusage=5 -dlimit=2 /`, (4) re-provision the reserve. Both balance services carry Guard 0 since 2026-08-24: skip when IO PSI some avg10 ≥20% or zram ≥80%. The DAS was ALSO absent the whole 2-day boot (all USB disks missing since before the 2026-08-22 05:55 boot — physical reseat needed, `scripts/das-link-recovery-check.sh`) — an aggravator, not the freeze cause. Related: after crash reboots VERIFY `/run/booted-system` == `/run/current-system` — the 06:31 boot loaded an Aug 16 rollback generation (no XFS mount, no guards) and ClickHouse wrote telemetry to the ROOT fs for 13 min until the 06:42 deploy re-mounted XFS over it ("Directory to mount over is not empty") — shadowed data under the mount needs manual cleanup.

**Emergency reserve:** A 10 GiB `fallocate`d file at `/btrfs-emergency-reserve`, created on boot by `btrfs-emergency-reserve.service`. Delete it (`rm /btrfs-emergency-reserve`) for instant 10 GiB free space when you need to run balance, repair, or survive metadata ENOSPC. The file is NOT auto-recreated after deletion — manually re-provision with `sudo systemctl start btrfs-emergency-reserve` after recovery. Tracked via Prometheus metrics (`btrfs_emergency_reserve_present`, `btrfs_emergency_reserve_bytes`) and Gatus alerts on Discord if the reserve goes missing. **Snapshot-pinning caveat (T14, 2026-09-11):** the reserve's extents are PINNED by daily btrbk root snapshots — `rm`'ing it frees its 10 GiB only as the pinning snapshots expire (root pin window = 2w sharp — calendar-anchored, see the Snapshot-pinning doctrine above), not instantly, so "instant 10 GiB" holds only when the newest snapshots don't yet reference the file's extents (true at cold provision, weaker at steady state). A periodic rewrite timer was evaluated and NOT adopted: extra CoW churn for near-zero benefit while chunk-unalloc (~222 GiB as of 2026-09-02) is the real headroom — the `unalloc >10G` re-check stays the safety gate before any space-hungry op, and the reserve is the last-ditch margin, not the first. Full tree sweep table: **Snapshot-pinning doctrine** (top of this section).

### HDD Backup Pool & DAS Topology (evo-x2, 2026-08-17)

**`/mnt/pool`** — 2× Toshiba MG08ACA16TE 16 TB (sdb+sde, 912 power-on hours, near-new) in BTRFS RAID1 (`-m raid1 -d raid1`, label `pool`, uuid `f981fc51`), mounted BY-LABEL since 2026-08-27 (`/dev/disk/by-label/pool`, `noatime,nofail,compress=zstd,commit=300`) — was one member's by-id, which broke the mount whenever THAT member vanished even with the other healthy. VERIFY on first DAS return post-change: `ls /dev/disk/by-label/pool` must exist (rollback = the old by-id path, in git history). All four DAS disks share ONE USB link (`8-1`; the link's PCI controller RENUMBERS across boots — `c7:00.4` → `c8:00.4` by 2026-09-23, so the `zfs-vm-*` scripts resolve it at runtime from the JMS567 bridge `152d:0567`, never a pin — same doctrine as sd-letters) — time-slice backup jobs against buildcache-gc (Sun 05:00). **Never match DAS disks by KERNEL sd-letters in udev rules** (removed 2026-08-27: a retired-ZFS-pool `hdparm -S 120` spindown rule on `KERNEL=="sd[ab]"` letter-collided with a LIVE pool member + the buildcache SSD; letters reshuffle on every replug — match by ID_SERIAL if disk-specific rules are ever needed again).

- **Layout:** subvols `services/{immich,paperless,atticd,monitor365,discordsync}` (immich + paperless + atticd live; monitor365 populated 2026-08-18 via `data-to-pool-migration.service` while its service stays disabled — files carry stale uid 966:956, re-chown on re-enable; discordsync hosts the attachment archive since 2026-09-22 — see the DiscordSync section), `backups/`, `archive/` (private-cloud forensics, BitLocker rescue `desktop-u51ngkt`, cold `monitor365-archive` — archive/ is NOT btrbk-pool snapshotted: RAID1 is its only redundancy). The empty `services/browser-history` subvol from the 2026-08-16 layout is DEAD (2026-09-21 re-scope: its DB belongs on the Samsung hot tier, not the pool; btrbk row removed; subvol deletion needs root)
- **Backup tier on the pool:** btrbk root+data sends → `backups/{root,data}` (root receives kept FOREVER since 2026-08-21, `TimeoutStartSec=6h` seed-sized, mount-gated); `btrbk-pool` (23:45) snapshots pool `services/*`; `btrbk-pool-clean` (23:50 nightly + deploy-enqueued `--no-block`) runs `btrbk clean` on all three configs — the sanctioned GC for garbled (interrupted-receive) target subvols. A garbled target subvol with a snapshot's name BLOCKS re-sending that snapshot forever ("exists, but is not a receive target" → skipped backup); cleaning unblocks the automatic nightly re-send while local sources still exist (2026-08-21: healed the Aug 14/15 root gap this way; recurred 2026-09-18 via a manual Ctrl-C mid-`btrbk run`, same heal). Note btrbk `run` is SILENT during send/receive: per-action lines only fire at snapshot creation, progress lives in the journal's sudo command lines, and the exit transaction summary is the receipt (the 09-18 catch-up run: 2 root days + first hermes full send = 38 min under idle ionice). `forgejo-backup` (03:30, `forgejo dump` zip, 7d); `pocket-id-backup` (04:00, WAL-safe `sqlite3 ".backup"`, 14d); `browser-history-backup` (02:15, online `sqlite3 ".backup"` of the DynamicUser-owned DB, root + CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH, 14d); Twenty + Manifest pg_dumps via `backup.dir` in `mkDockerService` (`lib/docker.nix` — tmpfiles + `RequiresMountsFor` so a detached DAS FAILS the unit instead of contaminating `/mnt/pool` on the root fs); immich DB backup + paperless exporter land pool-side via their dataDirs. All registered in `backup-coordination`. `btrfs-verify-pool-backups` daily guard (mount + `btrfs device stats` + received-backup freshness by name-parsed date). **2026-08-18 findings:** (1) the guard's device-stats branch was a PHANTOM GREEN its whole life — `awk` missing from the unit `path` made the `if btrfs … | awk` condition false (pipefail inside `if` is non-fatal) so the mirror check silently skipped; fixed by adding `gawk`+`findutils` (unit scripts must list EVERY binary they exec). RECURRENCE 2026-08-31 (exit-127 class, worse than phantom-green): commit `fa9e56b7` added a PSI/zram Guard 0 using `awk` to BOTH `btrfs-balance-{metadata,data}` WITHOUT adding `pkgs.gawk` to their `runtimeInputs` — every balance run since ~Aug 25 died `awk: command not found` (exit 127, OnFailure alert) while root chunk-unalloc sat at ~6.4 GiB CRITICAL. The weekly ENOSPC-prevention layer was fully dead for a week; fixed by adding `gawk`+`coreutils`+`gnugrep` to both scripts' runtimeInputs. (2) `/mnt/pool/backups/data` has ZERO complete received subvolumes — every nightly `btrbk-data` aborts on the known /data EIO inode (docs/todo/storage.md P0, decided stance: keep failing until the corruption repair), and since 2026-08-20 it also dies `oom-kill` (page cache charged to the unit cgroup, 20.6G peak); root-tree sends are healthy
- **Restic app-dumps dedup repo (`modules/nixos/services/restic-app-dumps.nix`, 2026-09-22, DEPLOYED system-796 — password verified 0600 on the host; first nightly run 05:45 2026-09-23):** nixpkgs `services.restic.backups.app-dumps` mirrors the app-dump dirs under `/mnt/pool/backups` (incl. `browser-history` since the 02:15 dump landed) into one chunk-dedup repo (`/mnt/pool/backups/restic-app-dumps`, nightly 05:45+10m jitter, `--keep-daily 14 --keep-weekly 8`, ioTier.background, 12h TimeoutStartSec for the first seed) — layered ON TOP of the per-service dump units, whose freshness rows stay untouched. Password is a machine-local random file (`restic-app-dumps-setup` oneshot, deploy.sh provisioner-restarted, searxng-secret-key pattern — NO sops: the repo is pool-local until the offsite Borg leg, losing the host loses it either way; losing the password file loses the repo — it is at `/var/lib/restic-app-dumps/password`, `UMask = "0077"` on the unit after review-fix `6251c198` — live-verified `-rw-------`). Freshness via ExecStartPost `.last_success` marker + integration registry `backup` row (backup-coordination metric + the aggregate "All Backups Healthy" Gatus check). `initialize = true` means a lost pool re-initializes the repo on the first post-recreate run. Excluded by design: `root`/`data` (btrbk-owned btrfs receives) and `forgejo-subvol`/`github-voice-corpus`/`pixel6` (extend `services.restic-app-dumps.paths` if wanted)
- **DRIVE FREEZE — do not touch:** both SanDisk SDSSDA240G were FROZEN by user decision ("do not touch them; yet"). **2026-09-22 the freeze lifted for the two SanDisks**: the ssd-btrfs Docker earmark (never taken up — Docker data-root is /data/docker on QLC, verified) was DROPPED and both SSDs will merge into ONE btrfs filesystem at /mnt/buildcache (2-device `scripts/buildcache-btrfs-convert.sh`, awaiting a maintenance window — see the Build Cache SSD section). smartd watches both MG08 members (`-d sat`, by-id). The WOOACME W3A894-512GB (low-endurance, own front-port controller c7:00.3) is NO LONGER CONNECTED — zero enumeration in lsblk/by-id, no journal evidence in retained history (verified 2026-09-22); nothing in the config references it. If it ever reappears, the old freeze stance applies until the owner says otherwise
- **Vibration/shock baseline (2026-09-12, `scripts/hdd-vibration-check.sh`)**: FWTG PoH 1523, G-Sense 2; ZUFWTG PoH 1319, G-Sense 4; health PASSED + realloc/pending/uncorrectable/UDMA-CRC all 0 on both. G-Sense is a LIFETIME counter with NO timestamp — only same-drive DELTAS between dated runs are meaningful (2/4 over ~1.3-1.5k PoH = normal handling, not an active vibration problem). Disk_Shift raw on these units is vendor-packed 48-bit (FWTG `0x10140006`, ZUFWTG `0x140004` — millions-scale while normalized sits at 100); normalized ~100 + raw deltas are the signal, never the absolute. Live amplitude needs an external accelerometer (phone on the enclosure)
- **Pool SMART metrics (`modules/nixos/services/pool-smart-metrics.nix`, 2026-09-12)**: textfile collector (buildcache-metrics pattern: root + `CAP_SYS_ADMIN CAP_SYS_RAWIO CAP_FOWNER`, mktemp in the sticky dir) every 5 min → `pool_smart_*` gauges with per-serial labels + precomputed aggregate flags (`all_healthy`, `scrape_errors`, `media_flag`, `media_increased`, `temp_over`, `gsense_increased` — Gatus pat() can't do numerics). Deltas (media counters, G-Sense) come from a state file at `/var/lib/pool-smart-metrics/state` — first run re-baselines. Zero drives present keeps `all_healthy` untouched (whole-DAS outage is owned by Pool Mounted/DAS-link checks — no duplicated alert). **WHY: smartd's only remote channel rides the mail relay (dead until the Resend domain is verified)** — the Gatus checks "Pool Drives SMART/Media Counters/Temperature" (Filesystem group) are the remote death-alert path. G-Sense increases deliberately have NO Gatus check (fires exactly when the enclosure is physically moved = self-inflicted noise; dashboard + `gsense_increased` metric carry the forensics). deploy.sh restarts the unit post-switch (§10 metric-presence + smoke); post-deploy §13 validates the flags. Script logic fixture-tested via a fake `smartctl` on PATH (arg order: `-d sat -H|-A <dev>`). SigNoz: `systemnix-pool-storage` dashboard (18 panels; provisioner files carry the API-NORMALIZED spec — GET the live dashboard and write `.data.spec` back, or the provisioner PUTs on every deploy). All SigNoz dashboards now carry `refreshInterval: 5m` (was empty since bring-up)
- **Paperless:** admin password in sops `paperless_admin_password` (`platforms/nixos/secrets/paperless.yaml`), consumed via upstream `LoadCredential`; exporter manifests land pool-side via dataDir. Full v3 config (PG backend, NPU AI, Tika/Gotenberg, trash, barcodes) + engine-switch bootstrap trap: see the dedicated Paperless-ngx section above
- **CoW + backups mental model:** `rm` of migrated data frees nothing while 14d root snapshots still reference the extents (and a running btrbk seed is SENDING them) — plan space-heavy ops around btrbk retention; the 95% deploy-gate block on 2026-08-17 was self-inflicted by deferring a planned removal
- **DAS-outage recovery semantics (verified live 2026-08-31, 9-day outage):** NO manual re-seeding is ever needed. (1) btrbk auto-resumes incrementally from the newest COMMON snapshot (pool had @.20260820, local @.20260821 → tonight sends the missing ones); because btrbk is also the pruner, NOTHING expired during the outage — the parent chain survives intact. (2) `Persistent=true` timers catch up AT BOOT for windows missed while the machine was OFF — but a timer whose window fired (service failing on the mount gate) before shutdown does NOT re-fire at boot: btrbk-root fired 23:00 pre-shutdown → next run is the regular 23:00 slot, while data/pool/pool-clean (missed 23:30 windows while off) re-fired at the 14:30 boot. (3) During the outage NO local snapshots were taken either — the mount-gate failure aborts before the snapshot phase, so the NVMe 3d rollback window had the same hole. (4) Dump-style backups (forgejo/pocket-id/immich/twenty/manifest/cv) are regenerated fresh — nothing "old" to send; they went green at boot catch-up within ~30 min. Three bugs found + fixed same day: `cv-backup` 226/NAMESPACE (pool fs NEVER had `/mnt/pool/backups/cv` — a root-fs shadow dir under the mountpoint masked this all outage via early-exit "no pipeline.sqlite"; fixed with mount-gated `cv-backup-dir` oneshot, atticd-storage-dir pattern, + `RequiresMountsFor` on cv-backup, + deploy.sh restart list) and `paperless-exporter.timer` shipped without `Persistent` by nixpkgs (the only backup timer that misses boot catch-up; now overridden true in paperless.nix) and `cv-backup` SILENT NO-OP: root + `harden {}`'s empty CapabilityBoundingSet obeys DAC and cannot stat through cv-server's 0750 `cv:cv` `data/` dir → `[ ! -f $db ]` was TRUE with the DB present → exit 0 "no pipeline.sqlite yet" forever (the backup NEVER ran since deployment; the 226 masked it). Fix: `CapabilityBoundingSet = "CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH"` on cv-backup (backup-health-metrics precedent for root readers of foreign-owned trees). All of it is regression-tested in `tests/test-cv.nix` steps 9-10 (VM boots a real /mnt/pool disk via `emptyDiskImages`; the test's first RED run is what exposed the silent no-op — never trust a fix without the test that proves the artifact lands)

### Offsite Borg (Hetzner StorageBox, DORMANT until go-live)

**Implemented + DEPLOYED DORMANT 2026-09-22 (system-796), `services.offsite-borg.enable = false`** (`platforms/nixos/system/backup.nix`; runbook + go-live checklist: `docs/services/offsite-borg.md`; restore runbook + timed drill: `docs/services/offsite-borg-restore.md` + `scripts/borg-restore-drill.sh` — real/local/`--selftest` modes, stand-in-proven 2026-09-23, real-repo drill = go-live checklist step 9; hardened 2026-09-24: real-mode `mountpoint -q /mnt/hot` gate (unmounted Samsung would let borg cache onto the ROOT fs), record setup precedes every env check (early dies leave FAIL records), borg ALWAYS resolved from this flake's locked nixpkgs (drill == job binary), optional `--verify-data` deep-integrity phase (borg 1.x dry-run extract already chunk-verifies; the borg2 flag is used only when the binary supports it), records pruned to newest 100; the drill's real-mode `BORG_ENV_FILE` default is eval-time-pinned to `config.sops.templates."borg-env".path` by an unconditional assertion in `backup.nix` — drill-literal or template-path drift fails `nix flake check` naming the expected literal, so change the drill default and the template path together; the drift negative case is regression-tested in `checks.x86_64-linux.borg-restore-drill-fixture` (eval-time deepSeq guards: script-drift copy + template-path override both fire the pin, control proves the harness clean; case 8 "mutation-negative" adds the phantom-fixture guard — a sed-neutered drill COPY, root gate → true, sed-anchor rot-guarded, must invert the root-gate case; full 8-case harness replayed green against the real script standalone 2026-09-25 while the machine-wide `/run/binfmt` sandbox breakage blocked the literal derivation build — 2026-09-25)). The 3rd copy for 3-2-1: Borg 1.4.5 over StorageBox SSH port 23, `repokey-blake2` (passphrase = sops `borg.yaml` `borg_password` — a REAL random value since implementation; the owner must hold a recovery copy, policy open in docs/todo/storage.md). Dedicated ed25519 key from sops (`borg_ssh_key`, pubkey in the runbook for the Hetzner console) + `StrictHostKeyChecking=yes` against sops `borg_known_hosts` (PLACEHOLDER = fail-closed; pin via `ssh-keyscan -p 23` at go-live). **Go-live gate is owner-held inputs** (hostname/username — "Offsite Borg go-live inputs" row in docs/todo/storage.md): a go-live tripwire ExecStartPre fails the job with the checklist pointer while `BORG_REPO` (sops `borg-env` template → EnvironmentFile overriding the unit's remote-shaped placeholder) still says PLACEHOLDER, so nothing can half-deploy against a fake host. Job: nixpkgs `services.borgbackup.jobs.hetzner` (06:30 daily, Persistent, `auto,zstd,9`, prune 7d/4w/6m, `TimeoutStartSec=2d` for the WAN seed, doInit on first run). **Excludes are the sizing doctrine (BX11 = 1 TB)**: /data model/Steam/llamacpp/cache trees, code trees (GitHub-backed: `~/projects|forks|worktrees`, also an IO-storm guard against nightly full re-reads), pool btrfs receive mirrors (`backups/{root,data}` — the /data leg is EIO-corrupt and would fail the job), the restic repo (duplicates the dumps this job backs up directly), forgejo-subvol (re-mirrorable), paperless export/index/trash/consume, immich thumbs/encoded-video. Borg cache/config live at `/mnt/hot/borg/*` (Samsung tier — multi-GB caches on `@` would be btrbk-pinned forever; emergency-reserve doctrine) via the mount-gated `borg-offsite-dir` oneshot (deploy.sh provisioner list). Monitoring: `/var/lib/borg-offsite/.last_success` marker → `backup_healthy{backup="offsite-borg"}` + the aggregate "All Backups Healthy" Gatus check; the shared backup-coordination loop also emits `backup_ever_succeeded{backup}` (MTIME≠0 gate — never-worked is distinguishable from stale); the job unit carries ioTier.background via mkForce over nixpkgs' `IOSchedulingClass = "idle"` (idle starves on this box — a starved nightly job pages stale with no path to green); enable-gated smoke entries: pre-deploy §13 (tripwire/marker/env + the IO tier asserted on the eval'd serviceConfig — best-effort/6 must ride the go-live change; ioTier.background deliverable deploy-gated, not review-only) + post-deploy §16 (same wiring + the IO-tier grep on the deployed unit + the backups.prom row; the PLACEHOLDER verdict stays with the root-run tripwire — a user-run probe cannot read the 0400 env and would phantom-green) — both blocks extracted VERBATIM into `scripts/lib/offsite-borg-smoke.sh` (sourced by both gates; byte-verified parity) and fixture-tested by `scripts/test-offsite-borg-smoke.sh` (21 pass/fail/warn/skip/classifier branches through the sourced lib; §13's eval-failure path discriminates the DISABLED shape — the mkIf-wrapped job's "does not provide attribute …borgbackup-job-hetzner.serviceConfig" eval error — via `ob_classify_eval_error`, so only that exact shape SKIPs while any other eval failure FAILs LOUD with the raw error: the 2026-09-24 fix for the silent-skip where a daemon restart or config error read as "disabled"; §13/§16 were also UNRUNNABLE in the packaged gates until 2026-09-24 — the lib-extraction commit staged only metrics-gate.sh/pressure-report.sh into the `nix run .#pre-deploy-check`/`post-deploy-check` wrappers, so every deploy died at the §13/§16 source line) + the `offsite-borg-smoke-selftest` flake check + the `offsite-borg-positive-render` flake check (eval-time: renders evo-x2 with enable=true via priority-50 extendModules and deepSeq-asserts BE/6 + the tripwire/marker/borg-env wiring on the rendered job unit — the §a6-class hand probe is permanent, fires on every flake check incl. `--no-build`; note `lib.mkForce` is priority 50, so a drift override must be <50 to beat it, and configuration.nix's plain `enable = false` forces probe overrides to ≤50); OnFailure → Discord.

### Kernel & Network Hardening (2026-09-10, borrowed from paepckehh/nixos)

An external-repo study (github:paepckehh/nixos — a 532-module "company as Nix" repo with kernel/sysctl/sshd/known-hosts hardening in a central `siteconfig`) yielded a small adopted set, all in `platforms/nixos/system/`:

- **`boot.nix` `extraModprobeConfig`**: 14 exotic network protocols (sctp, dccp, tipc, rds family, x25, ax25, netrom, rose, ipx) hard-disabled via `install <mod> /bin/false` — blocks manual modprobe too (stronger than `boot.blacklistedKernelModules`, which only stops alias auto-load). Do NOT blacklist esp4/esp6 (future IPsec) or k10temp (temperature telemetry).
- **`boot.nix` sysctls**: ICMP-redirect/source-route/send-redirect deny (v4+v6), `tcp_rfc1337=1`, `icmp_ignore_bogus_error_responses`, `rp_filter=2` (LOOSE — strict `1` would drop packets during eno1→wlan0 wifi-failover windows; do not "upgrade" to strict).
- **`networking.nix`**: `programs.ssh.knownHosts.github` pins github.com + ssh.github.com to GitHub's ed25519 host key (verified against api.github.com/meta at pin time) — all git+ssh flake inputs/pushes now fail LOUDLY on MITM instead of TOFU. Key rotation = update the pin (GitHub rotates rarely; RSA rotated 2023-03).

**Evaluated and REJECTED from the same source (do not re-suggest):**

- `kernel.kptr_restrict=2` — breaks bpftrace/bcc kallsyms forensics, an explicit diagnostic tool class here; live value 1 (privileged-only) is the right tradeoff.
- `kernel.kexec_load_disabled` / `security.protectKernelImage` — kdump preloads its capture kernel via kexec_load at boot; disabling the syscall risks a systemd-sysctl↔kdump ordering fight and forfeits the 2026-08-22 crash-forensics capability.
- `kernel.sysrq=0` (their default) — full SysRq REISUB is deliberate on this freeze-prone box.
- `perf_event_paranoid=3` — would block user-space perf profiling on a dev workstation (live: 2).
- `linuxPackages_hardened` — breaks latest-kernel requirement for Strix Halo + r8125 out-of-tree module.
- Stateless root (tmpfs `/` + `/nix/persist`), AppArmor+`killUnconfinedConfinables`, scudo allocator, `nixStoreMountOpts=["ro"]` (breaks every nix build), journald volatile (needs persistent forensics), emergency mode disabled (stuck-boot history), zram writeback device (QLC storm class) — all wrong for this box's state-heavy, debug-heavy profile.

**Worth considering later (not implemented):** disko AUTOINSTALLER ISO (nixos-anywhere) — the document-only geometry part IS implemented (`disko/samsung-tlc.nix` + `checks.disko-samsung-tlc` eval guard + `tests/test-disko-layout.nix` VM rehearsal, runbook `docs/services/disko.md`; destructive disko is rescue-only on a blank by-id-verified Samsung, never applied to live disks); pointing some flake inputs at the EXISTING Forgejo mirror infra for GitHub-outage immunity (hagezi 404 precedent; tradeoff: builds then need Forgejo up); `nix.settings.allowed-users = ["@wheel"]` (lars ∈ wheel, verified); YubiKey PAM (u2f) for sudo/login — a YubiKey OTP+FIDO+CCID is ATTACHED (facter scan 2026-09-23; FIDO2 udev rules already shipped in `platforms/nixos/hardware/bluetooth.nix`), so this is implementable whenever wanted Their repo is far BEHIND SystemNix where it counts: zero VM tests, zero eval-time audits, no per-service systemd hardening, no monitoring — the siteconfig central-kernel-hardening idea was the one pattern worth taking.

### BFQ I/O Priority Tiers (evo-x2)

The evo-x2 has a 2TB QLC NVMe (Lexar NQ790) with an SLC write cache that BTRFS CoW churn exhausts within 22-47h. BFQ I/O scheduling prevents build storms from freezing SSH sessions and the desktop compositor.

**Tier system** (lower BE priority = higher I/O precedence):

| Tier              | Helper               | BFQ Class/Pri  | Services                                            | Purpose                                 |
| ----------------- | -------------------- | -------------- | --------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------- |
| Interactive       | `ioTier.interactive` | BE/1           | sshd                                                | SSH must always respond                 |
| Desktop           | `ioTier.desktop`     | BE/3           | niri, dms, pipewire, crush (wrapper)                | Compositor/audio must stay responsive   |
| Service (default) | `ioTier.service`     | BE/4           | Most services                                       | Default for unclassified services       |
| Heavy DB          | `ioTier.heavyDB`     | BE/5           | clickhouse, monitor365-server                       | Latency-sensitive databases             |
| Background        | `ioTier.background`  | BE/6           | signoz, discordsync, browser-history, ollama, attic | Standard daemons tolerating I/O latency |
| Build             | `ioTier.build`       | BE/7 + Nice=10 | nix-daemon, forgejo-runner, PMA                     | Batch builds and CI — lowest BE         |
| Maintenance       | `ioTier.maintenance` | idle + Nice=10 | fstrim, clamav                                      | Only runs when nothing else needs I/O   |

**Usage:** Import `ioTier` from `lib/default.nix`, merge with `harden {}` via `mkMerge`:

```nix
inherit (import ../../../lib/default.nix lib) harden ioTier;
# ...
serviceConfig = lib.mkMerge [
  (harden { MemoryMax = "1G"; })
  ioTier.background
];
```

**Crush wrapper:** The `crush` binary is wrapped with `ionice -c 2 -n 3 nice -n 5` (BE/3) via `writeShellApplication` in `base.nix`. Works in all contexts (interactive, scripts, MCP) — not just interactive shells like the old alias. Scheduled tasks (`crush-update-providers`) use the raw binary directly via `lib.getExe'`.

**DB-heal oneshot:** DiscordSync's 11 GB SQLite integrity check runs as `discordsync-db-heal.service` (Type=oneshot, RemainAfterExit=true) with its own 10-min timeout — extracted from ExecStartPre so deploy activation isn't blocked by DB recovery during build storms.

### Build Cache SSD (`/mnt/buildcache`)

**Module:** `modules/nixos/services/buildcache.nix` — SanDisk SDSSDA240G (240 GB, SandForce SF-2000, DRAM-less) on USB 3.0, ext4 `noatime,lazytime,commit=120,data=writeback`, `nofail` + automount. Deployed 2026-08-14.

- **Stale mountinfo after USB hot-plug (deploy 2026-08-16)** — When the buildcache USB SSD is hot-unplugged/replugged (or the enclosure power-cycles), the kernel reassigns the device letter (`sda1` → `sdc1`) but does NOT clear the existing VFS mount entry. Result: `findmnt /mnt/buildcache` reports `mounted on /dev/sda1` but `/dev/sda1` doesn't exist, and `ls /mnt/buildcache` returns `Input/output error`. The by-id symlink (`/dev/disk/by-id/ata-SanDisk_SDSSDA240G_174444471311-part1`) correctly points to the new device, so a fresh mount works — but the zombie mount prevents it. ext4 reports `errors_count > 0` and the superblock is in `emergency_ro,shutdown` mode (kernel forces read-only on detected corruption). `buildcache-init` chown fails with EIO, blocks `multi-user.target`, blocks activation. **Recovery:** reboot (cleanest, clears kernel mountinfo + remounts cleanly via by-id). Manual: `sudo umount -l /mnt/buildcache && sudo systemctl restart mnt-buildcache.automount && sudo systemctl start buildcache-init`. **Detection:** `findmnt /mnt/buildcache` showing a device letter that's NOT in `lsblk` = stale; or `cat /proc/self/mountinfo | grep buildcache` showing a major:minor that's not in `/proc/partitions`. Buildcache GC will continue trying to prune, but every write fails EIO until the mount recovers — silence is the signal, not a quiet success. **Phantom green (fixed 2026-08-16, recurrence #2):** during a 9-flap enclosure storm (usb 8-1 disconnected 9x in 36 min, 15:57–16:33), `buildcache_mounted` stayed 1 the whole time — the old collector check (`findmnt -o TARGET`) is satisfied by the zombie mount-table entry, and `df` serves stale in-kernel superblock numbers (reported usage 99%, unverifiable under EIO), so Gatus "Build Cache SSD" stayed green while every I/O failed. Fix: the collector now gates `mounted=1` on a real I/O probe (`timeout 15 ls -A "$mnt"`), verified live against the zombie — EIO ⇒ `buildcache_mounted 0` ⇒ Gatus fires. Your own shell cd'd into the mount is the main EIO spammer (`comm fish` in dmesg, millions of suppressed callbacks) — `cd` out before recovering. Failed `buildcache-init` (EIO chown) also blocks deploy activation — recover BEFORE `nix run .#deploy`.

- **Purpose:** All rebuildable build caches live OFF the QLC NVMe — the ephemeral build churn (64 GB Go build cache, Rust targets, npm/pnpm) was a root contributor to the SLC-cache-exhaustion WDT crashes and the 93% disk crisis. ~115 GB of caches moved.
- **USB hotplug self-healing stack (2026-08-16):** `x-systemd.device-bound` on the mount + udev rules (`power/control=on` for the JMicron JMS567 `152d:0567` bridge — known flap-under-load chip; `SYSTEMD_WANTS=buildcache-usb-recovery.service` on partition add via `ID_SERIAL`) + `buildcache-usb-recovery.service` (reaps zombie mounts via `systemctl stop` + `umount -l`, re-arms automount, verifies REAL I/O, runs init+metrics; also runs drive-absent: daemon-reload does NOT retroactively enforce device-bound on an existing zombie — verified live 2026-08-16). `buildcache-init` has `ConditionPathExists=device` so an absent/unflapped drive skips cleanly instead of failing EIO and blocking activation. deploy.sh starts the recovery unit after every switch. **The recovery service deliberately skips `harden {}`** — PrivateTmp/ProtectSystem create a slave mount namespace where `umount(2)` cannot touch the host mount table (silent no-op). Verified end-to-end on its first real event (18:37, "recovered: /dev/sdc1" in 4s). **CapabilityBoundingSet must include CAP_FOWNER (fixed 2026-09-18):** the recovery unit carried bare `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`, so the step-6 `/tmp/bc-fallback` sweep failed EPERM on EVERY run (the fallback dirs are lars-owned under the sticky-bit /tmp; sticky-bit semantics require CAP_FOWNER even for root — the memory-emergency-guard 2026-08-22 class) and the unit OnFailure'd on every deploy/replug. Now `CAP_SYS_ADMIN CAP_FOWNER CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE`. Same cap rule as the textfile collectors: any root unit that deletes/renames over foreign-owned files needs FOWNER regardless of harden{}.
- **Consumers:** `GOCACHE`, `GOMODCACHE`, `GOLANGCI_LINT_CACHE`, `PIP_CACHE_DIR`, `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH`, `npm_config_cache`, `CARGO_HOME` (set in `platforms/nixos/users/home.nix` — NixOS ONLY, not darwin). `CARGO_HOME=/mnt/buildcache/cargo` since 2026-08-17 (was the `@cargo` NVMe subvolume; seeded with registry/git/advisory dbs/bin/credentials.toml at migration). `~/.cache/goimports`, `~/.cache/go`, `~/.cache/go-build`, `~/.local/share/pnpm/store`, and — since the 2026-09-22 env-less-fallback sweep — `~/.cache/pnpm` (dlx/metadata cache), `~/.local/state/pnpm` (pnpm-state.json), and `~/.cargo/registry` (the env-less `CARGO_HOME`-absent path, converging onto the same registry the env'd `CARGO_HOME` populates) are HM symlinks into the mount. Rust `target/` dirs: `snapshots.nix` symlinks `~/projects/<p>/target → /mnt/buildcache/rust/<p>` (via `services.buildcache.rustProjects`). **pnpm 11 ignores `npm_config_*` env vars AND .npmrc for `store-dir`** — the store is redirected via the `~/.local/share/pnpm/store` symlink instead (mechanism-independent). Env-var coverage only reaches processes started with the new session vars — long-lived pre-change processes kept writing the old NVMe paths until the source dirs were removed on 2026-08-14. **`~/.cache/go-build` became an HM symlink 2026-08-16:** env-less processes (systemd user services, dbus-activated apps, emergency shells) fall back to Go's DEFAULT `~/.cache/go-build` — during the USB outage a real dir reappeared there and silently re-routed build churn onto the NVMe (5.4 GB in hours). The symlink converges env-less processes onto the mount, same mechanism-independence as the pnpm store link
- **Device identification:** `/dev/disk/by-id/ata-SanDisk_SDSSDA240G_174444471311-part1` — the kernel creates ata-serial by-id symlinks via SAT even though the USB bridge hides the model at the SCSI layer. Stable across sdb/sdc swaps between the two enclosures. SSD 2 (serial `174244451713`, btrfs, label ssd-btrfs) is UNMOUNTED/unused and joins the 2-device buildcache btrfs merge at the next maintenance window (Docker earmark dropped 2026-09-22).
- **Monitoring:** `buildcache-metrics` (every 5 min) writes `buildcache.prom` — mount presence, SMART health (`smartctl -d sat`), usage %. Gatus alerts on `buildcache_mounted`, `buildcache_smart_healthy`, usage >85%. The collector ALWAYS writes the .prom (even when the drive is absent) so a dead drive flips to 0 instead of serving stale greens. smartd monitors both SSDs with `-d sat`.
- **No TRIM through the USB bridge** (`lsblk -D` = 0B). If write perf degrades from stale-block pressure, reformat the drive — it's a cache (`mkfs.ext4 -L buildcache <device>`, then re-run `nix run .#migrate-buildcache` semantics).
- **Failure mode:** if the drive dies, builds fail with missing-directory errors until `GOCACHE`/`GOMODCACHE` are reverted in home.nix. Acceptable trade: Gatus alerts on Discord within minutes.
- **Corruption tolerance:** `data=writeback` + no PLP + SandForce SRAM means a dirty shutdown can corrupt cache entries. Go verifies content hashes itself; cargo/npm/pnpm just need a `clean`/`prune`. Never store anything irreplaceable here.
- **Cache-key unification (2026-08-15):** `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` + `GOEXPERIMENT=jsonv2` in `platforms/nixos/users/home.nix` sessionVariables collapse all Go compilation to ONE cache key. Before: `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` silently downloaded newer toolchains demanded by go.mod (go-codec's `go 1.26.6` → 240 MiB toolchain + 15k duplicate cache entries in one day), and per-repo GOEXPERIMENT split the cache 2x (75 repos flag-less, 70 flagged). `jsonv2` gates only the `encoding/json/v2` package (v1 byte-identical; without the flag a v2 import is a hard build error — verified go1.26.5). Drop the var when Go graduates the experiment (unknown experiment = loud build error). `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` makes go-codec fail LOUDLY until its floor aligns with nixpkgs — deliberate signal, repo was mid-upgrade (dirty tree, untouched)
- **Go's native trim is defeated by gopls:** the 5-day-unused LRU trim (`cache.go`) is mtime-based, but gopls's `markUsed` refreshes mtimes on every analysis pass — with 5 gopls instances nothing ages out, so go-build is effectively UNBOUNDED. Bounded by `services.buildcache.gc` (weekly Sun 05:00, `ioTier.maintenance`, runs as primaryUser): npm cache verify, pnpm store prune, stale rust targets (>14d), `go clean -cache` at ≥90% usage (nuclear guard — the disk can never wedge). rm (not trash) on stale targets is deliberate: trashing 30G of rebuildable cache would write it to the NVMe trash. **gc needs a `ReadWritePaths` hole for `~/.cache/pnpm`** (dlx + project registries — verified live: prune fails under pure `ProtectHome=read-only`). `TimeoutStartSec=45min`: `go clean -cache` at 100G+ scale is metadata-bound on the DRAM-less USB drive. **`pnpm store prune` in the gc unit needs `--store $mnt/pnpm-store` + `WorkingDirectory = mountPoint`** (2026-08-16): pnpm resolves its store relative to CWD/HOME state when `PNPM_HOME`/XDG vars are absent — a bare unit cwd=`/` made it try to write `/_tmp_*` and fail EACCES under `ProtectSystem=strict`, silently skipping prune every week (the Aug-15 "verified live" manual run inherited a caller CWD and masked this). ReadWritePaths holes needed: `~/.cache/pnpm` AND `~/.local/state/pnpm` (pnpm-state.json). deploy.sh runs `buildcache-gc` post-switch so every deploy verifies the prune path end-to-end
- **`buildcache-init` runs on EVERY boot (idempotent)** — mkdir/chown/chmod only. An init-once `ConditionPathExists=!…/.initialized` gate (removed 2026-08-15) made any NEW `buildcacheDirs` entry inert after first init (the sccache dir had to be mkdir'd by hand). It was the repo's only such guard (audited). Boot-time cost is trivial; RequiresMountsFor + ConditionPathIsMountPoint still protect the root fs from contamination. **Boot-time `status=15/TERM` on this unit is BENIGN (root-caused 2026-08-31):** at 16:37:24 systemd started the unit in the coldplug transaction, the automount/mount activation superseded its start job → SIGTERM to the 64ms-old process → retried and SUCCEEDED within the SAME second (journal shows Failed→Stopped→Starting→Finished all at :24). Once per boot, self-heals instantly, startLimitBurst untouched — journal noise only, do not "fix" boot ordering over it
- **Outage-displaced cache symlinks block the NEXT deploy AND re-contaminate the NVMe (2026-08-16)** — while the mount is dead/EIO, tools (and humans clearing caches) replace the HM out-of-store symlinks `~/.cache/{goimports,go,go-build}` with REAL dirs; env-less tools then build straight onto the NVMe (5.4 GB go-build + 1.2 GB root-owned `go-mod-fallback` with an auto-downloaded toolchain accumulated in one afternoon), and the next `nh os switch` ABORTS home-manager activation with `checkLinkTargets` "Existing file ... in the way". Self-heal stack: `buildcache-usb-recovery` step 2.5 + deploy.sh pre-switch both reap non-symlink occupants (exact names, `rm` not `trash` — rebuildable cache data) BEFORE activation; the `~/.cache/go-build` symlink converges env-less Go processes onto the mount. **2026-09-22 sweep extension:** the fallback surface now also covers `~/.cache/pnpm`, `~/.local/state/pnpm`, and `~/.cargo/registry` (HM symlinks + the mount-gated `migrate-buildcache-fallback-caches` HM activation block that pre-creates the targets on the SSD and removes real-dir occupants before `checkLinkTargets`); the reap stack is FOUR hand-kept surfaces (deploy.sh pre-switch `.cache` loop, the deploy.sh non-`.cache` loop for the two non-`.cache` names — added 2026-09-22 after a reviewer caught the missing pre-switch leg, `d60d598c`, without which the next deploy would have aborted at checkLinkTargets — `buildcache-usb-recovery` step 2.5 both loops, and the HM activation block); single-sourcing is queued. `buildcache-init` does NOT yet provision the three new targets (only the activation block creates them — post-recovery the symlinks dangle until the next deploy; queued).
- **2026-08-15 verification round (post-overhaul):** gc executed manually as the unit user — 12s, freed ~4G (npm 1.4G garbage + pnpm 2.5G/382 pkgs), 44%→41%; sccache proven end-to-end (clean rebuild 6.1s→2.3s, 12/12 dependency cache hits, store written to the mount); Gatus ≥85% alert lifecycle VERIFIED from the 96% event (TRIGGERED 03:37 → RESOLVED 21:58, both sent to Discord — alert delivery works for this path; monitor365 silence is a separate issue)
- **sccache for Rust:** `RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`, `SCCACHE_DIR=/mnt/buildcache/sccache`, `SCCACHE_CACHE_SIZE=32G` (home.nix; sccache in packages). Content-addressed cross-project compile cache: project B's serde is a HIT (rustc never runs), hard LRU cap cargo lacks — ends the per-project `target/` duplication growth (35 GB was mostly duplicated dep artifacts). Nix builds unaffected (sandboxed). Stale target dirs are cheap to delete — they rebuild from sccache hits
- **21 satellite repos broken for other contributors:** import `encoding/json/v2` but set GOEXPERIMENT nowhere in nix (devShell hard-fails without this machine's global env). List via `scripts/report-goexperiment-gaps.sh`; fix pattern = dnsblockd (`nix/devshell/default.nix` + `nix/packages/default.nix` `env.GOEXPERIMENT`)
- **2-device btrfs merge (DECIDED 2026-09-22, awaiting window):** both SanDisks merge into ONE btrfs fs at /mnt/buildcache (`scripts/buildcache-btrfs-convert.sh` rewritten for 2 devices: `-d single -m raid1`, ~447G raw, ~4x effective with zstd:1 — rust targets alone are 121G; XFS rejected: no transparent compression). Failure domain unchanged (both disks already share the ONE USB link + JMS567 enclosure). Mounting stays via DISK1's by-id path (pool precedent — btrfs auto-assembles members); the module flip (fsType/options, recovery `findmnt -t ext4`→`-t btrfs`) must deploy TOGETHER with the reformat in the same window. go-mod is staged/kept; everything else (rust targets, sccache) rebuilds on demand. **Source:** docs/todo/storage.md
- **Old `/rust-cache` (p9, ext4, 100 GiB):** mount + contents retired 2026-08-16. **Partition deletion SUPERSEDED 2026-08-22: p9 is now the LIVE XFS ClickHouse store (label `clickhouse` — see Filesystems gotchas). NEVER run the old `sudo sgdisk -d 9` deletion — it would destroy the live ClickHouse partition.** (Original 2026-08-17 decision: delete-only, freed space unallocated; p9 is the LAST partition — ends 176 sectors before disk end, only neighbor `/data` (p8), NOT root (p6, separated by the 1.1 TB p8 — "grow root" was physically impossible without relocating p8). Superseded by the 2026-08-22 XFS repurpose.) Remaining user-run cleanup if not already done: `sudo rmdir /rust-cache` + `sudo btrfs subvolume delete /mnt/btrfs-root/@go @npm @cargo`. The `@go`/`@npm`/`@cargo` automounts were removed from `snapshots.nix` 2026-08-17 — `CARGO_HOME` redirected to `/mnt/buildcache/cargo` (seeded 2.7 GB), `~/go/bin` (golines, templ) salvaged via `/mnt/buildcache/go-bin-salvage`, `@npm` was empty. `@cache-home` deliberately KEPT (see subvolume layout above).

## Critical Rules

- **NEVER commit a secret to a repo — not in status reports, not in commit messages, not in "manual step" command examples, not ANYWHERE.** The ONLY exception: sops-encrypted files (`platforms/*/secrets/`). Agents must never need the raw value: reference `config.sops.secrets.<name>.path`, render via sops templates, or write `<REDACTED — paste key interactively>` placeholders for manual steps. The user holds secret values; the repo never does. Enforced by gitleaks (staged-tree, blocking) + `scripts/scan-history-secrets.sh` (full history) — see the Secret Leak Incident section for what happens otherwise
- **Use flake commands** — `nix run .#deploy`, never raw `nixos-rebuild`/`darwin-rebuild`
- **`--keep-going` FIRST when a deploy is blocked** — when the toplevel build fails, run `nix build .#nixosConfigurations.evo-x2.config.system.build.toplevel --keep-going` (or `nix flake check --keep-going`) BEFORE fixing anything: one pass enumerates ALL root failures. Domino deploys (fix eval → switch → hit next FOD → switch again) wasted ~25 minutes across 4 switch attempts on 2026-08-27; the enumeration would have caught every domino in one command
- **Test first** — `nix flake check --no-build` (syntax) or `nix eval .#nixosConfigurations.evo-x2.config.system.build.toplevel` (eval)
- **`trash` not `rm`**, **`git mv` not `mv`**, **2-space indentation**, **`config.allowBroken = false`**, **No OpenZFS on macOS** (kernel panics, ADR-003)
- **Open new terminal** after deploy (shell changes need new session)
- **Land the replacement BEFORE removing the old source — and diff which entities existed ONLY there first** (2026-08-31 crush lesson): deleting a config file that still owns live entities (a model, a provider, a credential) breaks them even if a replacement is "coming"; `glm-5.3-flash` vanished and requests silently fell back to zai's pricier default until caught. Sequence: deploy replacement → verify parity (`crush models` before/after or domain equivalent) → THEN delete. Never assert success from output text alone — assert WHICH entity served it
- **A verification close-out must answer the question the item ASKED, not a sub-proof** (2026-09-18 browser-history gate class): "the binary gates on a fresh DB" does not answer "is the gate closed in prod" — the read-model count-gap is exactly the variable separating the two, and the prod probe returned 201 while the close-out headline claimed the gate live (a reviewer had to catch it; both sites corrected in `0e65881f`). When a probe MUTATES prod state or its first result contradicts the expected invariant, that contradiction IS the verdict and leads the close-out; a result observed once is "happened", never "can". Adjacent to "assert WHICH entity served it": assert WHICH question your evidence answers
- **Never write a secret VALUE into a command line — interactive shells included.** `sops --set '["key"] "VALUE"'` and `export KEY=VALUE` land verbatim in `fish_history` (plaintext, forever) — the 2026-08-18 Resend leak was exactly this pattern from an agent, and 2026-09-01 the LIVE minimax + zai keys were found in fish_history from USER command lines (purged via `printf 'all\n' | fish -c "history delete --contains '<prefix>'"`, confirmed 0). Interactive secret entry goes through `sops` (editor) or reads from `/run/secrets` — never inline
- **Never hardcode** `localhost:PORT` — derive from config. All ports in `lib/ports.nix`, all images in `lib/images.nix`. **Eval-enforced since 2026-09-14**: `port-registry-audit.nix` scans every unit's Exec*/Environment text for port literals (host/colon/flag forms) and fails `nix flake check` on any port not in `lib/ports.nix` — justified exemptions go in `services.port-registry-audit.allowPorts` with a comment
- **Docker images are ALWAYS on latest** (user directive) — pin `tag` + `digest` for reproducibility (postgres-sidecar pattern), never float. `scripts/check-image-updates.sh` validates `lib/images.nix` against Docker Hub (digest drift for pinned, semver for app images); `.github/workflows/image-updates.yml` runs it daily and opens an issue on drift. Floating unpinned tags are skipped by design — convert them to digests when they matter. DB-backed apps (Twenty) get migration review before major/minor-cascade bumps
- **Never silently substitute placeholder identity in git commits** — if `user.name`/`user.email` cannot be resolved, FAIL LOUD. Hardcoded `"Unknown Author"`/`"unknown@example.com"` fallbacks masked broken git config and produced ~6,400 unattributable commits. SystemNix's `services.projects-management-automation` sets `gitIdentity` which translates to `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` env vars on the daemon — env precedence beats config lookup, so the daemon always has a valid identity. See `docs/gotchas-archive.md` for the full incident.
- **Never force-enable a disabled service in the working tree "just to verify", and never manually activate a built toplevel** (`sudo ./result/switch-to-configuration …` or any activation outside `nix run .#deploy`) — a manual activation leaves NO profile generation (the 2026-08-18 00:33 google-sync 226 crash-loop ran for 47 min with zero generation trail; deploy.sh post-switch steps are also skipped). The SAFE verification pattern is a throwaway expression that never touches the tree: `nix build --impure --no-link --print-out-paths --expr 'let f = builtins.getFlake (toString /home/lars/projects/SystemNix); sys = f.nixosConfigurations.evo-x2.extendModules { modules = [{ services.X.enable = true; }]; }; in builtins.head sys.config.systemd.services.X.serviceConfig.ExecStartPre'` — if it fails with "string has context with the output 'out'", the error names the store paths; build the leaked `.drv^out` directly and run the real artifact
- **Concurrent agent sessions share this tree — attribute honestly, expect mid-edit races** — the auto-commit daemon batches MULTIPLE sessions' work into shared commits (2026-08-18: fastflowlm/smartd/data-to-pool rode the black-screen-followup commits). Rules: (1) when the tree grows changes you didn't author, flag it to the user immediately, don't silently co-verify them; (2) your "flake check passed" only covers YOUR files — the batch may break after the other session's next edit; (3) mid-session file modifications (`file modified since read` from edit tools, shifting line numbers) = another session is active: re-read before every edit, keep negative-test neuter/restore cycles ATOMIC (single shell command), and verify evals of shared surfaces (evo-x2, flake check) only at quiescent moments — a NEW module file on disk that is not yet `git add`ed makes EVERY evo-x2 eval fail with "attribute missing" (the tracked-files trap) until the owning session adds it; (4) when committing YOUR file out of a shared dirty index, use a PATHSPEC commit (`git commit -m … -- <path>`) — a bare `git add <file> && git commit` sweeps every other staged file into your commit AND push (12 CV files rode a push this way, 2026-08-30); (5) **daemon-race discipline: before AMENDING into an auto-commit, verify WHAT the daemon actually staged** (`git show --stat HEAD`) — the daemon batches parallel sessions' files, and an amend absorbs all of them into your rewritten commit (2026-09-14 footer-commit fragmentation)
- **History-rewrite verification checklist — run ALL of these after ANY amend/rebase/filter-branch/filter-repo that rewrites claimed history**: (a) `git merge-base --is-ancestor <new-sha> HEAD && echo OK` (the new history is actually reachable); (b) `git log --all --grep=<claim-text>` (the claim's message is present where expected); (c) `git for-each-ref | grep <old-sha>` (no branch/tag still pins the pre-rewrite SHA — a leftover ref silently resurrects the old history on the next push). Never assert "rewrite landed" from the rewriting command's exit code alone (the 2026-09-14 message-only-rewrite session). **AND re-run (a)/(b) after ANY later rebase/update-ref onto origin/master: an unpushed rewrite lineage is ABANDONED by rebasing onto origin history, silently resurrecting the old messages locally** (2026-09-15 recurrence: the `120ada36` gitleaks-message purge regressed this way — `0ae59e3b` un-ancestored, `120ada36` reachable again after the 09-14 10:00 + 09-15 04:11/05:08 rebases; while the push is held, local message rewrites are cosmetic — the durable fix is the push-time re-filter in the purge runbook, plus key rotation). **SAGA CLOSED 2026-09-15 (verification-only): NO further local message-only rewrites** — `120ada36` was re-verified REACHABLE ON ORIGIN post-push (`git merge-base --is-ancestor 120ada36 origin/master` → OK, origin `ad6edcbb`); the sentence dies only at the push-time re-filter (the runbook's replacements file is now BUILT INTERACTIVELY — the full secret literals were redacted out of this file 2026-09-15 after they kept the secret-history scanner red AND re-exposed the still-live Context7 key in a public tree) or goes inert via key rotation
- **Commit-message evidence rule: a claim of the form "tool X rejects Y" in any commit message, TODO, or report requires an ACTUAL RUN of tool X against Y in that session** — a fabricated rejection premise (the 2026-09-13 "gitleaks rejects bare 40-hex" claim, retracted 2026-09-15; its rule attribution was ALSO wrong: `sq0atp-` keys the SQUARE rule, `sourcegraph-access-token` gates bare hex on its own keywords) becomes tested invariant via `checks.x86_64-linux.gitleaks-coverage-selftest`
- **GitHub push protection IGNORES .gitleaks.toml allowlists — it pattern-matches raw blobs in the push range (2026-09-15 GH013 push block)** — the gitleaks-coverage-selftest fixture carried a handcrafted `sgp_<40hex>` literal (synthetic, allowlisted repo-side, passes pre-commit + CI) and GitHub still blocked the push on 4 commits; gitleaks allowlists never reach GitHub's scanner. Fixture/detector-test tokens must be TEMPLATES: `@HEX40@` placeholders substituted with a deterministic sha256-derived hex at scan time (flake.nix `expect_detect`), so no rule-matching literal is ever tracked; drift-mutations must still collapse ENTROPY (`sgpX_aaa…`), because `sgpX_` keeps the `sgp` keyword substring alive for the keyword-gated bare-hex alternative. If a literal that both gitleaks AND GitHub flag is ever unavoidable, the resolution is the push-protection unblock URL with reason "used in tests" (allowlists that one secret), not an improvised history rewrite. **2026-09-16 recurrence + unblock playbook:** a documented-FAKE Slack token QUOTED into a status report and hardcoded fixture components inside `scripts/audit-push-protection-literals.sh`'s own EARLIER iterations (`sgp_0123…`, bare `hex40='0123…'`) each blocked the push — fake is not an exemption, and one file carrying both a rule keyword and ANY 40-char component suffices. Status reports must SHAPE-DESCRIBE tokens (`xoxb-<12 digits>-<16 letters>`), never quote even fake ones; every fixture component must be runtime-derived (the audit script now also covers the Slack shape). Unblock playbook: backup branch → `git filter-repo --force --refs origin/master..HEAD --replace-text` (exact + `regex:` lines) → verify against the NEW-BLOB SET (`git rev-list --objects origin/master..HEAD | cat-file --batch-check` → grep prefix-required shapes) until flagged-hits=0 — working-tree greps and one `git log -S` sweep MISS historical file VERSIONS, so expect MULTIPLE passes; blobs shared with pre-range history are NOT rewritten (boundary blobs pass through — safe: the remote has them and never rescans) → `git town continue` finishes the interrupted sync
- **Prose rev citations use the SHORT-REV form (7-char)** — full 40-char SHAs only inside commands where git needs them. Evidence tables cite the short form; the full form is recoverable via the cited command (`git rev-parse <short>`)

---

## Non-Obvious Gotchas

> Full incident narratives, commit hashes, dates, and root-cause analysis are in [docs/gotchas-archive.md](./docs/gotchas-archive.md). Below are only enduring rules — things hard to discover from code alone.

### Git zero-byte object corruption (boot death mid-commit, 2026-09-15)

A boot death at 09:32:40 (mid PMA-daemon commit) left 10 loose objects as **0-byte files** while the loose ref + index metadata survived → every git command died `fatal: bad object HEAD`, git-town panicked (`branchesQuery` index-out-of-range on git's broken-ref error output). Recovery runbook (zero data loss, ~5 min):

1. `git cat-file -e <sha>` **false-greens on 0-byte loose files** (stat-only check) — validate with `git fsck --full`, never `-e`.
2. Real tip = the last `.git/logs/refs/heads/master` entry's new-sha (reflog entries are written before corruption; the lost commit's reflog line may never have landed).
3. Backup `.git` → tar in /tmp; `trash` the 0-byte objects; `git update-ref -m "recover: ..." refs/heads/master <reflog-tip>`.
4. Missing blobs referenced by the index = staged content: `git hash-object -w <worktree-file>`; a hash EQUAL to the index sha proves byte-exact restoration (it did for all 3 lost blobs).
5. `git write-tree` rebuilds the whole tree chain from the index AND repopulates the cache-tree ext — here it reproduced the lost root tree byte-exact (`0e45a87d…`, the exact sha the cache-tree pointed at).
6. fsck validates reflog OLD-shas too — after recovery, `git reflog delete <ref>@{0}` any entry referencing a permanently-lost object, or fsck errors forever.
7. Prevention now in `platforms/common/programs/git.nix`: `core.fsync = "loose-object,index"` (git does NOT fsync loose objects by default; on this box's crash history that is an unacceptable gamble).

### Multi-agent write discipline (2026-09-19)

- **Content-pin BEFORE every write**: `git rev-parse HEAD` + `git status --short` + `git log --stat` since your last known rev — parallel agent sessions commit continuously and may have touched YOUR target files in "heuristic" daemon commits. Diff-review anything new before editing.
- **When the daemon commits your in-flight work** (heuristic message, ~every 10 min): verify its contents with `git show --stat`, then `git commit --amend` the unpushed HEAD into a properly-messaged commit. Never `git reset`; amend-forward preserves the tree.
- **Lint-scanner FPs get fixed in the SCANNER, not suppressed at the call site** (2026-09-19 dead-guard-lint): the `$((` arithmetic form false-positived as a shell capture — fix = 1-char lookahead in the flake.nix awk scanner (`substr(line, RSTART + RLENGTH, 1) == "("` → skip), NOT `# dead-guard-ok` on the foreign line that tripped it (llama-rag.nix `leaks=$((leaks + 1))` was never touched).

### Big self-contained HTML reports (house pattern, decided + owner-ratified 2026-09-21)

- **Single file, zero dependencies — inline the JS.** Planning/status HTML artifacts follow the html-report-kit doctrine (self-contained, no CDN, no external fonts). When a JS library is required (mermaid), INLINE it — ~3.6 MB for mermaid v11.17.2 — so the artifact renders fully offline. Never CDN-with-fallback. Canonical bundle kept OUT of the repo at `~/.local/state/systemnix/mermaid-v11.17.2.min.js` (the shipped artifact itself is the in-repo carrier; `/tmp` copies die to the tmp cleaner).
- **One artifact per TOPIC — supersede, don't accumulate.** Each such file costs one multi-MB blob per git revision (history already carries a 7.9 MB inflated blob from the 2026-09-20 formatter incident). When a new visualization replaces an old one, add an in-file SUPERSEDED banner linking the successor (see `2026-08-31_samsung-disk-layout-visualization.html`), never keep two "current" pages.
- **Treat the inline bundle as GENERATED content — never format these files.** A prettier-family formatter inflated the 3.6 MB disk-layout page to 7.8 MB TWICE on 2026-09-20 (15:43-16:08, riding parallel-session daemon commits; no user-shell or PATH formatter involved, nvim carries no prettier). If the bundle must be replaced, splice it in with a script, then **verify with `bash scripts/verify-html-diagrams.sh <file>`** (headless render: SVG count, error-bombs, anchors) — the gate also catches the not-self-contained/CDN regression. **ENFORCED since 2026-09-21: `nix fmt` excludes `docs/**/*.html` repo-wide** — the `formatter` output in flake.nix wraps treefmt-full-flake's wrapper with a regenerated config (the upstream wrapper bakes its `--config-file` store path, so the config text is extracted from the wrapper, string-patched to prepend the exclude to the GLOBAL excludes list, and re-served; formatter programs/versions stay byte-identical). Nix gotchas in that override: `builtins.match` STRIPS store-path context — re-add it with `/. + path` or the file is missing in the build sandbox; and a text-extraction break (wrapper no longer carries `--config-file`) fails LOUDLY at eval by design. Recurrence signature if the exclude is ever lost: daemon commits oscillating a docs HTML between ~3.6 MB and ~7.8 MB (+/-200k-line diffs).

### Nix & Nixpkgs

- **SystemNix overlays can NEVER reach packages built inside followed input flakes — and a fix must CHANGE the affected drv path, evaluated from the CONSUMING flake** (2026-09-20, nodejs-slim saga): `hermes-agent`'s flake evaluates its packages against the FOLLOWED root nixpkgs via `inputs.nixpkgs.legacyPackages` — OVERLAY-LESS (chain proven by `nix why-depends --derivation`: toplevel → system-path → hermes-agent → nodejs-26-npm → nodejs-26.9.0 → nodejs-slim-26.9.0). Fixes for such chains belong in the input's own flake or via the lock. Verification doctrine: (1) **drv-path-diff** — any override/fix must change the failing derivation's path evaluated from the CONSUMING flake; eval-green alone is the weakest signal; (2) **harmful-shim-lifecycle** — a shim whose own comment says "drop once the lock carries it" and SURVIVES that condition actively breaks substitution (on nixpkgs 20b1ddd the nodejs shim forked a THIRD drv no cache serves, forcing 2 from-source nodejs builds; removed in `1bfe5ae2`).
- **`mkMerge` on flake-parts top-level `config`** — Does NOT work; use inline config or imports. `mkMerge` on `serviceConfig` inside `systemd.services.<name>` IS safe.
- **`//` on `serviceConfig` discards priority** — Shallow merge clobbers `mkDefault`/`mkForce`. Always use `lib.mkMerge [...]`. Enforced: `scripts/audit-serviceconfig-merge.sh` (pre-commit + CI, selftesting) rejects `serviceConfig = X // Y` source lines
- **`//` between module-config branches that share the `services` key keeps ONLY the last branch** (2026-09-14, integration.nix): `config = { ... } // optionalAttrs (...) { services.a.x = …; } // optionalAttrs (...) { services.b.y = …; }` is a SHALLOW merge — every branch carries the top-level `services` key, so all fan-outs except the LAST silently vanish (live: only the pocket-id branch fired; miniflux's vHost/checks/tile/backup/monitoring went dark while evals stayed green). Same class as the `serviceConfig = X // Y` rule, one level up. Combine cross-module config branches with `lib.mkMerge [ base (lib.optionalAttrs (options ? services.a) {…}) … ]` — never a `//` chain
- **A registry entry with `subdomain` + `port` + `vHost.layer != "none"` RENDERS A VHOST THAT OVERRIDES THE HAND-WRITTEN ONE** (2026-09-15, paperless): caddy.nix merges hand-written `virtualHosts` with the registry's `extraVHosts` — a registry plain-layer entry for `paperless` silently REPLACED the hand-written vHost and DELETED its `/admin/*` 403 hard-block (SSO-only defense), while evals stayed green and the vHost count was unchanged. Rule: whenever a vHost must stay hand-written (custom blocks: paperless /admin, seo GSC exemption, dnsblock redirect pair, monitor @noCache, timers file_server), the entry MUST set `vHost.layer = "none"` — it keeps DNS + tile derivation while caddy owns the proxy. Caught only by the baseline diff — never trust "evals green" for surface-preserving refactors (see the worktree-baseline gotcha below).
- **Duplicate entries in `monitoredServices ++ extraMonitoredServices` = duplicate metric series = node_exporter rejects the WHOLE textfile** (2026-09-15, pocket-id): the registry fan-out appends unit names to `extraMonitoredServices`; a unit left in the default list while its entry sets `monitored = true` produces two `system_service_state{service="X"}` lines — the whole system_health.prom goes dark and every dependent Gatus check reds out fleet-wide. Coupling rule: removing a unit from the default list and setting the entry's `monitored = true` are ONE atomic change. Final default list (2026-09-15): [monitor365, monitor365-server] only — disabled services keep default-list monitoring (missing-unit-tolerant) because their enable-gated entries vanish.
- **Surface-preservation baselines: eval a PRE-MIGRATION GIT WORKTREE, not /tmp files** (2026-09-15) — /tmp baseline JSONs die to tmp cleaners and go stale relative to lock churn; the reproducible method is `git worktree add /tmp/pre-mig <pre-change-commit>` + `nix eval` the same option paths in both trees, then set-compare (order-insensitive for endpoint/tile lists; `logoFile`-style store-path diffs from old-lock source hashes are noise, not drift). This is what caught the paperless /admin deletion — per-file "looks right" inspection did not.
- **`with pkgs;` hides missing attrs** — Falls through to enclosing scope on missing attrs. Use explicit `pkgs.` prefixes.
- **`lib.types.path` on multi-GB runtime files is doubly wrong — use `types.str` + quoted literals (llama-vlm, 2026-09-19)** — `types.path` copies the referenced file into the store when the config is built, and unquoted absolute-path literals (`modelPath = /data/ai/...`) abort pure eval with `access to absolute path ... forbidden in pure evaluation mode`. For values only ever string-interpolated into ExecStart args, declare `types.str` and quote the literals. A parallel session's fresh `types.path` declaration blocked EVERY deploy on the box (eval blockers are fleet-wide, not per-feature) until the type was flipped
- **`buildGoModule` silently drops unknown `env` attrs** — Only forwards a whitelist (`CGO_ENABLED`, `GOWORK`, etc.). Use `export VAR=value` in `preBuild` for anything else.
- **`buildGoDir` swallows build-constraint errors** — Returns 0 with empty output. Add post-build assertion that `$out/bin/` is non-empty.
- **flake-parts `mkFlake` does NOT accept a bare list** — Use `{ imports = [ ... ]; }` attrset instead. Nested lists crash evaluation.
- **`builtins.toString null` = `""`** — Does NOT throw. `config.users.users.<name>.uid` is `null` at eval time for `isNormalUser`. NEVER use it in slice/service names without null-checking.
- **deadnix `--fix` removes lambda params** — Does NOT add `...` when removing all params. Verify output leaves `...` in patterns whose callers pass extra attrs.
- **stdenv runs flake-check scripts with `shopt -s nullglob` — a command stored in a variable and expanded unquoted (`$strip "$file"`) silently DROPS every word containing glob chars, turning the check phantom-green (2026-08-27, `signoz-query-lint` v1)** — a `strip="grep -v '^[[:space:]]*#'"` loses its single quotes at expansion time (they are DATA, not syntax); the pattern word then contains `[...]`/`*`, matches no file, and nullglob REMOVES it — the pipeline becomes `grep -v <file>` with no pattern, outputs nothing, and every trap condition sees EMPTY input and passes. The build succeeds while the check guards NOTHING — verified by running the identical script text standalone (bash, no nullglob) where it correctly failed. Two rules: (1) indirect commands in check scripts MUST be shell functions (`strip() { grep -v '...'; }`) — quotes parse at definition time, no word-splitting of the command ever; (2) NEVER trust `nix build .#checks...` exit 0 alone for a check you just wrote — negative-test it THROUGH nix (`scripts/negative-test-lints.sh` is the persisted harness: copies the git-tracked set to a git-less flake source — plain-dir flakes see ALL files, no tracked-files filtering — applies one incident-shaped mutation, builds the REAL check derivation, and asserts the lint's own FAIL marker appears; an eval error does NOT count as a catch. Its first run caught module-shape-lint's `\b` accepting renamed wrappers). The static sweep `scripts/audit-shell-nullglob.sh` (pre-commit + CI) rejects the command-position-var class on every commit. /tmp paths don't exist in the nix sandbox — another silent-empty-input trap.
- **`nix fmt` is NEVER path-scoped in this repo and RE-LOCKS moving-ref inputs** — two distinct traps: (1) it reformats WHOLE files, so never run it while a parallel session owns the tree — stage your semantic edits and let the owner's fmt pass format (2026-08-27: `nix fmt -- <files>` reformatted 837 lines across gatus-config.nix + cv.nix under a parallel session, recovered only by luck); (2) plain `nix fmt` re-evaluates the flake and SILENTLY re-locks every moving-ref input (browser-history, CV, go-cqrs-lite ride `ref=master`) to current upstream heads, churning flake.lock as a side effect of formatting — always prefer `nix fmt --no-update-lock-file -- --ci` (checks formatting, zero lock writes), and treat any flake.lock diff after a formatter run as accidental churn to revert, never to commit
- **ONE formatter arbiter: pre-commit and CI MUST run the same nix formatter** — pre-commit formats staged `.nix` with the repo's own locked `.#formatter.$system` (treefmt/nixfmt-RFC), and CI arbitrates with `nix fmt -- --ci` over the SAME locked formatter. Until 2026-08-29 the hook ran floating `nixpkgs#alejandra` instead — a SECOND formatter with a DIFFERENT style — so every commit re-broke CI's fmt check with alejandra-shaped output and the tree oscillated between two styles (the "nix fmt --ci nondeterminism" red class; the drift was NOT treefmt version drift). Rule: never add another formatting tool for an already-formatted language; a new formatter means replacing the old everywhere in the same change
- **catppuccin-gtk + Python 3.14** — Overlay in `overlays/shared.nix` pins `python312`. nixpkgs default python3 breaks the build script.
- **nixpkgs 2026-09-15 removed `go_1_25`/`buildGo125Module` (EOL) — sops-nix master still calls `buildGo125Module` (2026-09-17)** — `overlays/shared.nix` carries TEMPORARY aliases `buildGo125Module = buildGo126Module; go_1_25 = go_1_26;` (sops-nix is evaluated against consumer nixpkgs, so its `sops-install-secrets` breaks every evo-x2 eval the moment nixpkgs drops the alias). Symptom: `error: Go 1.25 is end-of-life, and 'buildGo125Module' has been removed` "while evaluating the option `sops.package'".`nix flake check --no-build` PASSES while evo-x2 eval fails (flake check does not force the sops option) — always run BOTH. Drop the shim once sops-nix > 13616fff lands; until then a sops-nix input bump is a no-op (master IS 13616fff).
- **To strip a python package's checkInputs via `pythonPackagesExtensions` you MUST use `overridePythonAttrs` — `overrideAttrs` is a SILENT NO-OP for builder-level args (2026-09-17)** — `nativeCheckInputs` is folded into the derivation by `mkPythonDerivation` before stdenv's overrideAttrs runs, so the stdenv-level override writes a stray env var and changes nothing (control-verified: the drv hash stayed identical). Same class applies to any buildPythonPackage-only arg (`propagatedBuildInputs` interplay, `pyprojectFlags`, …). Control-test extensions with an observable (e.g. `pythonImportsCheck=""`) before trusting them.
- **microsoft RE-TAGGED playwright-python v1.63.0 + nixpkgs' playwright-webkit lost `libmanette` from buildInputs (2026-09-17 double break)** — both surface only on COLD builds (substituted paths keep working): (1) `django-polymorphic` carries `pytest-playwright` in `nativeCheckInputs` while DISABLING the tests that need it, dragging the re-tagged `playwright-python` source FOD into every paperless build (`Bad Request`-class hash mismatch); stripped via the `pythonPackagesExtensions` block in `overlays/shared.nix`. (2) `d2` uses the FULL `playwright-driver.browsers` bundle whose webkit member fails `auto-patchelf: libmanette-0.2.so.0`; d2 now gets the `browsers-chromium` preset (PNG export rides chromium). Diagnose graph entry points with `nix why-depends --derivation <toplevel.drv> <failing.drv>` (works on unrealized drvs; `nix derivation why-depends` does NOT exist). Drop both overlay shims when nixpkgs repairs playwright.
- **`applyPatches` now REQUIRES an explicit `name` when `src` is a fetched flake tree (2026-09-17 nixpkgs bump)** — the `_signoz-packages.nix` pattern `pkgs.applyPatches { src = inputs.signoz-src; patches = […]; }` (a `flake = false` GitHub input: an attrset with `outPath` but NO `name`) started throwing `applyPatches: please supply a 'name' argument` at EVERY `nix flake check` (derivationStrict forces the patched-src name), hard-blocking all deploys at eval. Fix: add `name = "<pname>-<shortRev>-patched";` to both applyPatches calls. Probe-depth trap when bisecting: `nix eval …goModules.name` does NOT force `src.name` (passed at the broken baseline); use `drvPath`, which runs `derivationStrict`.
- **Grep-only error capture drops Nix multi-line error BODIES (2026-09-05 deploy block with zero diagnostics)** — Nix ≥2.26 prints eval errors as a bare `error:` first line with the actual message on the following indented `… while …` lines; `nix flake check` output piped through `grep 'error:'` keeps ONLY the bare headline, so the pre-deploy gate printed a context-free `error:` and aborted. The fail branch now prints the raw output tail (`scripts/pre-deploy-check.sh` step 1). Same rule as the smoke-check lesson: when capturing errors for a gate, print raw context from the SOURCE, not just the lines your filter matched
- **Pre-commit flake-check can fail on GC-EVICTED flake-input sources — a docs-only commit that hits it is INNOCENT (2026-09-26, live docs-only block)** — the hook's eval leg died at `checks.x86_64-linux.hermes` with `error: path 'ix80s6aj697xcrzlnlc074wz6zd0avf4-source' is not valid`: a GC pass had evicted a flake-input source tree that only the hermes VM-test eval forces, and eval does not fetch. Heal that worked: run the failing check's eval once in isolation (`nix eval .#checks.x86_64-linux.hermes.drvPath` — fetches the missing sources from cachix), then re-commit. Root cause (which input, why evicted, prefetch leg) is queued in docs/todo/pipeline.md; do not misread the failure as your own breakage
- **`nixos-unstable` not `nixpkgs-unstable`** — Hydra only caches expensive builds (ROCm, CUDA) on the `nixos-unstable` jobset.
- **nix 2.34 daemon ABORTS when a substituter connect TIMES OUT (known upstream, open since 2020 — learnable from github:taciturnaxolotl/dots, verified 2026-09-10)** — nix#3768 "Crash (SIGABRT) when connecting to substituter times out" + nix#12871 "Abort in TunnelLogger::enqueueMsg" (stack: `ignoreExceptionExceptInterrupt` → `TunnelLogger::log` → rethrow → `std::terminate`): a failing narinfo worker sets the ThreadPool quit flag, the next worker's error-log write throws `Interrupted` from inside a catch block, the exception escapes the thread, and the whole daemon dies SIGABRT — clients see `error: Nix daemon disconnected unexpectedly (maybe it crashed?)` and every build fails until `systemctl restart nix-daemon`. NO nix.conf knob helps (`download-attempts`, `connect-timeout`, `--fallback` all tried by dots). OUR EXPOSURE: evo-x2 runs nix 2.34.8 with `https://cache.home.lan/monitor365` (attic, storage on the flaky DAS pool) — journal sweep 2026-06→09 shows ZERO hits so far, because our outage modes fail FAST (dnsblockd NXDOMAIN, or Caddy 502 when atticd is down) instead of connect-timeout; the abort race needs a HANGING connect (machine-wide IO storm starving Caddy accepts is the plausible trigger here). If the signature ever appears during an attic outage: root cause is this bug, not our config — dots carries a tested overlay patch (`nixVersions.nixComponents_2_34.appendPatches`, sets `allowInterrupts = false` on the daemon's TunnelLogger sink); adopt that shape rather than debugging from scratch.
- **nix-cache-pin evaluated and REJECTED (2026-09-10, source-audited)** — `github:caniko/nix-cache-pin` auto-pins nixpkgs inputs to revisions with full Hydra/narinfo cache coverage; well-engineered for its niche (closure verification, package version constraints, JSON plan transactions with fail-before-write, a `lockOnly` mode that updates ONLY flake.lock and preserves unrelated dirty nodes). Wrong fit for us: it targets `nixpkgs-unstable`'s cache gaps (we're on `nixos-unstable`, already Hydra-covered); its model is SEPARATE side nixpkgs inputs (`pkgsRocm`/`pkgsCuda`) while we have ONE root nixpkgs + 46 follows (pinning the root would hold the whole system back for the slowest package); the DEFAULT mode rewrites URL-rev pins into flake.nix (the `?rev=`-overrides-lock trap class — `lockOnly` avoids it but is opt-in); and adoption is genuinely zero (v0.1.0, ~6 weeks old, 0 stars/forks, single author, crate NOT published on crates.io despite its badge, issues DISABLED on the repo — PRs only, nightly Rust toolchain via the author's personal `rs-harbor` repo). (Custom binary caches ARE supported — per-pin `caches` option, Attic used in its own preset; an initial "can't see private attic caches" objection was falsified at source level.) Revisit trigger: a second nixpkgs input for a GPU overlay lands AND local-build pain materializes, or the project matures.
- **`import ../../../lib/default.nix lib` boilerplate required** — Each module self-imports helpers because `nix flake check` evaluates modules standalone (no injected args).
- **Pin policy (user directive 2026-09-16): `?ref=master`/`?ref=main` on every flake input unless upstream is BROKEN — hard rev-in-URL pins carry ONLY documented valid reasons** — a rev in the URL path (or `?rev=`) makes `nix flake lock --update-input <input>` a SILENT NO-OP, forcing every bump through flake.nix edits. **2026-09-16 audit outcome (gh-compare + goModules/package probes at each upstream HEAD):** pins DROPPED to `?ref=master` where the probe passed — md-go-validator, todo-list-ai (bun lockfile fixed upstream), go-nix-helpers (the /vN fix landed on master as `c42fd778`; consumer FODs file-and-image-renamer + crush-daily re-verified post-flip). Pins KEPT with re-verified evidence — upstream master STILL unbuildable (got-hash mismatch, 2026-09-16): library-policy (+47), go-auto-upgrade (+31), overview (+7), projects-management-automation (+9); DiscordSync (FIXED + LIFTED 2026-09-17: the staleness was repaired UPSTREAM — DiscordSync `df1a2bf0` refreshed BOTH goModules vendorHashes after the post-pin churn left them stale, protocol = probe got-hash → paste → push → re-probe green → re-lock; input is branch-ref governed (`?ref=master`), lock lifted c0604e46 → df1a2bf0 (+131 commits, new ADDITIVE `tursoSyncMonthlyBudgetBytes` module option, default 2.5 GB/month Turso sync breaker; `packages.default` builds green from our lock, `nix flake check` green. KNOWN upstream gap: `packages.cqrs-lint` still fails AFTER its FOD — "updates to go.mod needed", go-cqrs-lite cmd module drift, NOT consumed by SystemNix. Bump discipline: `nix flake lock --update-input discordsync --refresh` — --refresh is load-bearing, see the NAR-hash/daemon-cache bullet.
