(Evening re-lock 2026-09-17: a plain subtree re-sync moved the lock past df1a2bf0 to `5fe2af13`, FOD + package verified from our lock — the same hash-mismatch class returned when the input had moved without a subtree re-sync, so the update, not a manual node edit, is the durable bump verb); go-taskqueue (pinned rev 1a4eb480 NOT on GitHub — compare 404 — interim git+file continues, FLIPPED to `github:?ref=master` 2026-09-17: the push backlog landed as 1c48478 incl. the vendorHash fix; go-nix-helpers deliberately NOT followed, bank-sync FOD trap); qmd v2.8.3 is the current latest tag (no bump); nix-email FLIPPED v0.2.0 → `?ref=master` 2026-09-18 (v0.3.1 was the stale tag — master `b54f5c3` is flake-parts-migrated with its own CI + E2E suites, `checks.nix-email-contract` green against OUR nixpkgs via follows — the contract test IS the compat doctrine now, not a shared nixpkgs rev). **2026-09-17: the four "pre-2026-09-13-update" LarsArtmann pins FLIPPED to `?ref=master`** — each repaired UPSTREAM first, re-locked, and re-verified FROM OUR LOCK (all six build green, `nix flake check` green): library-policy `ef02247` (root cause was NOT vendorHash — the pinned samber-do-auditlog input carries a go.mod floor of 1.27.1; upstream set `goPkgAttr = "go_1_27"` — nixpkgs ships go_1_27 = 1.27.1 — and dropped the Go-1.27-graduated `goroutineleakprofile` GOEXPERIMENT, vendorHash refreshed), go-auto-upgrade `a6d1e65` (plain vendorHash refresh), overview `a0cfbc2`→`34217c2` (vendorHash refresh + a REAL compile break hiding behind a green FOD: a dep sweep re-bumped gobwas/glob to v1.0.0 while the project-discovery-sdk input sat pinned pre-API-migration — `undefined: glob.Glob` at package build; FOD green ≠ compile green. Fixed by unpinning the sdk input to `?ref=master` — its `e037066` carries the v1.0.0 API fix `glob.Compile`/`*glob.Pattern` AND a go 1.27.1 floor already matched by overview's `goPkg = go_1_27`), projects-management-automation `4b634211` (vendorHash refresh, no-follows wiring unchanged). **go-cqrs-lite: the `cqrs-lint-vendorhash-fix` BRANCH pin (d84e4d6a, diverged +247 behind master) FLIPPED to `git+ssh://…?ref=refs/heads/master`** — master `0b5813f45` carries a FRESHER cqrs-lint vendorHash than the branch (re-fixed at HEAD after the drift); the branch is superseded, deletion optional. **hierarchical-errors input RENAMED `erraudit`** (GitHub repo rename; same locked tree 7874d84 — zero hash churn; input name + URL + the `mkLarsPackages` attr all follow the new name; erraudit's pname was ALREADY `erraudit`). Wave lessons: (1) the PMA dep-sweep daemon keeps re-introducing the glob v1.0.0 break — the durable fix is fixing the CONSUMER upstream (project-discovery-sdk), not re-downgrading go.mod in every requirer (the 2026-09-05 downgrade class); (2) `nix build … | tail` chains mask FOD/eval exit codes — a `vendorHash.nix` syntax error produced a "FOD-OK" banner; always `set -o pipefail` (or capture rc) when piping build output through a filter; (3) verify pre-existing vs introduced with a `git worktree` BASELINE at the pre-change commit, and make the baseline probe AS DEEP as the failing check (`.name` eval does NOT force `src.name` — use `drvPath`, which runs derivationStrict). The signoz pair bump LANDED 2026-09-18 after its migration review (pins → `?ref=main` — SigNoz's default branch is main, NOT master; `nix flake lock --update-input` fails 422 `No commit found for SHA: master` on that repo class): signoz e0da06f7→67895d3 (+53), collector b514eb4a→a1d8ac3 (+8), vendorHashes + pnpm hash refreshed, sonic STILL v1.14.1 upstream so the Go-1.26 patches STAY (patch dry-runs verified against HEAD trees: signoz clean with offsets, collector hunk-1 rides fuzz-2 — same default as applyPatches). Migration verdict: ONE new metadata migration (pkg/sqlmigration/126_normalize_quick_filter_fields — transactional user-UI-state normalization, LOW risk), ZERO ClickHouse schema-migrator changes, config-surface safe (rendered signoz.yaml sets neither the removed `secondary_indices_enable_bulk_filtering` key nor `tokenizer`; the tokenizer default flips jwt→opaque = server-side sessions, fine for single-user impersonation — verify login post-deploy). All four packages build green from the new locks. Escape conditions for future flips: probe `nix build github:<repo>/master#default.goModules` (got-hash failure = upstream vendorHash stale = keep pin) — bun projects have no `.goModules`, probe `#default` directly, and AFTER a lock move re-build the input FROM OUR LOCK (`nix build --impure --expr 'let f = builtins.getFlake (toString /home/lars/projects/SystemNix); in f.inputs.<i>.packages.x86_64-linux.default'`) because `follows` can change the FOD inputs vs the upstream probe. The 2026-09-16 `ref+rev` flips (BuildFlow, branching-flow, file-and-image-renamer, browser-history, go-cqrs-lite, art-dupl) were converted to BRANCH-REF GOVERNED the same day: flake.nix carries only the ref, the lock holds the exact rev, updates flow via `nix flake lock --update-input`. URL edits require a matching lock `original` edit with `locked` byte-identical (a python sorted-keys/2-indent `json.dumps` round-trips flake.lock exactly — verified before writing).
- **`rev`/`narHash` are LOCK-ONLY fields — a flake.nix input accepts only `url`/`flake`/`inputs`** — pasting a lock node's `rev`+`narHash` into the input attrset fails EVERY flake command with `unexpected flake input attribute 'narHash'` (bb52c37b, 2026-08-30: a docs commit silently carried the broken edit; flake.lock already pinned the identical rev+hash, so deleting the two lines was the whole fix). To hard-pin a rev, encode it in the URL path and let the lock hold the hash (see the Pin policy above for when that is allowed).
- **flake.lock node-key mapping: root input `nixpkgs` maps to node key `nixpkgs_2`** — jq-ing the bare `nixpkgs` node shows go-nix-helpers' internal pin (harmless, looked like a "downgrade" during the 2026-08-16 bump scare). Read the lock's `root.inputs` mapping before comparing node keys to inputs; the failing command's own error output names the real rev
- **nixpkgs tarball lock regression (root cause fixed)** — The global flake registry at `channels.nixos.org` contains `exact: true` entries that rewrite `nixpkgs(ref=nixos-unstable)` → `tarball:channels.nixos.org/nixos-unstable/nixexprs.tar.xz`. When `nix flake update` runs with `use-registries = true`, it consults these entries and rewrites the flake.lock nixpkgs node from `type: github` to `type: tarball`. Registry overrides MUST use the registry's own key format (`from = { id = "nixpkgs"; ref = "nixos-unstable"; }`) — a combined id string (`from.id = "nixpkgs/nixos-unstable"`) is a DIFFERENT key and silently fails. **Fix (configuration.nix):** (1) `nix.settings.flake-registry` points to a local empty JSON file (`builtins.toFile`), eliminating ALL global tarball entries; (2) correct-format system registry overrides. Same fix mirrored in `platforms/darwin/nix/settings.nix`. **Recovery script** (if regression recurs): `bash scripts/fix-nixpkgs-lock.sh` (NOT `nix run .#fix-nixpkgs-lock` — flake eval fails when lockfile is broken). Eval-time `nixpkgsTarballGuard` in flake.nix remains as last line of defense.
- **Go submodule `replace => ../../sibling` breaks transitive consumers** — Published Go submodules MUST use published versions, not local replaces. `mkPreparedSource` only strips top-level replaces.
- **Go vendorHash mismatches are FOD** — `nix flake check --no-build` does NOT catch them. Batch-test individual Go packages before full builds.
- **Dual cargo build paths need different `outputHashes` keys** — crane uses full git URL key; `importCargoLock` uses `package-version` key. Same hash value, different key format.
- **`__intentionallyOverridingVersion = true`** — nixpkgs warns when `overrideAttrs` changes `version` without changing `src`. Set this flag in the overrideAttrs attrset to silence the warning when intentionally version-shimming (e.g., the libdisplay-info shim for niri-flake).
- **`go mod tidy` in `preBuild` for mkPreparedSource** — When `mkPreparedSource` adds `replace` directives for private deps, the Go dep graph shifts and `go.sum` becomes stale. Adding `go mod tidy` to `preBuild` (safe with `proxyVendor = true`) regenerates `go.sum` automatically, avoiding "inconsistent vendoring" errors. Also add any new `subModules` (e.g., `testhelpers`, `testhelpers/graphtest` from go-output) so Go can resolve them from the nix store path.
- **NAR hash differs between `github:` tarball and `git+ssh:` fetch for same rev** — The same git commit produces DIFFERENT narHashes depending on fetch type: `github:` tarball normalizes file permissions (all 0644), while `git+ssh:` preserves git's executable bits. If a flake input is fetched via `git+ssh:` (e.g., due to `git insteadOf` pollution) and later switched to `github:`, the narHash changes. The nix daemon caches fetchTree results in MEMORY by (url, rev) — a stale cached hash from one fetch type causes "NAR hash mismatch" when the lock file specifies the other. **All LarsArtmann flake inputs MUST have `go-nix-helpers.follows = "go-nix-helpers"`** to avoid independent `git+ssh:` locked versions that diverge from the top-level `github:` fetch. **Daemon cache recovery:** When the daemon has a cached `git+ssh:` hash, BOTH `nix flake lock --update-input` AND `nix flake prefetch` return the STALE cached hash, not the GitHub tarball hash. `nix flake check --no-build` passes with the stale hash (daemon serves cached store path), but `nix fmt` or any operation that triggers a fresh GitHub fetch breaks. The ONLY fix is `sudo systemctl restart nix-daemon` to clear the in-memory cache, then `nix flake lock --update-input go-nix-helpers` to re-lock with the correct GitHub tarball hash. Without sudo access, use the `--refresh` flake-command flag (`nix flake lock --update-input <input> --refresh`, `nix build <ref-url> --refresh`) — it forces a re-fetch and bypasses the cache entirely; VERIFIED 2026-09-17 on the DiscordSync master lift, where the daemon kept serving the PRE-PUSH `ref=master`→old-rev resolution until a `--refresh` probe/lock (the restart remains the only path for commands without the flag). **Follows encoding issue:** `nix flake lock --update-input` does NOT re-encode `follows` overrides in the root node of flake.lock — only a full `nix flake lock` (no `--update-input`) processes all follows from flake.nix. If follows are missing from the lock file root node, consumer flakes use their own independent go-nix-helpers lock entries instead of the root's pin

- **Daemon stale-fetch cache makes SAME-REV evals DIVERGE across flakes (2026-09-02 machine finding)** — the nix daemon can serve a STALE source tree for an already-locked rev: the same nixpkgs rev `34ab99075` evaluated `go_1_26 = 1.26.7` via SystemNix but `1.26.5` via PMA — different store paths from one lock rev, no eval error. Symptom downstream: a go-mod floor past the served toolchain fails the `-go-modules` FOD with `go.mod requires go >= 1.26.x` while an identical tree builds fine from another flake. Only fix: `sudo systemctl restart nix-daemon` (in-memory fetch cache); no-sudo workaround is the content-addressed `goTarballVersion`/`goTarballHash` pin (builds go from the go.dev tarball, immune to source-cache staleness). Per-consumer probe before blaming go.mod: `nix eval <flake>#<pkg>.go.env.GO_VERSION`-style checks per flake, compare against the same rev elsewhere.
- **Measure vendorHash against CLEAN HEAD, never a dirty worktree (2026-09-02)** — a vendorHash computed from a dirty worktree embeds the dirt's module set (uncommitted imports, WIP replaces); it fails the moment the FOD builds the committed source. Reset-or-worktree before pasting the `got:` hash. Related: `--update-input` against a REV-PINNED input is a SILENT no-op (fails with rc 0, moves nothing) — DiscordSync rode a hard `?rev=` URL pin; check the URL form before trusting an update, and move the pin in flake.nix itself. Also: `--update-input` re-encodes stale `original`/`follows` into a NEW node (`<input>_2`) while leaving the OLD node in the file as an orphan — when diagnosing lock state, walk `nodes[root].inputs` (the lock's top-level `root` key is a node-key string, NOT a mapping), never a bare `nodes[<input>]` read: the orphan can make a healthy lock look stale (cost a misdiagnosis round 2026-09-02).
- **Probe an input's go-modules FOD LOCK-FREE at the exact target rev BEFORE moving the lock (2026-09-03, CV chain)** — `nix build --impure --no-link --expr 'let cv = builtins.getFlake "git+ssh://…?rev=<FULL_REV>"; in cv.packages.x86_64-linux.default.goModules'` fails fast with the fresh `got:` hash for EXACTLY that rev — zero tree churn, no worktree, no lock move. This caught the CV handoff's got-hash going stale from source-only churn between revs (a `got:` hash is rev-scoped, NOT a property of the repo — 3rd occurrence of the source-churn class). Full protocol that unblocked deploys: probe at origin/master HEAD → paste into upstream `nix/packages.nix` (pathspec commit if the daemon lags; push) → `nix flake lock --update-input cv` → hermetic verify via `f.inputs.cv.packages.x86_64-linux.default.goModules` printing a store path → full package build. And when a bumped input's FOD breaks while deploys are needed NOW: roll the lock back to the last buildable rev AND leave a docs breadcrumb (docs/services/cv.md "Lock-state breadcrumb") — a bare lock revert with no note forces coordination-by-archaeology on the owning session.
- **git+file inputs of DIRTY local checkouts lock dirtyRev + a dirty-tree narHash — pin the rev in the URL (2026-09-08, go-taskqueue)** — `nix flake lock --update-input` against a local git+file input whose tree has uncommitted changes (with the auto-commit daemon, there almost always are) records `dirtyRev: <sha>-dirty` and the narHash OF THE DIRTY TREE — every eval breaks the moment the tree churns again, and `--update-input` cannot repair it (the silent no-op trap on `?rev=` URLs). Interim git+file inputs for not-yet-pushed repos MUST carry `?rev=<full-sha>` in the flake.nix URL and be bumped by EDITING the URL, then re-locking. Verify node shape with `jq '.nodes["<input>"].locked'` — a healthy pin has `rev` and NO `dirtyRev`. Build-verify the pin in the CONSUMING flake before proceeding: a rev that passes upstream `nix flake check` can still be stale for the SystemNix toplevel (parallel sessions land fixes between revs; 7890cc9→ca8a2f4 cost one full toplevel build). Flip to `github:…?ref=master` the moment origin carries the commits — CI cannot fetch git+file at all.

- **Pre-deploy §10's new-metric loan is AUTO-DERIVED (2026-09-13) — the manual `KNOWN_NEW_METRICS` list is a legacy escape hatch, not the mechanism** — `scripts/pre-deploy-check.sh` §10 extracts gatus metrics from the TO-BE-DEPLOYED config minus the RUNNING generation's config (located via `GATUS_CONFIG_PATH` in `systemctl cat gatus`, `--config.file` fallback) and auto-loans the diff; `scripts/lib/metrics-gate.sh` additionally WARNs ("retire the stale loan entry") whenever a manually-listed metric is already PRESENT in /metrics, so stale loans surface instead of masking phantom regressions. Do not re-add manual loan entries — retire them. **The to-be-deployed side MUST come from `nix eval` of the RENDERED `services.gatus.settings`, never the `.nix` source (fixed 2026-09-14)** — enable-gated `lib.optionals` blocks (monitor365, disabled since 2026-08-12) live in the source but never reach the deployed YAML, and source-vs-deployed diffing fired a permanent false loan for `cloud_sync_*`/`collector_events_collected` on EVERY deploy; the eval side re-escapes real newlines (`jq gsub`) so anchored `pat(*\n<metric>` forms stay extractable, with a source-grep fallback (warn-labeled) if the eval fails. **Endpoint-down WARN branches are per-service opt-ins, not automatic (2026-09-14)** — when a service's OWN `/metrics` endpoint is down, its gatus-patted metrics are absent and the classifier hard-FAILS (the phantom-metric default) unless the metric list is registered: `BANKSYNC_METRICS`/`BANKSYNC_UP` (bank-sync :8097 — a boot-time pool-mount dropout left bank-sync inactive all boot and hard-blocked unrelated deploys), joining `MONITOR365_METRICS`, `DISCORDSYNC_METRICS`, `CV_METRICS` (auth-gated 401). New service-with-own-/metrics + gatus pats ⇒ add the pair in BOTH `pre-deploy-check.sh` (endpoint probe flag + metric list) and `metrics-gate.sh` (elif branch) — fixture-tested in `scripts/test-pre-deploy-metrics.sh` (Fixture G: down ⇒ WARN, up-but-absent ⇒ still hard FAIL).
- ~~Known pre-existing check red: `checks.x86_64-linux.cv`~~ **FIXED 2026-09-14**: the VM fixture now seeds `CV_OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET` (mock env; CV's OIDC discovery is lazy — `OIDCFlow.ensureProvider` on first /admin use — so no auth.home.lan contact happens in the VM) — the full-build pre-commit flake-check leg is re-armed. Historical note: 2026-09-13→14 the check was red because cv.nix sets `oidc.enabled = true` with `client_secret` empty by design (it rides the `cv-oidc-env` bridge), so cv-server failed config validation; docs-only commits carried `--no-verify` (precedent `73145250`) in that window. Runbook for new-guard work + the full guard inventory: `docs/CONTRIBUTING.md` → "Eval-Time Guards". **Negative-test convention (eval-cache trap)**: a passing negative-test derivation ALWAYS has the same store path (pass/fail is chosen at eval) — an identical output path cannot distinguish "new cases pass" from "stale eval cache", so hand-probe every newly added case once via `extendModules` on evo-x2 before trusting the green
- **Deploy-blocker triple-fix (2026-09-15) — four stacked layers each independently blocked the deploy, two silently**: (1) `sops-key-audit` fired as DESIGNED: `cv_evaluation_citizenships` was declared in sops.nix (defense-portal bundle) without the key in cv.yaml — seeded via the sudo one-liner (empty value is INERT per CV source: `citizenshipsFromEnv` returns nil, clearance demands keep the categorical skip; user then pasted `"DE"`). (2) **`monitor365` REMOVED from the flake `packages` surface**: `nix flake check` derivationStrict's EVERY package, and monitor365's prepared source is permanently unbuildable (private wireguard-collector crate — the 2026-08-12 disable reason). The package surface entry only ever passed via a store-cached drv, so the FIRST input bump after cache eviction made every `nix flake check` fail `path ... is not valid` and hard-blocked every deploy; a parallel session's morning status report had flagged the exact breakage but nobody fixed it. Removing a disabled service's package surface is the fix — it re-arms on the next monitor365 bump otherwise. (3) **pre-deploy-check §1 benign classification was line-level, not block-level**: multi-line nix errors print a bare `error:` headline line that survives the `grep -vE "is not valid|..."` filter even when the block's message line matches — the gate hard-failed on a known-benign class. Fixed by attributing each bare headline to its block (awk state machine; fixture-verified that real assertion/attribute-error blocks still fail the gate). (4) **pre-deploy-check §10 died SILENTLY under `set -euo pipefail`**: the port-enumeration grep matched ZERO URLs (the gatus registry refactor moved per-service `/metrics` URLs into owning service modules; gatus-config.nix now uses a `nodePort` variable), the un-guarded pipeline returned 1, and the whole gate exited mid-section with rc 1 and NO verdict — indistinguishable from a crash. It now scans `modules/nixos/{services,desktop}/` with `|| true`. Doctrine: a WARN-grade enumeration grep must ALWAYS be set -e-guarded; a gate that dies without a verdict is worse than a FAIL. (5) CV upstream packaging fix: the 2c542b0d defense-portal bundle added `github.com/larsartmann/go-graph-rag` to go.mod without flake wiring, failing mkPreparedSource's validatePrivateDeps at the prepared-source build; fixed upstream at `ed8b92f` (`publicDeps` entry — the module IS Go-proxy-served at v0.1.0, so no rev-pinned input needed, unlike go-business-rules — plus vendorHash refresh). CV CI is dead, so input bumps still have NO upstream build signal: probe `goModules` before AND after moving the lock

### Systemd

- **`chmod` on a freshly chown'd (no-longer-root-owned) path needs CAP_FOWNER — CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE does NOT cover chmod** (2026-09-20, hot-user-caches + forgejo-subvol-bootstrap): a root bootstrap that creates a subvol, `chown`s it to the service user, then `chmod`s it EPERMs at the chmod under `harden{}`'s listed caps unless `CAP_FOWNER` rides along (chown succeeds via CAP_CHOWN, making the path non-root-owned; the kernel gates chmod on ownership/CAP_FOWNER specifically — the textfile-collector FOWNER class in a bootstrap shape). Live: `chmod: changing permissions of '/mnt/hot/users/lars/cache/nix': Operation not permitted` → unit failed → exit-4 on every activation.
- **Sandbox setattr with the SETGID bit EPERMs even with the cap in the bounding set — reorder or drop the bit, do not chase caps** (2026-09-22, discordsync attachments bring-up): `chmod 2770` on a dir whose group is outside the unit's groups EPERM'd with CAP_CHOWN+CAP_FOWNER+CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE+CAP_FSETID ALL in the bounding set (NoNewPrivileges=true), and `rsync -a` mode-preservation hit the same class on every foreign-group dir — while the identical `chmod 2770` succeeds as a plain user on the SAME filesystem outside the sandbox (measured live; mechanism unresolved — suspect NNP/capability effective-set handling at exec under the systemd sandbox). Robust patterns that sidestep the class entirely: (1) `chown root:root` → `chmod 0770` (NO setgid bit — it is decorative when the writer runs as the dir's owner) → `chown user:group`; (2) rsync with `--no-perms` + tolerance for perm-only stderr (any non-"failed to set permissions" line still fails the copy) + content-only verify (`grep -E '^[><ch]'` on itemized output). Root-context repro of the mechanism is queued (P0, docs/status/2026-09-22_14-35).
- **A oneshot that must run BEFORE an automount needs `unitConfig.DefaultDependencies = false`** (2026-09-20, hot-user-caches): default service deps add `After=sysinit.target`, but sysinit is After=local-fs.target and the automount the bootstrap must precede sits in the local-fs transaction → local-fs → automount → bootstrap → sysinit → local-fs ordering CYCLE ("Transaction order is cyclic"; at boot this class can wedge local-fs outright). The real dependency (the toplevel mount) must be the explicit `after`/`wants`/`RequiresMountsFor`; the default chain contributes nothing but the cycle. **SUPERSEDED IN PART (2026-09-25): DefaultDependencies=false fixes only the ACTIVATION-time cycle — the BOOT-time cycle survives it.** NEVER wire `wantedBy=`/`before=` against a fstab `.automount` unit from a unit that must precede the mount: at boot the automount sits inside the local-fs/sysinit transaction together with `systemd-tmpfiles-setup.service`, and the closing edge makes systemd's cycle breaker DELETE THE TMPFILES START JOB ("Job systemd-tmpfiles-setup.service/start deleted to break ordering cycle") — the main tmpfiles pass then NEVER RUNS, silently: `/run/binfmt` (nix sandbox `extra-sandbox-paths`) missing → EVERY sandboxed build dies `getting attributes of path "/run/binfmt"` at "setting up the build environment"; `/run/systemnix/sev1` missing → sev1-bridge 226/NAMESPACE ×2900; `/run/lock/systemnix-heavy`, `/run/lvm`, `/run/emeet-pixyd` too. Live 2026-09-24 22:35 — the FIRST boot after the hot-user-caches deploy (no reboot between deploy and boot = the cycle sat latent for 2 days). Detection traps: the journal shows tmpfiles-setup "Finished" from the INITRD instance (looks like it ran); `/run/systemd/units/invocation:` has entries only for the -dev passes; `stat -c %y /run` dates the loss but not the actor. The fix: pull the bootstrap from the on-demand `.mount` (`wantedBy`/`before` on `<escaped-path>.mount`) — the subvol only needs to exist before the FIRST REAL MOUNT, and the bootstrap then never enters the boot transaction at all. Regression-pinned by `tests/test-hot-user-caches.nix` (asserts tmpfiles-setup active + a probe tmpfiles rule applied after boot — only an actual BOOT catches transaction cycles). Belt: deploy.sh runs `systemd-tmpfiles --create --remove --exclude-prefix=/dev` after every switch (NO `--boot` — boot-only `D! /tmp` must stay boot-only or every deploy wipes /tmp); manual heal: `sudo systemd-tmpfiles --create --remove --exclude-prefix=/dev`. Follow-up worth doing: `boot.binfmt.preferStaticEmulators = true` (F-flag static qemu) drops `/run/binfmt` from extra-sandbox-paths entirely, making builds immune to this whole class.
- **NEVER declare a `fileSystems` mountPoint that is an HM `mkOutOfStoreSymlink` — systemd CANONICALIZES mount units through symlinks** (2026-09-20, hot-user-caches go-build): `~/.cache/go-build → /mnt/buildcache/go-build` turned the hot-disk subvol mount into `mnt-buildcache-go\x2dbuild.automount` — an autofs NESTED INSIDE the `/mnt/buildcache` autofs — failing at unit load (exit-4 every activation). A proper integration needs the HM symlink gated off AND a boot ordering that never loads the automount while the symlink exists (HM activates AFTER early-boot automounts); the go-build hot-cache relocation is parked in hot-user-caches.nix until that design exists.
- **`harden {}` ExecStart trap** — NEVER put `ExecStart`/`Type`/`RemainAfterExit` inside `harden {}`. Merge outside via `mkMerge`. Eval-enforced since 2026-09-14: `lib/systemd.nix` THROWS on any lifecycle key (Exec*/Type/RemainAfterExit/Restart) passed into `harden`/`hardenUser` — the illegal shape aborts evaluation with the fix in the message.
- **`runuser` + `harden {}` = PAM failure** — PAM can't complete session init. Run as `User` directly, add shell `runuser()` passthrough function.
- **Root → user-manager bus requires `--machine=<user>@.host --user` (systemd ≥254)** — sd-bus IGNORES a foreign `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` for `--user`: a root system unit calling `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR=/run/user/1000 systemctl --user …` dies "Failed to connect to user scope bus … Operation not permitted" (activitywatch-data-to-pool crash-looped every deploy on this until 2026-08-19). Use `systemctl --machine=lars@.host --user …` (machined proxies the connection from the host; verified live) and only treat stop-failure as fatal when the target process (pgrep) is actually still running.
- **`serviceOneshotDefaults` for oneshots** — `serviceDefaults` defaults to `Restart=always` which is INVALID for `Type=oneshot`. Use `serviceOneshotDefaults`.
- **`%h` not `$HOME` in ExecStart** — Hardened user services may NOT expand `$HOME`. Use `%h` (systemd specifier). Eval-enforced: `systemd-shape-audit.nix` class 4 flags a literal `$HOME` in system-declared user-unit Exec lines (HM-internal units are evaluated in the HM closure, not covered)
- **`switch-to-configuration` exit code 4** — Service in `start-limit-hit` blocks activation. `deploy.sh` runs `systemctl reset-failed` first. **nh wraps this exit 4 as its OWN exit 1** — deploy.sh greps the output for `Exited(4)` to keep the recovery path (post-switch restarts incl. `buildcache-usb-recovery`) reachable; the exit code alone cannot distinguish "activated with failed units" from "aborted".
- **A chronically-FAILING unit whose unit FILE changes exit-4s every activation that touches it (2026-09-09, root cause of the un-anchored-generation saga)** — stc RESTARTS units whose generated file changed; a timer-only oneshot in a permanent FAIL state (an owner-decided "keep alerting" stance) therefore exit-4s the FIRST deploy after ANY churn to its defining module — activation advances `/run/current-system` but skips the profile bump, and a reboot reverts (the Sep-8/Sep-9 `btrfs-verify-pool-backups` + snapshots.nix case: deploy was "green", generation never landed). Doctrine: never leave a unit in permanent FAIL that any deploy can restart — downgrade the chronic branch to WARN (`fatal=no` pattern, snapshots.nix verify) while REAL regressions (mount loss, device errors, root-backup staleness) stay hard-FAIL, and keep the chronic gap's visibility in layers activation never runs (OnFailure alerting, backup-coordination, Gatus). Diagnosis: `readlink /run/current-system` vs `readlink /nix/var/nix/profiles/system` after EVERY deploy that says "activation completed with failed units".

- **A doomed-but-restarting unit exit-4s activations WITHOUT any unit-file change (2026-09-09 16:20 flm class)** — the complement of the bullet above: with the flm EADDRINUSE corpse pinning :52626, every :52625 client connection (PMA/enricher, ~4-5 min cadence) re-requests a backend start ("restart immediately on client request") that dies in <1s; one landing INSIDE a switch transaction makes stc report `warning: the following units failed: fastflowlm.service` → exit 4 → profile bump skipped. Unit files were byte-identical across generations — stc never restarted it, the churn did. Worse: **stc's target cascade re-arms stopped-but-enabled sockets mid-transaction** (observed 2026-09-09 17:19:33 — "Listening on FastFlowLM … (public socket)" seconds into activation with no operator start, no mem-guard restore), so a manual `systemctl stop fastflowlm.socket` does NOT survive a deploy. Mitigation (deploy.sh, 2026-09-09): a pre-switch guard stops socket+service when the unit journal shows recent `bind: Address already in use`, and a post-switch guard re-stops them after the switch re-arms; consumers fail fast (ECONNREFUSED; PMA heuristic-commit fallback) until the reboot clears the corpse. Residual risk until reboot: a client connection in the seconds-scale window between stc's socket re-arm and its report — on exit-4 just re-run the deploy. Both guards fired as designed in the 2026-09-09 18:35 deploy (→ system-764, clean activation, profile bumped, 0 failed units at settle). SECOND re-armer found the same evening: the **memory-emergency-guard's restore branch** re-arms the socket BETWEEN deploys — it sees the stopped socket, assumes its own past sacrifice, and restores it within minutes while memory is healthy (live 17:56:24 + 18:44:55, "sacrifice sockets restored", 2 of maxRestoresPerDay=3, ~10 min restore cooldown); doomed-start churn then resumes and self-extinguishes only at the daily restore cap. Guard journal filter gotcha: its script output logs under syslog id `memory-emergency-guard-check` — `journalctl -u memory-emergency-guard` NEVER shows it (this artifact caused the original wrong rule-out). Post-reboot the whole interaction is gone (the deploy guards only fire on EADDRINUSE journals); a corpse-aware restore skip is a P1 candidate (docs/todo/stability.md).
- **`switch-to-configuration` ignores `restartTriggers` on oneshot+RemainAfterExit** — `deploy.sh` explicitly restarts the provisioner/storage-dir oneshots after `nh os switch`. EVERY new `*-storage-dir`/provisioner oneshot MUST be added to that restart list in `scripts/deploy.sh` or its fixes never re-run until reboot (bank-sync-storage-dir sat failed for 6 deploys before being added, 2026-08-19). Eval-enforced since 2026-09-14: `deploy-restart-audit.nix` cross-references every converger-pattern oneshot AND every oneshot+RemainAfterExit+restartTriggers unit (inert by construction) against the deploy.sh text — INDIRECTLY-enabled bridges (wantedBy=<service>) need a dedicated is-active-gated block because the loop's `is-enabled` gate skips them (cv-oidc-env, found by the audit 2026-09-14, is the reference block).
- **Secret-bridge oneshots need BOTH units restarted — and the provisioner loop CANNOT do it (2026-08-22 dnsblockd SSO outage)** — a provisioner-rotated Pocket ID secret reaches a consumer through a two-unit chain: `dnsblockd-oidc-secret` (oneshot, `RemainAfterExit=true`, rewrites the env file) → `dnsblockd.service` (reads `EnvironmentFile` at process start only). Neither restarts on deploy: the bridge is `wantedBy = dnsblockd.service` → **`is-enabled` returns rc=1 for indirect units, so the deploy.sh provisioner loop silently skips it**, and switch-to-configuration never restarts the daemon for a runtime-file change. deploy.sh has a dedicated block (is-active-gated) restarting bridge + daemon in order. The same shape applies to ANY consumer bridging provisioner secrets via env files (browser-history does the same pattern with `browser-history.service`). Symptom of a stale link: `invalid_client` on token exchange while the authorize step works. Full narrative: `docs/status/archived/2026-08-22_02-46_dnsblockd-oidc-secret-desync-diagnosis.md`
- **Pocket ID client rows can VANISH after a SQLITE_BUSY crash — "Client does not exist" ≠ secret desync (2026-08-22)** — under machine-wide IO pressure (crash-recovery night: btrbk sends + data-to-pool + zram refaults) Pocket ID's SQLite hit a fatal `database is locked` chain (health-check failure → exit 1 → restart), and after the 02:42 restart the `dnsblockd` OIDC client row was GONE from the DB (authorize → `The requested OAuth 2.0 Client does not exist`, `error=invalid_client` on `/authorize`). This is a DIFFERENT failure than secret desync (which 401s only at token exchange). The provisioner self-heals it on next run (client-missing → POST create + secret); manual probe to distinguish: `GET /authorize?client_id=...` → 302 `/interaction?...` = client OK, error redirect = client missing. Secret files surviving a DB recreation while the client is recreated = the desync class; DB losing the client row entirely = this class. Both healed by one provisioner run + the restart chain above. Full narrative: `docs/status/archived/2026-08-22_03-49_dnsblockd-oidc-outage-fixed-self-review.md`
- **`PathChanged` not `PathExists`** — `PathExists` fires immediately if file exists at unit start → re-fire loop → start-limit-hit. Use `PathChanged`. Eval-enforced: `systemd-shape-audit.nix` class 3 rejects PathExists/PathExistsGlob path-unit triggers (ConditionPathExists on services is the correct gate and is not flagged)
- **`WorkingDirectory` runs BEFORE `ExecStartPre`** — If ExecStartPre creates the dir, service fails with `status=200/CHDIR`. Set WorkingDirectory to a parent that exists.
- **`ReadWritePaths` entries must exist BEFORE the unit starts** — systemd builds the mount namespace before ANY ExecStartPre; a missing path aborts with `status=226/NAMESPACE` (an in-unit ExecStartPre mkdir can NEVER create its own ReadWritePaths). Create pool/data dirs via a mount-gated oneshot ordered `before` the service (atticd-storage-dir / google-sync-dirs pattern). Eval-enforced since 2026-09-14: `mount-gating-audit.nix` requires every ReadWritePaths entry under `/mnt/` to be gated by `RequiresMountsFor` (ancestor OR descendant form) or `ConditionPathIsMountPoint` — its first live catch: bank-sync-canary + immich-db-backup both wrote to /mnt/pool ungated (now fixed with RequiresMountsFor); ConditionPathIsDirectory deliberately does NOT count (the shadow-dir masking bug)
- **CPUQuota=200% default in `harden()`** — All services get a 2-core cap. Override per-service (AI services: 300-400%).
- **Docker services use `multi-user.target`** — NOT `graphical.target`.
- **`harden` vs `hardenUser`** — User services (systemd --user) MUST use `hardenUser`.
- **`findmnt`/`/home` probes are BLIND inside `harden{}` units (`ProtectHome = true` default)** — the unit's mount namespace replaces `/home` with an empty inaccessible tmpfs, so `findmnt -n /home/hermes` (or any /home path test) always fails with stderr suppressed — the `btrfs-verify-pool-backups` hermes gate sat as dead code and phantom-greened through the 2026-09-17 00:46 run while `@home-hermes` had ZERO pool receives (its first send had been guard-killed). Probe mount state via `systemctl is-active --quiet <name>.mount` instead (PID-1 truth, immune to ProtectHome); fixed in snapshots.nix same day.
- **`TimeoutStartSec` for ExecStartPre-heavy services** — systemd default is 90s. Services with slow pre-start (DB heal, DNS wait, state migration) exceed this during system switches (I/O contention). Set `TimeoutStartSec = "3min"` explicitly for discordsync (DB heal + DNS wait) and hermes (535 MB state migration). Without it, `nh os switch` reports "Failed to start" even though the service succeeds on retry.
- **`StartLimitBurst`/`StartLimitIntervalSec` in `serviceConfig` = SILENTLY IGNORED (CRITICAL)** — In systemd 261+, these directives are ONLY valid in the `[Unit]` section. Placing them in `serviceConfig` (which maps to `[Service]`) causes systemd to silently ignore them with a warning: `Unknown key 'StartLimitIntervalSec' in section [Service], ignoring`. Services with `Restart=on-failure` then restart INFINITELY with no limit. **Correct placement:** NixOS top-level options `systemd.services.<name>.startLimitBurst` / `.startLimitIntervalSec` (camelCase, outside `serviceConfig`), or `unitConfig.StartLimitBurst`. The `service-defaults.nix` helper documents this (lines 21-27); eval-enforced by `start-limit-audit.nix`.
- **`ExecStartPre = "+…"` is the escape hatch for privileged setup inside hardened units** — the `+` prefix runs that one command with FULL privileges (bypasses `User=`, `CapabilityBoundingSet`, `SystemCallFilter`, namespaces). Use it for ownership migration / chown-style preStart in units that are otherwise sandboxed: `crush-daily`'s in-sandbox `chown -R` SIGSYS'd at every boot (`~@privileged` blocks `fchownat`) AND would have EPERM'd anyway as the service User (2026-08-18; same pattern as browser-history's `+wait-server` gate). Alternative for the syscall part: `SystemCallFilter = lib.mkAfter [ "@chown" ]` (last rule wins) — but `+` is simpler and actually works as root. **`!` is the NARROWER sibling (systemd.service(5), verified 2026-09-10 against the local man page)**: it ONLY bypasses `User=`/`Group=`/`SupplementaryGroups=` — sandboxing (namespaces, `CapabilityBoundingSet`, syscall filter) STAYS applied. Prefer `!` when root identity alone suffices and the sandbox is compatible (dots' mkService uses it for dir chown preStart); `+` remains REQUIRED when the command needs capabilities outside the bounding set (the CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE textfile-collector class)
- **A `+`-privileged readiness gate MASKS the permission denial it gates against (2026-09-09 overview/daemon socket)** — overview's `+wait-daemon` ExecStartPre probed `/run/project-discovery/daemon.sock` as ROOT (root bypasses DAC), passed green, then the overview process itself (User=overview) got EACCES dialing the 0600 `lars:users` socket → exit 69 → start-limit-hit → exit-4'd activations. Two stacked causes: the LOCKED project-discovery-daemon rev `4f6e3c8` predated `PROJECT_DISCOVERY_SOCKET_MODE` (the env var was set to 0666 but the binary created the socket 0600), and the gate's `+` hid it. Rules: (1) when a locked input ignores a config env var, check whether the PINNED REV implements it before blaming the config (git grep at the locked rev); (2) readiness gates that assert the SERVICE USER can reach a resource must probe AS that user — a root-probe gate is a phantom green for exactly the DAC class it exists to catch. Socket now 0666 (configuration.nix `socketMode`), daemon ≥ `1a31c1a` honors it.
- **Timer-driven oneshots must NOT also carry `Restart=on-failure` with `RestartSec` ≈ the timer cadence** — the restart-retry and the next timer fire land as two start requests in one window; the rejected one counts against the rate limit and ONE failed run cascades into a self-re-arming `start-limit-hit` that blocks runs for hours (browser-history-agent 2026-08-18: `RestartSec=5min` + 5-min timer + `burst=2/1800s` → alternating blocked/success fires all day). The timer IS the retry mechanism: `Restart=no` + a generous short-window burst (`5/300s`). Eval-enforced since 2026-09-14: `systemd-shape-audit.nix` class 2 rejects ANY matching-timer unit with `Restart != no`; deliberate bounded-retry exceptions (rapid retries that self-extinguish long before the next timer fire, e.g. forgejo-ensure-repos 3×5s vs daily timer) go in `services.systemd-shape-audit.allowTimerRestart` with a justification comment
- **NEVER grant service access via home-dir ACLs — bind-mount instead; NEVER `chown -R`/recursive-chmod across a unit that has Bind*Paths** — (1) any later `chmod` on an ACL'd home dir rewrites the ACL _mask_ and silently kills every named entry while `getfacl` still lists them (`mask::---`, hermes 2026-08-20); (2) systemd builds the mount namespace BEFORE ExecStartPre, so a recursive ownership walk EROFS-es into the read-only bind and crash-loops the unit (hermes down 09:18→09:35, 2026-08-20) — and `find -xdev` does NOT protect (same-filesystem binds share st_dev); prune the exact bind path. Eval-time WARNING guard: `chown-vs-bind-audit`; regression test: `tests/test-hermes.nix`
- **A perms-heal fast path must probe EXACTLY the predicate set the heal repairs — ownership AND write bits, across the whole tree, not just the root** (2026-09-20 cv-state-perms incident): the probe checked only ownership, went phantom-green while cv-owned-but-readonly dirs (store-mode copies: nix store trees are 0555 and plain `cp -r` propagates that into the state dir) made the upstream `rm -rf assets` EPERM → cv-server start-limit-hit → exit-4 deploy. Rules: (1) fast-path predicate == heal repair set (`! -user u -o ! -group g -o ! -perm -u+w`, all three, whole tree); (2) find's `-perm`/`-user` test the symlink ITSELF (mode 0777) — safe against read-only store targets — but split heal chmods by `-type d`/`-type f` so nothing chmods THROUGH a symlink; (3) `chmod` on a freshly `chown`'d non-root-owned path needs CAP_FOWNER even when CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE is present (see the 2026-09-20 FOWNER rule); (4) journal a heal summary (count + sample + convergence check) so a RECURRING writer stays visible instead of an anonymous green. Sibling audited same day: hermes-perms' fast path now walks child ownership too (mode drift deliberately unprobed — the exec-preserving `X` chmod semantics have no find predicate).
- **Wedged `switch-to-configuration` blocks ALL deploys (exit 11 "Could not acquire lock")** — nixpkgs' Rust switch-to-configuration can complete every activation step, print its final report, and then hang forever (suspected missed dbus `JobRemoved` signal) while holding an flock on `/run/nixos/switch-to-configuration.lock`. Every later deploy then fails `Could not acquire lock` until the zombie PID is killed or the machine reboots (live incident 2026-08-18 13:44+: all work done at 13:44:08, process still wedged an hour later, next deploy exit 11). deploy.sh detects a >30-min-old stc holding the lock, prints the recovery command, and aborts; opt-in auto-kill via `DEPLOY_KILL_WEDGED_STC=1 nix run .#deploy`. Never auto-kill on age alone — a legit activation restarting a unit with a long `TimeoutStartSec` (btrbk-pool-seed: 6h) looks identical from the outside.

- **systemd-oomd kills nix-daemon during builds** — nix-daemon is the top memory consumer under `/system.slice` during builds (4-8G peak). Because it is **socket-activated**, an oomd kill triggers rapid restarts → `start-limit-hit` → daemon permanently dead → ALL nix operations fail. Fix: `ManagedOOMPreference = "omit"` + `OOMScoreAdjust = -1000` (`networking.nix`). Recovery: `sudo systemctl reset-failed nix-daemon.service && sudo systemctl start nix-daemon.service`. Pressure threshold is 60%/30s — tolerates transient spikes from nix builds, AI model loads, and Docker restarts while still catching genuine exhaustion; per-slice `ManagedOOMMemoryPressureLimit` matches (60%). Re-evaluate if kills recur

- **NEVER let any /tmp cleaner touch `systemd-private-*` — they are live service infrastructure, not stale tmp (forgejo 12-day mirror outage, 2026-08-18..30)** — systemd's PrivateTmp backs every service's `/tmp` with a host-side dir `/tmp/systemd-private-<boot-id>-<unit>-<rand>/` bind-mounted into the unit's namespace. The `tmp-cleanup` timer globbed `/tmp/*` and `rm -rf`d entries stale >4h WITHOUT excluding them (they are NOT dotfiles — the "dotfiles are protected" comment didn't cover this class; systemd's own tmpfiles-clean excludes them, custom cleaners must too). Forgejo's mirror-sync credential helper (`os.CreateTemp("/tmp/forgejo-clone-credentials-N")`, new in forgejo 15.0.x, enabled by the Aug 18 nixpkgs bump) then failed ENOENT on EVERY credentialed pull mirror for 12 days (~2,400 error lines/day; each failed sync aborts BEFORE the fetch — so all ~100 mirrors silently missed 12 days of upstream commits, while `updated_unix` kept advancing and the syncs LOOKED fresh). Self-perpetuating: a broken service writes nothing into its private tmp, so the dir always profiles as stale and cleanup keeps it deleted. Diagnosis traps that cost the investigation: the version-bump correlation was WRONG (the errored process ran the SAME `forgejo-lts-15.0.7` binary that runs clean today — diff 15.0.6→15.0.7 touches nothing in the mirror path), the "PrivateTmp checked, not on the main unit" note was WRONG (it is, via the nixpkgs module), and "mirrors still complete" was WRONG (runSync returns false before fetching). Fix: `systemd-private-*` exclusion in the tmp-cleanup loop (`scheduled-tasks.nix`), behavior-tested. Monitoring (Aug 22, verified live 2026-08-31): `system_forgejo_mirror_*` textfile metrics + Gatus "Forgejo Mirror Sync" — but note `updated_unix` advances on FAILED syncs too, so only the `errors_30m` journal counter can see fetch-failure storms; the DB-age metric only sees the dead-queue class. **Blast radius (full journal sweep 2026-08-31): 4 victims, ~16.9k tmp-ENOENT lines** — forgejo 16,318 + discordsync 550 (`failed to create temp file: open /tmp/discordsync-download-*` — attachment downloads failed the whole window but the post-heal reconcile loop drained the backlog with 0 failures; check reconcile logs before assuming lasting loss) + paperless-task-queue 5 (celery `/tmp/pymp-*`, hit at the 01:30 daily scheduler) + immich-machine-learning 2 (wgunicorn `mkstemp`); all four ran `PrivateTmp=true`. **Mechanism VM-PROVEN (`tests/test-tmp-cleanup.nix`):** after host-side deletion of the backing dir the unit's private `/tmp` stays LISTABLE (the bind mount resolves to the unlinked inode) but every file CREATION fails ENOENT — reads work, creates don't; that asymmetry is why services kept running while all temp-file writes died. **Prevention stack (2026-08-31):** (1) `modules/nixos/services/tmp-cleaner-audit.nix` — eval-time assertion rejecting any unit Exec*/script string that globs `/tmp/*` with `rm` and no systemd-private exclusion (inline-text class only; store-path scripts are invisible to it); (2) a self-assertion on the hoisted `tmpCleanupText` in scheduled-tasks.nix — stripping the case-arm exclusion fails `nix flake check`; (3) the VM test runs the REAL unit against aged fixtures + proves the mechanism; (4) `tests/test-tmp-cleaner-audit.nix` — pure-eval negative tests (evil inline caught, exempted passes, real config no false positives, tripwire wired)

- **`/tmp/.Trash-1000` is a RAM hoard the tmp-cleanup can never see (found 2026-09-17)** — `trash` on /tmp files creates a `.Trash-1000` ON THE TMPFS (trash-cli same-fs rule), and the cleaner's dotfile-protection skips it forever; a release-rehearsal session had trashed 35G of `pbx-installer-*` build scratch there, driving /tmp to 90% (44G of 48G tmpfs) and keeping the SigNoz "/tmp TmpFS Usage High" rule firing >24h. tmpfs trash provides ZERO safety (a reboot discards it identically), so emptying it is reboot-equivalent, not data loss: check `info/*.trashinfo` Path= (mount-root-relative = trashed FROM /tmp), then `trash-empty`. Rule: never `trash` anything under /tmp — plain deletion is the honest verb there; and read the SigNoz firing-alerts WARN line as a prompt to `df -h /tmp`, not as background noise
- **Ops scripts and logs NEVER live in /tmp — the tmp-cleaner silently eats >4h-stale entries (deploy-queue recurrence, 2026-09-19)** — an overnight deploy poll script AND its log under /tmp were deleted with zero breadcrumbs; the queue died undiscovered for hours. Operational artifacts belong under `~/.local/state/`, and gate-waiting pollers stay INLINE in the background shell (no script file to lose), log a line every poll (heartbeat), and print a gave-up breadcrumb at deadline. Know the full exit-code surface before writing the retry loop: `nix run .#deploy` rc=12 = pressure gate OR guard-trip recency gate (a Zone-6 trip in the last 60 min means the calm PSI reading is a dip between trips — freeze-#5 doctrine; retry when ≥60 min trip-free), rc=13 = deploy-lock contention (WAIT — a parallel session's deploy builds the same tree and may carry your work; re-check whether the profile advanced before re-firing), rc=14 = activation completed but the profile did NOT anchor (reboot-revertible — re-run the deploy after fixing the failing units it dumps), rc=3 = NEW smoke failures (anchored, non-revertible), anything else = stop and diagnose
- **Every new converger oneshot breaks EVERY host's eval until `scripts/deploy.sh` knows its name (deploy-restart-audit, fleet-wide 2026-09-19 hot-user-caches)** — `deploy-restart-audit.nix` cross-references the unit tree against the deploy.sh text at EVAL time: any `Type=oneshot` matching a converger pattern (`-storage-dir/-backup-dir/-dirs/-provision/-setup/-bootstrap/-oidc-secret/-oidc-env/-migrate`), or oneshot+RemainAfterExit with `restartTriggers`, MUST appear by name in `scripts/deploy.sh` — else `nix eval .#nixosConfigurations.evo-x2.config.system.build.toplevel` (and every deploy) dies on the assertion, on every host importing the module. Wiring procedure, in preference order: (1) provisioner restart loop (directly enabled units — `wantedBy` on ANY unit type, automounts included, generates the `[Install]` symlink `is-enabled` finds; re-run cost of an idempotent bootstrap is one existence check); (2) dedicated is-active-gated restart block for INDIRECT units the `is-enabled` gate skips (dnsblockd-oidc-secret / cv-oidc-env pattern); (3) `services.deploy-restart-audit.allowUnits` ONLY for upstream plumbing converged by its own module, with a justification comment where set. Run the Critical-Rules eval BEFORE declaring a new oneshot deployable — the failure message names the exact missing units

### Caddy & Reverse Proxy

- **`handle_path` STRIPS prefix** — Use `handle` when backend expects full path.
- **`${commonConfig}` required on ALL vhosts** — Security headers, compression, `-Server` suppression. Auto-applied by `protectedVHost`; manual vhosts must include explicitly.
- **`proxyTo` is canonical** — ALL `reverse_proxy` directives use `${proxyTo PORT}`. Never bare `reverse_proxy` — it omits `X-Real-IP`.
- **`auto_https off`** — `:80` catch-all vhost handles HTTP→HTTPS redirect. TLS certs are sops-managed, not ACME.
- **`tlsConfig`** — Enforces TLS 1.2+. `strict_sni_host on` prevents serving certs for unrecognized hostnames.
- **Admin API (port 2019) intentionally unauthenticated** — Firewalled out (only 80/443 open). `admin off` would kill `/metrics`.
- **Native OIDC services use plain `reverse_proxy`** — NOT `protectedVHost`. Forward-auth + native OIDC = double-auth loop.

### SSO / OIDC

- **Native OIDC is NOT free** — Verify upstream support: Homepage (no auth), SigNoz (Enterprise-only, impersonation mode + Layer 2), Twenty (billing-gated), SearXNG (no accounts). All four stay on Layer 2 forward-auth.
- **SigNoz Layer 2 — never use unconditional forward-auth** — SigNoz uses `protectedVHost` (LAN bypass + external forward-auth). The previous unconditional forward-auth (no LAN bypass) caused 500 errors for ALL users when oauth2-proxy hiccuped. `protectedVHost` keeps LAN traffic off the oauth2-proxy path entirely, so oauth2-proxy failures only affect external access.
- **Gatus native OIDC + DynamicUser** — Secret via `LoadCredential` (can't own files). Self-health probe must use `[STATUS] < 400` (302 redirect when OIDC on).
- **Forgejo OIDC** — Native OIDC via `forgejo-oidc-setup` oneshot. Auth source name ("PocketID") IS the URL slug — no spaces. `ENABLE_AUTO_REGISTRATION = true`.
- **Pocket ID client-secret desync** — Declarative recovery: `pocket-id-config.provision.regenerateSecretsFor = [ "clientId" ]`. `RemainAfterExit=true` makes `start` a no-op — use `RESTART`.
- **Pocket ID provisioner secret generation uses the MULTI-SECRET API (fixed 2026-09-02): `POST /api/oidc/clients/{id}/secrets` (PLURAL, optional body, 201 returns `.secret` exactly once)** — the old singular `/secret` route 404s on current pocket-id. Symptom of the stale route: client created/updated fine, then `ERROR: Failed to generate secret for 'X'` every provision run — every client FIRST provisioned after the pocket-id bump silently got no secret (Paperless was the first new client since the bump; forgejo/gatus/dnsblockd secret files predated it).
- **A value-less metric line ("`metric` + empty var") makes node_exporter reject the WHOLE textfile (live 2026-09-02)** — the system-health forgejo journal-scan failure path emptied only `FORGEJO_MIRROR_ERRORS_30M`/`ERRORING` while the emission block was gated on the non-empty `LAST_SYNC_AGE`, emitting `system_forgejo_mirror_errors_30m` with no value: invalid exposition syntax, so ALL 38 system_* metrics went dark at once (gatus red fleet-wide, every deploy blocked at pre-deploy §10). Rule: EVERY conditionally-computed metric must be emitted only when its OWN variable is non-empty (fail-closed absence), and sibling metrics set/emptied TOGETHER must be gated TOGETHER. **State-file round-trips defeat per-site helpers (2026-09-20)**: the nrestarts state WRITER bypassed `systemctl_value`'s `[not set]` guard, persisted literal `[not set]` rows, and the emitter re-served them raw — node_exporter rejected system_health.prom whole (node_textfile_scrape_error=1, seven system_* metrics dark across deploys) while every eval stayed green. Sanitize at BOTH sides of any persisted round-trip (integer `case "$v" in *[!0-9]*)` guards at write AND emit), never assume a guard at one call site covers another. The §10 gate now keys on positive infra signals instead of hard-failing: `node_textfile_scrape_error=1` (whole textfile rejected → absent metrics are warnings + the offending value-less lines are printed) and `system_forgejo_mirror_scrape_errors=1` (journal scan failed → its pair absent BY DESIGN).
- **oauth2-proxy `--whitelist-domain=.${domain}` REQUIRED** — Without it, post-login redirect fails with 500.
- **oauth2-proxy `partOf` pocket-id-provision** — `LoadCredential` secrets need service restart when regenerated.

### BTRFS & Filesystems

- **All mounts set `nodiscard`** — QLC NAND (`discard=async`) causes 253ms discard latency → BTRFS commit stalls → WDT reset. Daily `fstrim.timer` (idle priority) handles TRIM.
- **SLC cache exhaustion is the root crash cause** — QLC NAND uses an SLC write cache that BTRFS CoW churn exhausts within 22-47h when fstrim is weekly. With cache gone, every write hits QLC directly (~253ms), building an exponential I/O queue that freezes the kernel → WDT reset. Daily fstrim + `commit=300` mitigates this. See `docs/gotchas-archive.md` for full incident narrative.
- **`commit=300` on all BTRFS mounts** — Reduces metadata write frequency ~10x vs default 30s, preserving SLC cache blocks. Data loss window: 5 min (acceptable with daily snapshots + CoW journaling).
- **ext4 uses bare `discard`** — `discard=async` on ext4 → mount fails → emergency shell.
- **Non-`nofail` mounts = boot hazard** — Any non-root mount without `nofail` that fails brings down `local-fs.target`.
- **Compression is filesystem-wide** — `compress=zstd` on any mount applies to ALL subvolumes. Only `subvol`/`subvolid` and VFS options are per-mount.
- **`/nix` on its own `@nix` subvolume (migrated 2026-08-17)** — Was a plain dir inside `@`, so btrbk snapshots AND pool sends carried the ~47 GiB nix store (CoW-pinned up to 30d/12w). Store is rebuildable — needs no snapshots. Sibling subvolumes are never part of a snapshot of `@`. Migration: `scripts/migrate-nix-subvol.sh` (reflink bulk copy via `cp -a --reflink=always` — rsync has NO `--reflink` flag, that is cp syntax; recurred 2026-09-16 in migrate-hermes-subvol.sh v1); old `@/nix` deleted post-verification, space frees as snapshots expire. `/home` still lives inside `@` (intentionally snapshotted).
- **`rm` doesn't free space when snapshots reference data** — Reclamation happens as btrbk snapshots expire (14d retention).
- **`findmnt -o MAJ:MIN` on btrfs returns the ANONYMOUS superblock devt (`0:48`), never a member's devt** — btrfs superblocks get anon device numbers; comparing them against `lsblk` member devts can NEVER match (would misclassify every healthy mount as stale; caught by `tests/test-pool-recovery.nix`). For mount identity on btrfs use the fs **UUID** (`findmnt -no UUID` vs `lsblk -nrno UUID <member>`) or a real-I/O probe.
- **`compsite` is memory-intensive** — Needs `MemoryMax=2G` minimum on a 47 GiB nix store. Runs every 6h (not 5min).
- **`/tmp` tmpfs capped at 48 GiB** — Via `systemd.mounts` (static `tmp.mount`). NEVER use `fileSystems."/tmp"` — generates runtime fstab entry → unmount failure.
- **ClickHouse lives on a dedicated XFS partition (2026-08-22)** — `/var/lib/clickhouse` is XFS on the QLC disk's ~100 GiB tail partition (label `clickhouse`, by-label mount in hardware-configuration.nix; kernel enumeration flips — live 2026-09-14: nvme1n1p9), moved off root `@` to stop QLC CoW write amplification + forever-pinned btrbk pool receives. `clickhouse.service` (signoz.nix) self-wires `RequiresMountsFor` + `ConditionPathIsMountPoint` ONLY when the host declares the mount (`builtins.hasAttr "/var/lib/clickhouse" config.fileSystems`) — the mount absent ⇒ ClickHouse refuses to start (never writes telemetry to the root fs underneath). Migration runbook: `scripts/migrate-clickhouse-xfs.sh` (`prepare` BEFORE first deploy, `finalize` after green smoke — deletes shadowed originals via a `--bind /` view; root space frees as root snapshots expire — pin window 2w sharp, calendar-anchored, see the BTRFS Snapshot-pinning doctrine). **User-run sudo scripts resolve EVERY binary up front** (PATH → system env → `nix build` of the flake's pinned nixpkgs): `sudo`'s secure PATH hides user-profile tools and gptfdisk/parted are NOT in system env — the first prepare run stopped the stack and THEN died at `sgdisk: command not found`; the tool preflight must run BEFORE anything is stopped. XFS cannot shrink; telemetry backup coverage LANDED 2026-09-23: `clickhouse-db-backup.timer` (03:00, native server-side `BACKUP ALL EXCEPT DATABASES system, INFORMATION_SCHEMA, information_schema` → `/mnt/pool/backups/clickhouse`, 3-run retention, `.last_success` → backup-coordination + aggregate Gatus check; `<backups><allowed_path>` in extraServerConfig is the single legal File() destination — BACKUP executes inside the server process; first run deploy-gates) — closes docs/todo/storage.md. Monitoring: `clickhouse-xfs-metrics` textfile collector (5 min, real-I/O gated, fail-closed) + Gatus "ClickHouse Data Mount"/"ClickHouse Data Usage". XFS labels cap at 12 chars — hence the short label. `btrfs-health` metrics do not cover it by design. **NEVER count/verify a live DB's file tree** — the prepare script counted source entries at preflight while ClickHouse still ran and its background merges deleted one part file before `stop_stack`, failing the entry-count gate 299214 vs 299215 despite a 0-delta checksum pass; all source metrics are taken AFTER quiescing (the copy rsync also carries `--delete` so reruns converge to an exact mirror — merge churn between attempts otherwise leaves extraneous parts the checksum gate cannot flag). `clickhouse-xfs-ownership-heal` (`+`-privileged ExecStartPre on clickhouse.service): the interrupted-migration-era tree carried mis-owned inodes that crash-loop CH with EACCES (`status=233/RUNTIME_DIRECTORY`); the heal chowns to `clickhouse:clickhouse` only when `find ! -user clickhouse` hits
- **`mkFilesystem` helper** — `lib/filesystems.nix` validates mount options at eval time. Use it instead of raw `fileSystems`.

### ZRAM & Memory Reclaim

- **`vm.swappiness=150` for zram-only swap** — With zram as the ONLY swap, low swappiness tells the kernel to prefer page-cache reclaim (disk I/O on BTRFS/QLC NAND) over zram swap (in RAM) — this fed the recurring BTRFS I/O storms (`kworker/inode_switch_wbs` at 100% CPU, I/O PSI `full avg10=79%`). Correct range for zram-only: 100-200 (kernel docs: "for zram, use 100+")
- **ZRAM fill is monitored** — `system-health` emits `system_zram_swap_fill_percent` (orig_data_size/disksize from `/sys/block/zram0/mm_stat`) + `system_zram_fill_over_threshold` (≥90%); the Gatus "ZRAM Fill" check alerts Discord. Metrics are emitted ONLY when `/sys` is readable — absence fails the check fail-closed (no phantom greens)
- **ZRAM sizing for zram-only swap** — live zram0 = **~47 GiB (`memoryPercent = 50`) on ~94 GiB visible RAM** (MemTotal after the 18 GiB GPU VRAM carveout — the config's old ~33/~110 figures predated the carveout). A too-small zram (was 17%/16 GiB, then 30%/28.2 GiB) fills under normal load, and with no disk-swap fallback the kernel falls back to aggressive page-cache eviction = BTRFS disk I/O storms. **2026-09-02 sizing lesson:** the 28.2 GiB device sat at **97% fill (27.4 GiB swapped, 10.7 GiB physical at 2.6x) as STEADY STATE — 53% MemAvailable, 0.26% PSI, a perfectly healthy machine whose swap gauge read "critical"** (that reading fed the same-day sev1 alert-spam incident). Fill-% is only a meaningful cliff signal with headroom: at 50% the same load reads ~58% and the unevictable-shmem cliff moves ~19 GiB further out. Physical cost is bounded by compression (~18 GiB at full 47 GiB) and idle cost is ZERO (pages allocated only when stored) — sizing zram generously is free. Takes effect on REBOOT (zram is sized at boot). The 1 GiB VRAM carveout (the BIOS FLOOR — 512 MiB is not settable on this board; live since 2026-09-05 boot 13:16, GTT-first) grew visible RAM to ~124.3 GiB — the percent-based device auto-scaled to 62.2 GiB at 50% (verified live)
- **`vm.watermark_scale_factor=100`** — At 10 the kernel waits until memory is very low, then does aggressive synchronous "panic reclaim" I/O bursts. 100 (the default) starts reclaim earlier and gradually, avoiding sudden BTRFS writeback storms
- **`vm.dirty_ratio=5`, `vm.dirty_background_ratio=1` for QLC NAND** — 10% of ~94 GiB = 9.4 GiB of dirty pages before forced writeback = huge writeback bursts on QLC NAND. 5%/1% spreads writes more evenly
- **`vm.vfs_cache_pressure=150`** — Prefers reclaiming dentry/inode cache (cheap, no disk I/O) over page cache (expensive, requires disk reads/writes)
- **`vm.page-cluster=0` + `vm.watermark_boost_factor=0` (2026-09-05)** — every mainstream zram deployment (Fedora, Pop!_OS, ChromeOS) ships both; we sat at kernel defaults (3/15000) despite zram-only swap since forever. page-cluster=0 kills readahead-window amplification on swap-in (a clustered swap-in reads 8 pages to fault 1); watermark_boost=0 stops kswapd boosting off swap faults (pointless feedback on zram)
- **zram compression ratio is ~3.2x live** — Measured via `/sys/block/zram0/mm_stat`. zstd level=1 is optimal (level 3 gains 1.7% ratio for 11.5% less speed)

### Docker & Containers

- **`oci-containers` backend defaults to Podman** — Set `backend = "docker"` when Docker is already enabled.
- **`Requires=docker.service` on compose units = permanent outage after any docker blip (fixed 2026-08-31)** — a Requires= dependency failure at boot (dockerd's containerd startup timed out ONCE under I/O storm, then self-healed via its own Restart) fails the compose unit's start JOB with result=dependency, and job failures NEVER re-trigger `Restart=always` (restarts only apply to executed processes) — manifest + twenty stayed down until a deploy. `mkDockerService` now uses `wants` so ExecStart failing against a not-yet-ready daemon feeds the normal Restart loop. Companion rule: EVERY compose service incl. DB sidecars MUST set `restart = "always"` — manifest's postgres lacked it, died exit-255 at the docker restart, and the app crash-looped against the dead DB for 15+ min (gatus red) until `docker start mnfst-postgres-1`. Runtime remediation for that class: restart the compose unit (needs root) or `docker start <db-container>` (works via docker group).
- **Docker 29.x `userland-proxy-path`** — `daemon.settings.userland-proxy = false`.
- **Attach-flavor compose units (dozzle) do NOT survive a docker daemon restart (found 2026-08-31 live test)** — `docker-dozzle.service` runs `docker compose up` ATTACHED (main process = compose); a `systemctl restart docker` kills the attached process → ExecStop/ExecStopPost run compose down (container REMOVED) → unit lands inactive(dead) with Result=success until next boot/deploy. Detached-flavor units (manifest, twenty) ride through fine. Verified in the 2026-08-31 convergence test: mnfst-postgres-1 self-healed via restart=always in ~17s, manifest/twenty stayed active, twenty-worker-1 came back via compose dependency gating (starts only after twenty-server-1 reports healthy — do NOT read a temporarily "Created" worker as a failure), but dozzle needed `sudo systemctl start docker-dozzle.service`. Consider converting dozzle to the detached flavor if daemon-restart resilience matters for it.
- **`docker system prune --filter until=N` is a false-green on docker 29.x — use granular prunes (fixed 2026-08-31)** — the weekly `docker-prune` unit logged `Total reclaimed space: 0B` while 2 dangling images (2-3 wk old) and a tagged zoo sat eligible; plain `system prune` NEVER removes tagged-but-unused images (old version tags like `twenty:v2.7.3` persist forever without `-a`) and skips volumes without `--volumes`. The unit now runs `container prune` + `network prune` + `image prune -af --filter until=168h` + `builder prune -f --filter until=168h` (`platforms/nixos/system/scheduled-tasks.nix`): `-a` + until collects unused tagged images OLDER than the 7-day bound while fresh builds survive (verified live: 34→9 images, 8.6→2.1 GB; website images built 3 h earlier kept); builder prune only takes cache records untouched ≥7 d — the 8 GB warm cache from this week's builds stays by design and ages out in later runs. Volumes deliberately never auto-pruned (3.6 GB "reclaimable" parked there is real data).
- **containerd bbolt corruption** — Recovery: stop docker → `mv meta.db meta.db.bak` → remove `containers/`/`containerd/`/`network/` dirs → restart.
- **systemd-oomd kills Docker containers under system-slice pressure (Twenty worker)** — Docker containers run as `docker-<id>.scope` under `/system.slice`. systemd-oomd picks the largest memory consumer to kill when system-slice pressure exceeds 50% for 20s. The Twenty worker (~856MB, largest container) was SIGKILL'd (exitCode=137) every ~15s: oomd kills → `restart: always` restarts → Node.js init spike → more pressure → oomd kills again → infinite loop (136 restarts in 40 min). Docker reports `OOMKilled: false` because systemd-oomd kills via cgroup signal, not Docker's own OOM handler. Fix: `mem_limit: "2g"` + `memswap_limit: "2g"` + `NODE_OPTIONS="--max-old-space-size=1536"` in the compose file. `ManagedOOMPreference=omit` canNOT be set on Docker scopes from NixOS (transient units). The nix-daemon oomd exemption reduces cascade pressure events, which indirectly reduces worker kills.

### DNS (dnsblockd)

- **dnsblockd is the SOLE DNS resolver on :53** — Replaces unbound entirely. Embedded sdns handles DNSSEC, DoT/DoH, local zones, blocklists.
- **dnsblockd is oomd-exempt + kernel-OOM-last (2026-09-06)** — `ManagedOOMPreference = "omit"` + `OOMScoreAdjust = -1000` on the unit (nix-daemon critical-infrastructure doctrine; the long-standing P0 closed). It was oomd-killed 730×/day at the old 50%/20s threshold; the 60%/30s threshold + MemoryMax=4G + GOMEMLIMIT=3GiB tamed it, but oomd still ranked it by memory.current under /system.slice PSI >60%/30s. A kill costs ~2min of DNS while the 3.9M-entry blocklist reloads, a kill loop hits StartLimitBurst → dead sole resolver, and DNS death cascades (local zones, deploy-blocking `cache.home.lan` resolution 2026-09-02 class, sev1/Discord alerting blind).
- **Stats API (:9090) wedge under IO starvation — mechanism CONFIRMED 2026-09-06, fix PUSHED and DEPLOYED (corrected 2026-09-13: the earlier "implemented upstream (unpushed)" claim was stale — the cached-response healthHandler is on dnsblockd origin AND is an ancestor of SystemNix lock rev `94c9cb93`, the deployed `dnsblockd-94c9cb9` binary; verified via `internal/server/health.go:97` calling `CachedResponse()` + merge-base ancestry)** — `/health` called `healthProbe.Evaluate(r.Context())` INLINE: go-health's Evaluate runs the tracking-DB `PingContext` synchronously on the request path, and under extreme IO PSI the 4-conn sqlite pool is starved by stalled batch-writer flushes → the handler wedges mid-request (CLOSE_WAIT) for as long as the pool stays starved. Wedged twice (2026-08-27, 2026-09-06); DNS itself kept resolving both times. **2026-09-06 forensics:** SIGTERM 09:15:09 progressed ("stopped data retention cleanup loop" 09:15:19) then hung 80s in batch-writer flush/DB close → `stop-sigterm` timeout → SIGKILL 09:16:39 (unit stats: 1.1G mem peak, 1.5G read/669M written over 2h). NO goroutine dump captured (runbook not used — evidence lost; ALWAYS run `scripts/dnsblockd-goroutine-dump.sh` before restarting a wedged instance). /metrics analysis: its collectors are all in-memory (promhttp over Go/Process/OTel — no DB); the tracking middleware dispatches post-response; the stats server's 30s WriteTimeout kills wedged CONNECTIONS but the handler GOROUTINES leak until the DB unsticks — CLOSE_WAIT pileup is clients closing before the server's 30s. **Upstream fix (dnsblockd, 2026-09-06, in the deployed lock rev):** healthHandler serves go-health's background cache (`CachedResponse()`, refreshed every 1s by the probe loop, each batch bounded by the 5s go-health timeout) instead of inline Evaluate; a staleness guard (>15s = background loop itself wedged) flips the response to 503 + failed database check so monitors see RED instead of a hanging endpoint; no-cache fallback (pre-Start) is one timeout-bounded live eval. Regression tests: burst-requests-serve-from-cache (eval counter must not advance per-request), stale-cache-serves-503-fast, pure degradeStaleHealth table. go-health's own ReadinessHandler already had this cache-first design — only dnsblockd's rich /health bypassed it. No dedicated release tag carries it yet (tag-pinning consumers would miss both this and the OTLP scheme fix; cutting one is tracked in docs/todo/upstream.md). Post-deploy live behavior confirmation (cache-served responses, fast 503 on staleness) still pending — docs/todo/services.md dnsblockd live-probe items. Detection layers that worked 2026-09-06: SigNoz "DNS Blocker Stats API Wedged" rule + `system_dnsblockd_metrics_fresh` gauge + Gatus check. The 2026-08-27 residual (deploy.sh dnsblockd-restart block not firing despite active bridge — unexplained) still stands; do not rely on it. Root driver: the ONE-USB-LINK IO storm class (2026-09-06: uncoordinated parallel agent sessions building/testing through `/mnt/buildcache` + pool sends on one link — IO PSI some avg10 hit 98.9%, load 722, 704 D-threads); heavy jobs must ride the `heavy-job` wrapper (workload-admission.nix), never raw `go test`/`cargo nextest` storms.
- **`dnsIPv6Enabled = false`** — evo-x2 has no global IPv6. Omits IPv6 root servers, avoids 5-15s timeouts.
- **Wildcard `*.home.lan` does NOT resolve** — Only explicitly listed subdomains in `dnsLocal.localSubdomains` resolve. New service subdomains MUST be added.
- **`restartTriggers` on dnsblockd** — Without it, config changes may not restart the process → stale config → DNS outage.
- **`ProtectSystem=strict` + SQLite** — Needs `WorkingDirectory` alongside `StateDirectory` or CANTOPEN errors.
- **Manual `/etc/resolv.conf` edits break local DNS** — Nix config writes static resolv.conf with `127.0.0.1` first and `9.9.9.9` as fallback. Every deploy restores it. Do NOT reorder: `127.0.0.1` MUST be first so `*.home.lan` resolves via dnsblockd; `9.9.9.9` provides resilience when dnsblockd is down or slow
  - **2026-09-02 incident (the class, proven live):** during a NIC-vanish DNS outage the user `sudo nano`'d resolv.conf to `1.1.1.1` as a workaround; after the NIC self-recovered (03:24, no reboot) the leftover edit killed `*.home.lan` for ~10h while dnsblockd was HEALTHY on :53 — gatus stayed green because its "DNS Resolver" check probes `127.0.0.1` directly, never the system-resolver path (blind spot). Cascade: attic substituter `cache.home.lan` unresolvable → `nix flake check --no-build` prints `error: unable to download ... .narinfo` → pre-deploy gate step 1 greps ANY `error:` → **deploy blocked by the very deploy that would fix DNS (chicken-and-egg)**. Fixes shipped: (1) gate filter excludes narinfo-unreachability errors (infra noise, never syntax); (2) `system_local_dns_resolves` textfile metric (getent `auth.home.lan` through the SYSTEM resolver) + anchored gatus check "Local DNS System Resolver" closes the blind spot. Recovery runbook for the class: verify dnsblockd health first (`dig cache.home.lan @127.0.0.1 +short` must answer), then `nix run .#deploy` (activation rewrites resolv.conf) — never hand-edit it back under pressure without confirming dnsblockd actually resolves
- **Dashboard auth token via sops env** — `auth_token` is passed via `DNSBLOCKD_AUTH_TOKEN` EnvironmentFile (koanf env override), NOT the nix-store config YAML. The sops secret `dnsblockd_auth_token` is owned by `primaryUser:users` so the DMS DnsStatsWidget can read it for its `Authorization: Bearer` header. The sops template `dnsblockd-auth-env` (root-owned) feeds the systemd EnvironmentFile. Two renderings of the same key, different owners, different consumers. Protected routes: `/stats`, `/api/allow`, `/api/report`, `/api/cache/flush`. Unprotected: `/health`, `/metrics`, `/dashboard`, `/api/dashboard-data` (cookie-gated).
- **HaGeZi blocklists come from the GitLab mirror** (`gitlab.com/hagezi/mirror`), NOT GitHub — GitHub's automated fraud detection repeatedly locks `hagezi/dns-blocklists` (404s all 22 fetch derivations, blocking every deploy). Lists track the mirror's mutable `main` branch with SRI-hash pinning: content drift fails the build loudly; fresh blocklists require periodic hash refreshes

### Monitor365

**DISABLED since 2026-08-12** (`enable = false` in configuration.nix, agent + server): the vendored `wireguard-collector` crate lives in a PRIVATE LarsArtmann repo — the Nix build can never fetch it ("could not read Username", 404 on sum.golang-class fetch). Re-enable requires an owner decision: publish the crate to crates.io, make the repo public, or vendor it into the monitor365 workspace. Post-deploy checks auto-SKIP when the units are absent; Gatus checks are enable-gated.

- **Package alias trap** — `pkgs.monitor365` = agent (CLI), NOT server. Server is `monitor365-server` (symlinkJoin with WASM UI).
- **No JIT SSO provisioning** — Users MUST be pre-provisioned via bootstrap or `create-admin` CLI. SSO login fails if email doesn't match existing user.
- **DuckDB not SQLite** — Uses `.duckdb` extension. `normalize_db_path` converts `.db` → `.duckdb` as safety net.
- **Daily event limit override** — `monitor365-schema-migrate` runs `UPDATE tenants SET max_events_per_day = 1000000000` on every boot. Do NOT remove — server re-syncs upstream default on bootstrap.
- **DuckDB WAL corruption self-heal** — `ExecStartPre` removes `.wal` on every startup (always means unclean shutdown). Restores from backup if main DB missing.
- **DuckDB pool deadlock watchdog** — `monitor365-server-watchdog` (every 5min) checks `/health` + counts "pool acquire failed" journal errors. `Restart=always` only covers process exit — degraded-but-alive states need active health probes. **Must use `journalctl --grep` + `-n` cap** — the naive `journalctl | grep -c` pattern serialized 270+ MB and burned 98% CPU every 5 minutes because it piped every journal entry through grep. `--grep` filters inside journalctl; `-n 21` enables early termination
- **Graphical collectors need** — `input`/`video` groups, `ProtectProc = "default"` (not `invisible`), `%t` (not `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`) in ExecStart.
- **utoipa-swagger-ui overlay** — `overlays/linux.nix` deletes 0444 zip between cargo check/build. Remove when upstream fixes `fs::copy`.
- **libspa-sys vendored Cargo.tomls** — ALWAYS strip `[lints]` sections when regenerating vendor patches. `workspace = true` fails in sandbox.

### DiscordSync

- **Always-on API server** — Upstream ALWAYS starts HTTP API on `:8080` (conflicts with SigNoz). Override `apiAddr` to `127.0.0.1:8085`.
- **Attachments are pool-native (2026-09-22, MIGRATION COMPLETE — 20.5 GB verified identical, NVMe source removed, API green)** — the 2026-09-21 Phase-2 re-scope of the Own-tools NVMe→pool leg moved ONLY the BLOBs: `services.discordsync.attachmentsDir = /mnt/pool/services/discordsync/attachments` (the attachment archive; the DB stays on NVMe — the Phase-2 hot-db wave owns it). The option wires: `ATTACHMENT_STORAGE_PATH` → the pool path, the mount-gated `discordsync-attachments-dir` leaf creator (provisioner-restarted), the service's `RequiresMountsFor` + `ReadWritePaths` pool gating (a detached DAS fails the service as a clean dependency — bank-sync pool-native precedent; `pool-recovery` converges it), and the one-time `discordsync-attachments-migrate` oneshot (STATIC unit — deploy.sh's dedicated no-block block starts it, the provisioner loop's is-enabled gate skips static units): stops discordsync, `rsync -aHAX --no-perms`, content-only checksum-verify, `rm -rf` source (rm, not trash — the blob bytes would write back onto the NVMe), restarts discordsync; ConditionPathIsDirectory makes every later run a clean skip. `null` (default) restores the legacy in-state `<dataDir>/attachments` layout incl. its tmpfiles rule — pool-less hosts/VMs are unaffected. **Bring-up lessons (2026-09-22, three failed migration runs before green): (1) `chmod 2770` (setgid) on a group outside the sandbox's groups EPERMs EVEN with CAP_FSETID in the bounding set — the dir-creator now does `chown root:root` → `chmod 0770` (no setgid — decorative anyway) → `chown user:group`, idempotent and cap-independent; (2) `rsync -a` mode-preservation hits the same class on every foreign-group dir — the migrate uses `--no-perms` on BOTH passes and tolerates perm-only rsync stderr (any non-"failed to set permissions" line still fails the copy; the verify pass filters itemized output to `^[><ch]` content-only lines); (3) the FSETID/sandbox setattr MECHANISM is unresolved (works as plain user on the same fs, fails as root+caps in the unit) — see the Systemd gotchas.**
- **API startup race (5-11 min)** — API binds after thumb-hash backfill. NEVER add `ExecStartPost` readiness gate — it crash-loops the service. Use Gatus (60s interval).
- **SQLite corruption self-heal** — `ExecStartPre` runs `PRAGMA integrity_check`; corrupt DB moved to `.corrupt-<timestamp>`. Re-syncs from Turso cloud.
- **Module consumption pattern** — `imports = [ inputs.X.nixosModules.default ]` + layer SystemNix specifics via `lib.mkMerge`. See Monitor365 as gold standard.
- **samber/do `InvokeNamed[Interface]` on concrete registrations is ALWAYS a startup-fatal (2026-08-16/18 outage)** — `do.InvokeNamed[T]` matches T against the registration's exact type parameter (stored wrapper is `serviceWrapper[T]`); an interface type NEVER equals a concrete `ProvideNamedValue` registration, even when the value implements it.
