DiscordSync rev `e71e8086` resolved its four named health checkers as `do.HealthcheckerWithContext` → every startup fataled `DI: service found, but type mismatch` → exit 69, delayed ~30 min by the thumb-hash livelock → Gatus red for 2 days, 404k `database is locked` errors, message loss via capture-DLQ write failures. Fixed upstream `085fa539` (invoke by concrete type + `TestInvokeHealthCheckServices_ResolvesNamedCheckers` guard). Diagnosis trick: a uniform ~30-min exit-69 cadence with `Consumed 29min 57s CPU` in systemd's unit log = startup-path livelock before the fatal; `API server stopped: http: Server closed` with the process STILL ALIVE = a shutdown that hung mid-exit (zombie — restart manually). Pocket ID SQLITE_BUSY spikes are collateral of discordsync IO storms on the same BTRFS filesystem.
- **Turso cloud sync DECISION-PENDING — local-first stance ENCODED (2026-09-11, task queue; live journal verified)** — the Turso free plan is hard-blocked since 2026-08-16: the journal logs `turso push failed … SQL read operations are forbidden (reads are blocked, do you need to upgrade your plan?) code: BLOCKED` on every push attempt (~5-min cadence; upstream circuit breaker trips after 5 consecutive failures → 1h backoff, live-verified 2026-09-11 08:34 — 167 failures/24h, so the noise is bounded, not literally every 5 min). The token AUTHENTICATES (an auth failure looks different), so **re-auth cannot fix this — the only paths are a user Turso plan upgrade or permanent local-only**. The module already encodes the superior local-first posture (`discordsync.nix`): `backend = "turso-sync"` + upstream `OpenTursoSync` quota fallback = fully local SQLite operation with AUTOMATIC cloud-resume the moment the plan is upgraded, whereas a bare `sqlite` backend would BOTH lose auto-resume AND cost a 40+ min startup backfill (FTS5 trigger contention, live-measured vs ~21 min). The Gatus "DiscordSync Turso Sync Active" check (`discordsync_turso_local_only_mode`) deliberately stays RED while local-only — that IS the standing stale-mirror signal; do not silence it. Sops keys (`discordsync_turso_url/auth_token`) stay declared for the auto-resume. Re-open as a task only when the user decides: upgrade (sync resumes on next service start, zero config change) or permanent local-only (then remove the TURSO_* env from the sops template + drop the Gatus check). The remaining WARNs (5 per attempt-run, then 1h silence via backoff) are upstream logging noise, harmless.
- **Capture-DLQ churn from cgroup OOM under a zram-full box (2026-09-02 incident)** — 13 `Failed with result 'oom-kill'` cycles in one afternoon (zero in the prior 3 days): with zram 100% full + flm's 24.6 GB unevictable model resident + a concurrent build storm stalling btrfs writeback, discordsync's cgroup filled to `MemoryMax=2G` with un-cleanable dirty page cache → kernel cgroup OOM kill every 7-25 min → in-flight appends dead-lettered → `/api/health/backup` critical (1927 capture-DLQ events). The DLQ did exactly its job (zero loss). **Runbook:** replay = `POST /api/capture-dlq/replay` on loopback :8085 (no auth key configured; INSERT OR IGNORE idempotent, batch of 1000 per call — loop until GET depth 0; entries survive a mid-replay kill). Projection DLQ replay needs the projection NAME (`POST /api/dlq/replay?projection=<name>`); the 2 legacy poison events (messages `no rows` on a parent-less message.updated, reactions FK-fail on a missing parent — 2026-08-16 loss-era orphans) fail deterministically on every replay and evaluate "ok" — do NOT purge them (they are the only record of the gap). **Trap: the API itself 503s while its DB is busy** (post-restart backfill window; body says "Service Busy"), and Gatus 60s checks flap green between kills — judge health by the journal/restart churn, not the endpoint check. Upstream fix shipped in DiscordSync (2026-09-02): `/api/dlq` list + dashboard DLQ view scanned TEXT `failed_at` into `time.Time` → every list request 503'd (`unsupported Scan`), dead letters unlistable since the endpoint shipped; now returns the raw stored string, regression-tested (`TestHandleDLQ_ListReturnsStoredFailedAtText`). The box-level fix is the reboot pending the 2026-09-02 zram 50% sizing, not anything in discordsync.
- **Immich cross-archive comparison (LIVE config 2026-09-19, key paste pending)** — `services.discordsync.immich.enable = true` wires the /lookup page's "Also check Immich" toggle (upstream ADR-062, in the locked rev `06a06b00`): the server proxies hex SHA-1 hashes to Immich's `bulk-upload-check` (`internal/immichclient`; key scoped to `asset.read` + `asset.upload` ONLY — the endpoint's permission guard demands `asset.upload` even though nothing is uploaded). `IMMICH_URL`/`IMMICH_API_KEY` are COLD config validated both-or-neither by the binary (`ErrImmichConfigIncomplete`); both ride the `discordsync-env` sops template, rendered ONLY when the option is on, with the URL defaulting to LOOPBACK `http://127.0.0.1:<ports.immich>` (co-located — no Caddy/DNS/TLS on the lookup path). **The key lives in its own encrypted file `discordsync-immich.yaml`** (split-file precedent): agent sessions can encrypt a NEW file with the public key but CANNOT add a key to `discordsync.yaml` (needs the host age identity; `sudo` is blocked in agent sandboxes). Ships PLACEHOLDER-inert; go-live = create the key in Immich UI → interactive `sops` edit of the yaml → deploy (runbook: docs/services/discordsync.md). `discordsync-immich-verify` (oneshot, daily timer, User=discordsync, reads the rendered template) is the misconfig pager: 401/403 from a REACHABLE Immich = exit 1 + OnFailure (wrong key/scope), while unreachable Immich = WARN skip exit 0 (availability is the Gatus Immich check's job — a down Immich must not double-page as a DiscordSync misconfig). In system-health `extraMonitoredServices`; template's `restartUnits` includes the verify unit CONDITIONALLY (restartUnits must never name a unit absent from the generation — the immich-disabled shape would fail activation).

### Desktop (DMS / Quickshell / Helium)

- **Never pull `graphical-session.target` into the user-manager boot transaction (2026-08-18 black-screen incident)** — any unit enabled via `default.target` that sets `Wants=graphical-session.target` starts the target at lingering boot (before SDDM exists), which starts `niri-session-manager` (`Requires=niri.service`) → a headless zombie niri with zero outputs. SDDM's `niri-session` then exits "A niri session is already running" → black screen, monitor sleeps. Amplifier: `niri-drm-healthcheck` restart-looped the zombie every 2 min. Fixed three ways: (1) `aw-watcher-window-wayland` no longer sets `Wants=` (its gate wrapper waits for the compositor socket instead — indefinitely, session may come hours after boot); (2) `niri.service` carries `ConditionEnvironment=XDG_SESSION_ID` (only present in the manager env after a real SDDM login ran import-environment) and `Restart=on-failure` (a clean exit is logout — `always` respawned a headless niri that blocked the NEXT login); (3) `niri-drm-healthcheck.sh` has the same login-screen guard as `display-watchdog.sh` (never restart niri when no user graphical session exists — a restarted headless niri is just as headless). Do not "fix" silent autostart units by adding `Wants=graphical-session.target`; wait on the compositor socket or use `After=` + a gate wrapper. Also: `writeShellApplication` scripts that shell-parse command output need every binary in `runtimeInputs` (display-watchdog shipped without `gawk` → its login-screen guard silently always said "no session").
  - **Machine-enforced since 2026-08-18:** `modules/nixos/desktop/session-boot-audit.nix` (`services.session-boot-audit`) fails `nix flake check` if ANY user unit reachable from `default.target` Wants/Requires/BindsTo `graphical-session.target` — it graphs NixOS-shape units, HM-shape units (`Unit`/`Install` sections), AND raw-text `systemd.user.units` (parsed), seeds the built-in `default→basic→{paths,sockets,timers}` chain, and reports the full offending chain. Exceptions go in `allowedUnits` with a reason. Negative-tested against the exact historical bug. `niri-drm-healthcheck` (user unit) also carries `ConditionEnvironment=XDG_SESSION_ID` (the healthcheck has nothing to heal pre-login), and the `niri_zombie` metric + Gatus "Niri Zombie Session" check (running niri + no graphical session, 2-check grace for the logout window) is the runtime tripwire. The `XDG_SESSION_ID` condition is fact-backed: `niri-session` imports the FULL login env (`systemctl --user import-environment`, no args) before starting `niri.service`, pam_systemd sets the var in every real session. Regression source: `6ea92969` (2026-08-15) added the fatal `Wants=` while "fixing" the watcher for real boots
  - **Qt6 never had a gtk2 theme — `qt.platformTheme/style.name = "gtk2"` kills Qt6 QQC2 apps** (2026-08-18): every unwrapped QtQuick.Controls app then aborts `module "gtk2" is not installed` (niri-flake-polkit crash-looped 49×, auth dialogs dead). Use `platformTheme.name = "adwaita"` (qadwaitadecorations — a Wayland-decorations plugin; note the HM-set `QT_QPA_PLATFORMTHEME=adwaita` itself resolves NO platformtheme plugin and is inert-by-design) + a built-in QQC2 style (`fusion`). Flagged as debt since the 2026-04-03 audit, ignored until it broke auth. **The adwaita/fusion switch was silently defeated 2026-04-28→09-02 by a stale `QT_STYLE_OVERRIDE = mkForce "kvantum"` session var (removed 2026-09-02): kvantum was NEVER deployed, so every QWidget app ran an unresolvable style override while the config claimed fusion. Qt style selection has exactly ONE owner — the `qt.platformTheme/style` block in home.nix; never hand-set QT_STYLE_OVERRIDE/QT_QUICK_CONTROLS_STYLE. post-deploy-check's "polkit dialog render sanity" fails on any deployed-but-unresolvable Qt style env var and on QQC2 `module ... is not installed` journal aborts.** The runtime polkit agent host is the DMS quickshell instance (QuickAuthDialog.qml)
- **niri-session-manager restore-storm (2026-08-31: 152 empty ghostty terminals per login)** — the manager saves EVERY niri window every 15 min to `~/.local/share/niri-session-manager/session.json` and re-spawns all of them at login. Ghostty is `gtk-single-instance`: one process, many surfaces, each surface saved as a separate window with `terminal_state: null` (content is not restorable) → restore spawns bare `ghostty` per saved window (~20/s, `MAX_SPAWN_CONCURRENCY=5`), every restored empty shell gets re-saved at the next 15-min tick, and the pile only GROWS (+2/login from spawn-at-startup). Fix: `com.mitchellh.ghostty` MUST stay in `single_instance_apps` — the app lists now live in **`platforms/nixos/users/niri-session-manager-apps.nix` (single source of truth since 2026-09-02)**: it feeds the generated config.toml in home.nix, carries `mkInvariantViolations` (asserted at HM eval time AND by `tests/test-niri-session-config.nix` with negative cases — every terminal app-id ∈ single_instance_apps; transient dialogs like `gcr-prompter`/`xdg-desktop-portal-gtk` ∈ skip_apps). ALL terminal app-ids are single-instance at restore time (one spawn per terminal, incl. multi-process ones — an empty shell has no state worth N copies); `emacs` pinned preemptively for the daemon class (dormant Mod+Shift+E keybind, not installed). A literal restartTriggers on the config is deliberately NOT wired: upstream restore re-runs on EVERY process start under `Restart=always`, so a mid-session restart replays the spawn storm — the `niri_session_manager_config_stale` metric (niri-health collector) makes the until-next-login window observable instead. Cleaning a poisoned session: kill the single-instance ghostty pid FIRST (else the next periodic save re-records every surface), then rewrite session.json — and `trash` the `session-*.bak` files too (a "corrupt" file makes the manager fall back to the newest valid BACKUP, resurrecting the storm). Upstream hazards (LarsArtmann/niri-session-manager v0.3.0): restore re-runs on EVERY process start (one crash = another full spawn storm), and save does not dedupe same-pid single-instance surfaces
- **Auto-discovered modules are FLAKE-PARTS wrappers, not bare NixOS modules** — files under `modules/nixos/{services,desktop}/` are imported into the flake-parts module set; they MUST declare `flake.nixosModules.<filename> = { config, lib, ... }: { ... };` (see `niri-config.nix` / `session-boot-audit.nix` for the shape). A bare NixOS module `{ config, lib, ... }` in those dirs evaluates WITHOUT error in the flake-parts namespace and silently contributes NOTHING to hosts — nothing imports it, no options appear, no assertions fire. Symptom: your new module's options "don't exist" on the host despite clean evals. **WORSE variant (signoz-coverage, 2026-08-31 19:35): a bare module whose `let` bindings force `config.systemd.services` (e.g. for eval-time assertions) does NOT evaluate silently — it HARD-fails every nix command with `attribute 'systemd' missing` until wrapped; a mid-flight session committing such a file blocks ALL deploys.** When un-wrapping a foreign bare module, also remember: `...` in the lambda pattern takes NO trailing comma, and the outer attrset needs its own closing brace
- **`nix eval …config.system.build.toplevel.drvPath` does NOT check NixOS assertions** — assertion checking is only forced by `nix flake check` (pre-commit + CI). An assertion-failing config evaluates cleanly to a drvPath; verifying a guard REQUIRES `nix flake check --no-build` (or an actual build). Also latent: forcing ALL assertion messages (e.g. `nix eval --json …config.assertions`) crashes on the sops templates module (`owner: ${cfg.owner}` with owner=null when a template sets only uid) — only failed assertions' messages are coerced in normal operation
- **`extendModules` probe overrides: hand-rolled override attrsets now need `content`, NOT `value`, and a PLAIN `enable = false` in configuration.nix forces probe overrides to priority ≤ 50 (2026-09-23, offsite-borg positive-render probe; both error shapes re-verified live against the locked nixpkgs 2026-09-24)** — two stacked traps for probe authors: (1) the module-system override wrapper's inner field is `content` (`mkOverride = priority: content: { _type = "override"; inherit priority content; }`), so the long-documented `{ _type = "override"; priority = 50; value = true; }` literal dies `error: attribute 'content' missing` at `lib/modules.nix:1458` (the `strip` function reads `def.value.content`) — never hand-roll the attrset, write `lib.mkOverride 50 true` (`mkForce` IS `mkOverride 50`); (2) plain values carry priority 100 (`defaultOverridePriority`), so the host's plain `enable = false` conflicts with a probe's plain `enable = true` — `has conflicting definition values … Use lib.mkForce value or lib.mkDefault value to change the priority` — probes must override at priority ≤ 50, and a probe that must beat an EXISTING `mkForce` needs priority < 50 (configuration.nix's plain `enable = false` is exactly why `offsite-borg-positive-render` uses `mkOverride 50 true`)
- **Eval-time `readFile`/`readDir` on a BUILT package output forces its realization — `error: path '<pkg>.drv' is not valid` after every nixpkgs bump (2026-09-06, niri)** — `niri-config.nix` read its user unit texts from `${niriPkg}/lib/systemd/user`, so EVERY `nix eval` / `nix flake check --no-build` required niri to be physically present in the store; each nixpkgs bump changes the drv, and the pre-deploy gate died at eval until niri happened to be rebuilt (deploy evals survived only because build-allowed evals realize niri mid-eval — the gate's dependence on store state was invisible while the store was warm). Fix: read unit texts from the niri SOURCE tree (`${niriPkg.src}/resources/` — pure, the fetch is always available at eval) and replicate niri-flake's single install substitution (`ExecStart=niri` → `ExecStart=$out/bin/niri`; its `replace-service-with-usr-bin` branch is the nixpkgs meson flavor, not niri-flake's). Generated units verified byte-identical to the packaged ones. General rule: eval-time code must never coerce a package OUTPUT (readFile/readDir/hashFile/`/. +`) — coerce `src`, `passthru`, or plain interpolations only
- **DMS `inputs.nixpkgs.follows` MANDATORY** — Mismatched Qt causes silent runtime crashes.
- **DMS modals already follow the focused monitor** — Spotlight/clipboard/emoji/keybinds modals open on niri's focused output (upstream fix #869, included since DMS 1.5.3). No settings key needed; there is NO way to pin modals to a screen. `notificationFocusedMonitor` only affects notification popups. Monitor navigation binds: `Mod+Ctrl+H/J/K/L` (or arrows) = focus monitor, `+Shift` = move column/window to monitor. **Multiscreen "Now" batch (2026-08-22):** `Mod+Tab` = `focus-monitor-next`, `Mod+Shift+Tab` = `move-column-to-monitor-next`, `Mod+Ctrl+Tab` = `move-workspace-to-monitor-next` — Tab cycles monitors without edge-crossing rebinds. Named-workspace jumps: `Mod+M/B/E/C/V` → main/browser/dev/chat/media. When 2nd LG arrives: clone DP-1 output entry + rebind `Mod+H/L`. Named workspaces pinned via `open-on-output` (DP-1 = main/browser/dev, DP-2 = chat/media; missing output falls back to primary). Rofi (Sway backup) uses `monitor = "-1"` = focused monitor.
- **DMS `systemd.enable` defaults false** — MUST explicitly set `programs.dank-material-shell.systemd.enable = true`.
- **DMS owns wallpaper management** — awww RETIRED. `enableDynamicTheming = false` (matugen conflicts with Catppuccin Mocha).
- **DMS notification conflict** — Dunst disabled. DMS owns `org.freedesktop.Notifications`.
- **DMS config split-brain** — Both `settings.json` and `plugin_settings.json` are HM-managed symlinks (NOT user-owned). DMS may replace the `settings.json` symlink with a real file at runtime (expanding 19 declarative keys to 530+ with defaults + UI changes). `deploy.sh` backs up real-file versions before `nh os switch` overwrites them. UI changes to either file are ephemeral — only declarative Nix settings survive rebuilds.
- **Hardened root units that `rm` foreign-owned trees need `CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` in the `harden{}` call (2026-09-06, `nix-build-cleanup`)** — build sandboxes are `nixbld`-owned (0700 dirs + Go modcache trees made read-only), and `harden{}`'s default empty `CapabilityBoundingSet` strips root's DAC override, so every cleanup run died in a wall of `rm: Permission denied` (the same class was observed and never fixed since 2026-07-21). Fix: `CapabilityBoundingSet = "CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE"` inside the `harden {}` argument (niri-config precedent; first post-fix run freed 2.6 GB). Companion: deploy.sh STARTs `nix-build-cleanup.service` post-switch every deploy — stc never restarts inactive oneshots on unit-file change, so without a per-deploy run a broken unit sits unverified until the 4h timer (silent-failure history: 2026-07-18, 2026-08-16, 2026-09-06). Related symptom rule: `service-health-check.service` failing is just the checker reporting OTHER failed units — debug its list, not the checker.
- **Textfile collectors must write a UNIQUE tmp (`mktemp`), never `$OUT.tmp`, when the unit is hardened root (2026-09-05)** — the sticky 1777 textfile dir eventually collects a FOREIGN-owned leftover (one manual run as another user is enough), and `harden{}`'s empty CapabilityBoundingSet means root cannot `open(O_TRUNC)` a file it doesn't own: the collector fails every tick and the failing oneshot blocks EVERY deploy at test-activation (Exited(4)). Live class: a lars-owned `niri.prom.tmp` from a manual Sep-3 debugging run blocked `niri-health-metrics` for ~2 days and failed the 2026-09-04 deploy. Fix pattern (niri-config.nix): `TMP=$(mktemp "$DIR/<name>.XXXXXX")` + `chmod 644` (mktemp is 0600; DynamicUser node_exporter needs o+r) + `trap 'rm -f "$TMP"' EXIT` (no leak when a later step fails) + `CapabilityBoundingSet = "CAP_FOWNER CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE"` on the unit so the final `mv` over a foreign-owned target also can't wedge (memory-emergency-guard precedent). **Repo-wide enforcement (2026-09-06): `scripts/audit-textfile-tmp.sh` (pre-commit + CI) rejects fixed-tmp writes AND `/run/secrets-rendered` literals in every .nix file; all collectors were converted after mail-relay became the second live detonation (2026-09-02..06, see its section).**
- **cliphist RETIRED** — DMS owns clipboard history exclusively.
- **Quickshell `Process` has NO `onFailed`** — Use `onStreamFinished` with text-length check.
- **ScriptModel UAF (unfixed upstream)** — Quickshell 0.3.0 + Qt 6.11.1 use-after-free in ScriptModel. Mitigation: `Restart=always` + `StartLimitBurst=30`.
- **SDDM hides boot logs** — `console=tty2` redirects boot messages. `Ctrl+Alt+F2` = full log.
- **Helium GPU SIGBUS** — `--disable-gpu-watchdog` in wrapper. `--enable-zero-copy` was REMOVED (2026-08-22) to test whether it causes display hotplug crashes (zero-copy GPU buffer manager doesn't always handle output removal gracefully). If removing it prevents crashes, `--disable-gpu-watchdog` may also become unnecessary. Re-evaluate after observing hotplug behavior.
- **Helium zero-output death** — Niri has no virtual output support. When all outputs disconnect, Helium exits cleanly. `Restart=always` + `RestartSec=5`.
- **Helium video throttling (3 FPS)** — Missing anti-throttling flags caused Chromium to throttle video to 1-3 FPS when tab was backgrounded or scrolled out of view in niri. Four flags added: `--disable-background-timer-throttling`, `--disable-backgrounding-occluded-windows`, `--disable-renderer-backgrounding`, `--disable-background-media-suspend`. Brave/Darwin config already had the first three — they were never ported to Helium Linux.
- **helium-launch waits indefinitely** — No timeout (timeout caused empty-window loop). Becomes a monitor that blocks until existing instance dies.
- **Helium is Chromium 151** — Full ungoogled-chromium fork, NOT Electron. Widevine bundled separately.
- **VA-API flag renames (Chromium 131+)** — `VaapiVideoDecodeLinuxGL` → `AcceleratedVideoDecodeLinuxGL`, etc. Since 143+, VA-API works out of box; explicit flags are defense-in-depth.
- **`--disable-background-networking` kills extensions** — Blocks ExtensionDownloader. Do NOT re-add (Helium's ungoogled base already strips Google telemetry).
- **niri-flake libdisplay-info stale pin** — niri-flake's `make-niri` pins `libdisplay-info_0_2` via `callPackage` with `assert version == "0.2.0"`. nixpkgs removed that alias (it throws). niri's Cargo.lock uses `libdisplay-info-sys` 0.3.0 (requires C library `< 0.4.0` via pkg-config), but the real C library is 0.4.0 (backward compatible — APIs only added). The `niriLibdisplayInfoShim` overlay in `overlays/linux.nix` patches both: (1) derivation `version = "0.2.0"` to pass the assert, (2) `.pc` file `Version:` line via sed regex to `0.3.0` for pkg-config. Uses `__intentionallyOverridingVersion = true` to silence the nixpkgs warning. MUST be first in the overlay list (before niri's own overlay). Remove when niri-flake drops the pin.

- **Signal Desktop settings storage (2026-09-14)** — UI settings do NOT live in a plain config file: theme/chat color live in the SQLCipher-encrypted `~/.config/Signal/sql/db.sqlite` (`items` table + per-conversation `conversationColor` rows; key in `config.json`, `sqlite3` reads nothing). THE exception: **theme** — the renderer reads `themeSetting` via IPC from the Electron main process, which reads `ephemeral.json` FIRST (`theme-setting`: light/dark/system; verified against the deployed 8.25.0 bundle + upstream `settingsChannel.main.ts` `EPHEMERAL_NAME_MAP`). Wired declaratively as `activation.signal-theme` in `platforms/nixos/users/home.nix` (jq-MERGE, never symlink — Signal rewrites the file with window geometry; skips while `signal-desktop` runs; seeds fresh profiles; jq missing-file branch is REQUIRED or `set -eu` aborts HM activation). **Chat color is NOT declaratively configurable** — the ChatColorPicker writes per-conversation `conversationColor` rows into the encrypted conversations store (green presets: forest, wintergreen, basil, sea, lagoon; set once in the UI). No home-manager signal module exists. Investigation: `docs/status/archived/2026-09-14_16-16_signal-green-nix-declarative-config-capability-report.md`.

### SearXNG

- **`enable` infinite recursion** — Wrapper MUST NOT set `services.searx.enable` inside `lib.mkIf cfg.enable` where `cfg = config.services.searx`.
- **No `restartTriggers` in direct server mode** — nixpkgs only sets them for uWSGI mode. SystemNix adds them.
- **Port 8889** (not 8888 — SigNoz OTel collector owns 8888).
- **Engine init never retried** — Engines that fail network during `init()` at boot stay permanently disabled. DNS-gate `mkDnsGate` in `ExecStartPre` ensures DNS is ready.
- **`formats = [ "html" ]`** — Blocks JSON API (403). Deliberate privacy hardening. Test in HTML mode.
- **`autocomplete = "duckduckgo"`** — Google leaked every keystroke.
- **XFF health-check noise is benign** — `/healthz` is exempt from limiter.

### Other Services

- **`pkgs.nss` is libs-only** — `certutil` is in `pkgs.nss.tools`.
- **ClickHouse `background_pool_size` sanity check trap** — Reducing `background_pool_size` below the default (16) triggers cascading `Code: 36 BAD_ARGUMENTS` startup sanity checks: every `number_of_free_entries_in_pool_*` merge_tree setting (defaults 20/8/25) must be less than `background_pool_size * background_merges_mutations_concurrency_ratio` (default 2), and each fails one-at-a-time (whack-a-mole through deploys). Keep `background_pool_size` at default. The other 4 pool reductions (`background_schedule_pool_size=8`, `background_buffer_flush_schedule_pool_size=4`, `background_move_pool_size=2`, `background_fetches_pool_size=1`) are safe — no sanity-checked dependents (~145 threads saved). An eval-time assertion in `signoz.nix` catches `background_pool_size=2`
- **ClickHouse `Type=notify` without sd_notify** — Upstream sets `Type=notify` but the binary never calls `sd_notify`. SystemNix overrides `Type=exec`. Remove when upstream adds sd_notify
- **ClickHouse internal self-logs are unbounded + 1 Hz samplers (fixed 2026-08-17)** — the `system` db held 52 GiB / 9.13B rows (90% of the data dir): log tables had no TTLs, and `metric_log` (`collect_interval_milliseconds=1000`) + `asynchronous_metric_log` (`asynchronous_metrics_update_period_s=1`) sampled at 1 Hz (async alone: ~750 rows/s, 56M rows/day). Fixed in `signoz.nix`: 14d TTLs for all log families (config XML covers fresh tables) + `signoz-clickhouse-log-ttl.service` converging existing tables incl. zombie `<log>_N` copies from unclean restarts (stale, ~30 GiB, decay via TTL) + daily 04:20 timer. **`flush_interval_milliseconds` is the WRONG knob for volume** — it only batches rows into parts; row rate scales with the collect/update interval (verified live: flush 2000ms left the rate at 1 Hz). `metric_log` is WIDE (~274 KB column metadata): `MODIFY TTL` exceeds `max_query_size` (QUERY_IS_TOO_LARGE) → the script falls back to monthly partition drops, and its config block carries only the collect-interval halving. 13 permanently READ-ONLY zombies (TABLE_IS_PERMANENTLY_READ_ONLY: trace_log_14..18, query_metric_log_4..6, etc.) cannot be ALTERed via SQL — the script reports them for a human `DROP TABLE` decision (~10 GiB reclaim available)
- **PMA commit blackout (2026-08-22..09-02: 11 days, ~3,800 failed commits — fixed)** — every auto-commit failed on `429 rate_limit_error (2056)` while service liveness stayed GREEN the whole time. Four stacked causes: (1) minimax's Token Plan exhausted (billing state — the same dead key class as the crush minimax disable); (2) the daemon's go-commit flake input was pinned at `7321133`, which PREDATES `22f0e4c` (v0.8.0) where `DefaultChainFromEnv` learned `OPENAI_BASE_URL` — the SystemNix env vars sat in the daemon's environment pointing at FastFlowLM while the BUILT binary ignored them and sent OpenAI requests to api.openai.com with key `local` (eternal 401); (3) NO fallback — go-commit fails the whole commit when message generation fails (its retry middleware exists but is never wired in), and PMA's flat 5-min/project cooldown retried every dirty project for 11 days straight; (4) NOTHING monitored commit OUTCOMES. Fixes: PMA input → `22f0e4c` (the flake-lock pin can LAG the go.mod requirement — the prepared-source replace wins at build time; always check `jq '.nodes.go-commit.locked.rev'`), committer heuristic fallback on `errorfamily.Code(err)=="commit.generate"` (generation runs BEFORE staging → side-effect-free retry with `chore: auto-commit N changed file(s) (heuristic) on <branch>`, `Result.Fallback` + WARN journal line `committed via heuristic fallback`), escalating failure cooldown (5m doubling → 4h cap, resets on success), `MINIMAX_API_KEY` dropped from `pma-env` (empty template kept — never re-add an external provider key without a fallback plan), and `system_pma_commit_{scrape_errors,failures_1h,failures_over_threshold,heuristic_fallbacks_24h,fallbacks_over_threshold}` textfile metrics + the "PMA Commit Health" Gatus check (failures ≥3/h pages = blackout class; fallbacks ≥20/24h = LLM path dead while commits keep landing). First FastFlowLM-bound commits after a cold idle may ride the fallback once (30s HTTP timeout vs 2-5min cold load) — that self-heals as the socket warms; sustained fallbacks alert instead

- **PMA LLM-commit REGRESSION RECURRENCE (2026-09-07, fixed same day — the blackout's cause #2 came back through lock churn)** — PMA commit `6d4cb619` ("chore: auto-commit 313 changed file(s) (heuristic)") flipped PMA's own flake.lock go-commit pin BACK from `22f0e4c` to `7321133`, silently un-applying the 2026-09-02 fix: the deployed daemon ignored `OPENAI_BASE_URL`, sent every request to api.openai.com with key `local`, and 401-fell-back on 100% of commits. Diagnostic signatures that separate the failure classes: INSTANT fallbacks (`duration=170-900ms` = an HTTPS RTT to api.openai.com, ZERO connections to :52625) = env-chain dead / stale vendored rev; `duration=30s` fallbacks = real HTTP attempts timing out against flm (cold load or the v1.0.2 crash-loop window). Diagnostic chain that pinned it in ~30 min: (1) `go version -m <deployed-binary>` shows the go.mod REQUIREMENT (`v0.8.0`) but the `=> ./_local_deps/go-commit (devel)` REPLACE wins at build time — the requirement version LIES about the vendored code; (2) the vendored rev is decided by PMA's OWN flake.lock, not SystemNix's — SystemNix's root `go-commit` node was healthy at `9dfbf1e` while PMA's lock subtree carried the stale pin (consumer-subtree-drift class); (3) the regression mechanism: the PMA lock node's `original.url` had a hard `?rev=7321133` baked in from an old flake.nix edit, so any lock re-run re-pinned BACKWARD (same class as the rev-pin silent-no-op trap). Fixes deployed: PMA `1c144c8d` (re-lock to go-commit `9dfbf1e`, which also carries the SSRF `withTrustedBaseURL` bypass REQUIRED for loopback `OPENAI_BASE_URL` — post-v0.8.0 go-commit rejects 127.0.0.1 without it, the next trap in that direction) + PMA `9bbc7dfb` (vendorHash refresh — go-commit 9dfbf1e's dep refresh invalidates the FOD hash even with zero PMA go.mod changes). **Rule: after ANY projects-management-automation input bump, check the PMA lock's go-commit rev ≥ `22f0e4c` AND that `DefaultChainFromEnv` still reads `OPENAI_BASE_URL` in the vendored tree before deploying** (docs/todo/upstream.md carries the upstream CI guard). Verify with `journalctl -u projects-management-automation | grep "committed changes"` — LLM commits log `duration=10-20s` with NO `heuristic fallback` tag
- **PMA page-cache death-loop containment** — PMA reads 260+ git repos during discovery, charging ~16 GB of page cache to its cgroup. Without `MemoryHigh`, reclaim loops at 91% CPU (`memory.events max` climbing, `oom_kill = 0` — page cache is reclaimable) until system-wide PSI freezes the kernel. Contained by: `MemoryHigh=12G`, `MemoryMax=16G`, `CPUQuota=200%`, `MemorySwapMax=0`, `PMA_COMMITTER_WORKERS=2`, `PMA_DISCOVERY_WORKERS=2`, `ManagedOOMPreference=omit`. **Ceiling retuned 2026-08-14:** the 6G/8G ceiling from the 2026-08-09 analysis fell below the scan's grown working set — every restart pinned memory at ~6.3G in permanent direct reclaim and the discovery daemon goroutine starved (unix socket accepted connections, never answered; 3 hangs in one day, incl. a 21h one that 503'd Overview the whole time). 12G high restores scan headroom (pre-incident scans completed under a 16G max); 16G max keeps the freeze-proof hard bound. **`pma-daemon-watchdog`** (every 5 min) probes the daemon socket liveness and restarts PMA after 2 failed probes 30s apart — the hang class is "hung but active", invisible to systemd. Note: Overview has `partOf` PMA, so every PMA restart bounces Overview (it re-discovers on start). The `system-health` collector emits `system_service_memory_events_{max,high}` metrics with a Gatus "Memory Events Thrash" alert (collector runs as root with `ProtectSystem=full` — `/sys/fs/cgroup` unaffected). Full narrative: `docs/crash-analysis-2026-08-09.md`
- **Wise SCA (Strong Customer Authentication) gates bank-sync balance statements (~every 90 days, UK/EEA profiles)** — Wise answers `GET /v1/profiles/{id}/balance-statements/{id}/statement.json` with **403 + EMPTY body**; verdict + one-time token (OTT) live in the `x-2fa-approval-result`/`x-2fa-approval` RESPONSE HEADERS, which wise-go < v0.6.1 discarded (a bare unexplained 403 → zero transactions synced). Since bank-sync 2026-09-16 the OTT VALUE is never logged (bool `approval_token_issued` only). Renewal, in order: **the dashboard approval flow** (bank-sync ≥ 2026-09-16, `run` mode — Sync State card → "Wise approval needed" → Approve now: probe → channel → OTP → verify → auto-sync, riding the daemon's own Wise creds; OTT held in a server-side session, never rendered), then the CLI `bank-sync sca status` / `sca approve` (same Wise env as the daemon; `sca status` is the ONLY sanctioned token-value surface), then break-glass env drop-in `BANK_SYNC_WISE_SCA_APPROVAL_TOKEN=<ott>` for exactly one cycle (the service still carries `EnvironmentFile = ["-/var/lib/bank-sync-sca/token.env"]` — root-owned 0400, `-` makes absence a no-op). Full runbook: `docs/services/bank-sync-sca.md`. A plain 403 WITHOUT 2FA headers = personal-token regional restriction instead (statements unsupported outside US/CA/AU/NZ/SG/MY) — different fix
- **Wise API rejects non-UTC timestamps with 422 `wrong.date.format` (fixed 2026-08-21, wise-go v0.8.1)** — Wise demands `YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm:ssZ`; a numeric zone offset (`+02:00`) is refused. wise-go < v0.8.1 formatted caller-local `time.Time` as-is, and bank-sync's sync window `to` bound is host-local `time.Now()` — EVERY `/v1/transfers` call 422'd for ~2.5 days (2026-08-19..21), including the SCA transfers fallback that exists to ride out statement challenges (silent partial degradation: balances synced, transfers didn't; only per-run "15 sync failure(s)" journal lines revealed it). wise-go's `formatWiseTimestamp` now normalizes all outgoing query timestamps (`createdDateStart/End`, `intervalStart/End`, rates `time`) to UTC; callers may pass any zone. Symptom signature: `wise: api error (422): wrong.date.format` with `createdDateEnd=…%2B02%3A00` in the URL
- **bank-sync SQLite migration v5 MUST be idempotent + record its version (fixed 2026-08-21)** — the original v5 ran a bare `ALTER TABLE … ADD COLUMN last_error_code` and never wrote version 5 to `schema_migrations` (only v4 recorded itself; the fresh path seeds 1-5). On the pool DB (v4-era) the first start applied the column, and the FIRST RESTART crash-looped with `duplicate column name: last_error_code` → exit 69 → start-limit-hit (bank-sync down until the heal). migrateToV5 now checks `PRAGMA table_info`, skips an existing column, and records version 5 in the same transaction. General rule for this schema: every incremental migration must (a) record its version atomically with its changes and (b) tolerate re-execution — SQLite `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN` has no `IF NOT EXISTS`
- **bank-sync statement_coverage RFC3339 corruption — tolerant read + V10 canonicalization (fix committed locally 2026-09-06, UNPUSHED; writer never identified)** — `[corruption] db.scan: unparseable sync_states.statement_coverage` fired on every 5-min sync per balance because the mapper parsed the column with a single canonical layout while the stored values were bare RFC3339 (`2026-09-06T13:36:24Z`). Fix (bank-sync `a9b0b8e..60015cc`): `parseStoredTimestamp` tolerant multi-layout read (canonical + RFC3339 variants, static sentinel for err113) + migration V10 canonicalizing existing rows (V4 discipline: raw-BLOB read, Go rewrite, one tx incl. version record; `''` sentinel untouched) + 3 Ginkgo specs. **Forensics that did NOT add up (2026-09-06, exhaustive):** NO bank-sync binary can write that format — proven three ways: the only writer (SyncStateProjection `internal/cqrs/projections.go:~206`) binds `time.Time` (born that way in 5a66473); modernc v1.53–v1.57 defaults to `t.String()` (`… +0000 UTC`) and `_time_format=sqlite` (in every V8-era binary via `ensureSQLiteDriverOptions`, Aug 19) writes canonical `2006-01-02 15:04:05.999999999-07:00` (driver source + live probe both verified); and the DEPLOYED binary run end-to-end (`bank-sync demo` against a scratch DB) wrote canonical `2026-09-06 14:20:28+00:00`. No triggers, no schema defaults, no sqlc writes (read layer is pure SELECT), no second process (full /proc cmdline+fd scans), no timers/cron/user units. Yet the values ADVANCED every ~15:04 min from 2026-08-31 12:30 until 2026-09-06 13:51:49 — exactly when SCA paused the last statement-backed syncs — tracking statement-sync successes (the reads' "falling back to initial window" then re-trigger). The fix is correct regardless: tolerant reads stop the error loop, V10 heals history, and if the phantom RFC3339 writer returns post-SCA-approval, tolerant reads keep working (mixed formats are harmless; the corruption ERROR was the only failure). Watch after SCA approval: if new RFC3339 values appear, the writer is statement-sync-coupled and INSIDE the daemon despite the proofs — dump goroutines then. Counter resets + corruption lines stop only after push + `nix flake lock --update-input bank-sync` + deploy (vendorHash likely stable: go.mod untouched)
- **Deploy generation mismatch — and CHECK THE ANCHOR FIRST after ANY deploy rc** — `readlink /nix/var/nix/profiles/system` vs `/run/current-system` is step 1 after every deploy exit code, BEFORE diagnosing the smoke verdict: the 2026-09-20 rc=3 deploy had ALREADY exit-4'd at activation (cv-server perms + hot-user-caches chmod/cycle + db-heal timeout stacked) and the smoke NEW-failure verdict was secondary to the un-anchored profile — a crash or reboot in that window reverts the running system. `nix eval` may show correct values but deployed unit has STALE values. Deploy AGAIN if they differ. **Mechanism (2026-09-06): when activation exit-4s, nh advances `/run/current-system` but SKIPS the numbered-profile bump** — the running system then sits one generation ahead of `/nix/var/nix/profiles/system` and a REBOOT REVERTS it (deploy.sh's anchoring warning fires; live: system-770 stayed on the old build while the newer one ran). Fix the failing unit, then re-run `nix run .#deploy` — a clean activation creates the missing generation.
- **A `/nix` store migration's reboot gate is only valid until the NEXT deploy (2026-09-07 stuck boot)** — after the Samsung flip + `--final` sync (09-05), a parallel session deployed gens 762-775 on the still-live QLC store (09-06); the reboot auto-booted the new loader default whose `init=` path existed ONLY on the QLC `@nix`, while the flipped fstab mounts `/nix` exclusively from the Samsung → frozen pre-journald (zero journal + zero wtmp artifacts for the hung boot = the signature of an initrd/exec-init hang). Diagnosis order for "stuck in boot": `journalctl --list-boots` + `last -x` (which phase died) → `loader.conf` default + per-entry `init=` existence against the LIVE store (`[ -e "$init" ]`) — generation TITLES/numbers lie across a store swap, and after a store swap the SAME generation number maps to different toplevels per store. Recovery pattern that worked: hand-set `loader.conf default` to a store-verified entry (immediate reboot-safety) → deploy repo HEAD so new builds land on the live store → re-run after exit-4 to complete profile+bootloader → `stc boot` prunes the dead entries itself. **Prevention shipped:** `scripts/pre-reboot-check.sh` (`nix run .#pre-reboot-check`) audits the chain before any planned reboot.
- **`/nix/var/nix/gcroots/profiles` was a DEAD CALAMARES-INSTALLER SYMLINK since machine install (`/tmp/calamares-root-*/…`) — profile generations were NEVER GC-rooted (found 2026-09-09)**. With `nix.gc --delete-older-than 3d`, every generation was therefore unreachable; systems survived only via `gcroots/auto` indirect roots (each deploy's `result` symlink), so every era's closures died a few deploys later — this retroactively explains months of boot entries pointing at deleted store paths. The Samsung migration rsync CARRIED the broken symlink onto the new store. Damage when found: 14 of 16 menu entries pointed at closures deleted from BOTH stores (11 fully GC'd; 3 half-deleted files-without-db orphans = freeze-interrupted GC signature; `nix copy --from file:///mnt/btrfs-root/@nix` is NOT a recovery path — the QLC db calls them invalid). Fix (runtime repair, nothing declarative regenerates it — re-verify after any store re-provisioning): `ln -sfn /nix/var/nix/profiles /nix/var/nix/gcroots/profiles`. Ladder doctrine now: a SHORT closure-verified ladder pinned under `/nix/var/nix/gcroots/boot-rollback-ladder/` (current + one prior-lock-era system); an entry whose closure is gone is a guaranteed-stuck-boot trap and gets PRUNED, never kept (backup `/root/stuckboot-entry-backup/ladder-20260909/`). ESP boot assets mirror to the reserved Samsung p1 (`SAMSUNG-EFI`) WITHOUT bootloader binaries (`EFI/boot`, `EFI/systemd` excluded) — data copy for manual EFI-stub recovery only, cannot hijack firmware boot order. Full forensics: `docs/status/archived/2026-09-09_04-35_sync-more-things-rollback-ladder-forensics.md`
- **Forgejo SSH keys** — Forgejo doesn't read NixOS `openssh.authorizedKeys.keys`. Provisioned via admin API (`forgejo-ssh-keys` oneshot).
- **Forgejo `GET /admin/users/{u}/keys` → 405** — Use public `GET /api/v1/users/{u}/keys` for dedup. POST stays on admin path.
- **OpenSEO GSC callback** — Hand-rolled Caddy vHost (NOT `protectedVHost`) to exempt `/api/gsc/oauth/callback` from forward-auth. Do NOT simplify.
- **Browser History: WebAuthn + OAuth2, direct TLS proxy** — Caddy uses plain `reverse_proxy` (NOT `protectedVHost`). Forward-auth would intercept WebAuthn/OAuth2 API calls and break registration/login. The app has built-in passkey auth PLUS native OAuth2/OIDC via Pocket ID. OTel uses gRPC (port 4317, NOT 4318) because the Go code uses `otlptracegrpc`, not `otlptracehttp`.
- **Browser History: LoadCredential for OIDC secret bridging** — The OIDC oneshot runs inside `ProtectSystem=strict` (from `harden {}`). It CANNOT read `/var/lib/pocket-id/client-secrets/browser-history` directly. Use `LoadCredential = [ "pocket-id-secret:${config.services.pocket-id.dataDir}/client-secrets/browser-history" ]` — systemd reads the file as PID 1 at service start, makes it available via `$CREDENTIALS_DIRECTORY/pocket-id-secret`. Same pattern as Forgejo (`forgejo.nix:302-303`).
- **Browser History: DynamicUser StateDirectory isolation** — When the server has `DynamicUser=true` + `StateDirectory=browser-history`, systemd creates `/var/lib/browser-history/` owned by a random dynamic UID (mode 0700). No other service can read or write there. The OIDC oneshot MUST use its own `StateDirectory=browser-history-oidc` to write the OAuth2 env file.
- **Browser History: upstream `optionalEnv` env-var split** — Upstream `optionalEnv "OAUTH2_POCKET_ID_CLIENT_ID" cfg.oauth2.pocketId.clientId` ALWAYS emits the env var as a systemd `Environment` directive when the module option is set, even if the client secret is missing. This triggers OAuth2 provider creation → `ProviderConfig.Validate()` rejects empty ClientSecret → crash-loop. Fix: do NOT set `clientId`/`issuer` via module options; route all 3 vars through the OIDC oneshot env file.
- **Browser History: agent→server startup race** — When server+agent are co-located (evo-x2), both restart simultaneously during deploy. The server is `Type=simple` (marked "active" before Go binds the port), the agent is `Type=oneshot` with only 4 retries per batch (~7s). The agent races ahead, gets 502 from Caddy, fails all 4 retries, exits 1, and blocks the deploy with "Activation (test) failed: exit status 4". Fix: `after = ["browser-history.service"]` + `wants` + `ExecStartPre` health-gate that polls `http://127.0.0.1:8087/health` with `curl --retry 30 --retry-delay 2` (60s max). `TimeoutStartSec=2min` for slow boot disk I/O. **The agent timer is the retry (2026-08-18):** `Restart=no` + `startLimitBurst=5/300s` — see the systemd gotcha on Restart racing timers.
- **Browser History: the agent gate must accept ANY answered HTTP status, never demand 200 — the agent-freshness LIVENESS DEADLOCK (2026-09-20)** — since lock `10fe5d8a` the server carries upstream's `AGENT_FRESHNESS` health component: a restarted server answers 503 "degraded" (db ok, `agents.expected=true`) until the FIRST agent ingest lands. The old gate (`curl -sf --retry-all-errors`) treated 503 as not-ready → the agent could never run → no ingest → the server never recovered: the pair wedged for 90+ min after the 11:25 deploy restart (ZERO `/ingest` requests, agent aborting "server not ready after 7min" every timer tick). The gate now proceeds on any `[1-9][0-9][0-9]` status — a degraded-but-answering server can accept exactly the ingest that heals it. General rule: a readiness gate waits for the endpoint to ANSWER (bind/race window), never for full health — health-gating a producer against a consumer-dependent health signal is a structural deadlock.
- **Browser History: registration locked to single user** — `POST /auth/register` was open to anyone on LAN. Now gated by cqrs-htmx `ServiceConfig.MaxUsers` (0=unlimited, N=N users max). BOTH creation paths are gated: `Service.Register()` AND OAuth2 first-login auto-provisioning in `OAuth2Service.matchOrCreateUser` (a second user cannot be created via "Login with Pocket ID"; existing users always log in). Both return `ErrRegistrationClosed` (HTTP 403). The check-then-dispatch window is serialized by a shared registration mutex (TOCTOU-safe for in-process sync projections). browser-history exposes `MAX_USERS` env (default 1). SystemNix sets `MAX_USERS=1` explicitly. The `importUsers()` batch path is NOW gated too (upstream cqrs-htmx `68a2cd68`, 2026-09-18: holds the registration mutex for the whole batch + skips users past MaxUsers with "registration is closed") — not yet consumed by SystemNix until the cqrs-htmx tag + browser-history bump chain lands (TODO_LIST backlog).
- **Browser History: OIDC discovery at startup requires dnsblockd-ready gate (v4.7.0+, deploy 2026-08-16)** — Starting with browser-history input `4e7604d`, the server performs OIDC discovery (`GET https://auth.${domain}/.well-known/openid-configuration`) at startup when `OAUTH2_POCKET_ID_*` env vars are set. If dnsblockd isn't bound on `127.0.0.1:53` yet, Go's resolver falls through to `9.9.9.9`, which has no `auth.home.lan` → `no such host` → exit code 69 (UNAVAILABLE) in 2.1s. The previous `after = ["pocket-id.service" "pocket-id-provision.service" "browser-history-oidc-setup.service"]` is NOT sufficient — dnsblockd must also be up. **Fix:** use `mkOidcGate` from `lib/default.nix` (same helper Gatus + oauth2-proxy use). Adds `dnsblockd.service` to `after`/`wants` AND an `ExecStartPre` curl probe that polls the OIDC discovery endpoint with 60×2s retries before starting the server. Without this, every simultaneous restart of dnsblockd + browser-history (e.g. deploy) crashes the server. **The gate does NOT cover a dnsblockd stop AFTER the gate passed (live 2026-09-06 18:00):** deploy.sh used to restart browser-history BEFORE dnsblockd, so the gate polled the OLD daemon, dnsblockd stopped/reloaded its 3.9M blocklist mid-flight, and the Go resolver fell through to 9.9.9.9 → exit 69 → 2-min RestartSec self-heal. deploy.sh now restarts dnsblockd (+ secret bridge) BEFORE browser-history so the gate's curl-poll waits out the blocklist reload — keep that order.
- **`-config` suffix is intentional** — `services.audio-config.enable` avoids colliding with upstream `services.pipewire` etc.
- **ActivityWatch utilization watcher bloat + OOM (2026-08-18)** — `aw-watcher-utilization` wrote ~7.5KB full-system dumps (32 per-core CPU %, cumulative counters, loadavg, disk/net/sensors) every 5s: bucket grew to 1.71M events / 12.1GB (99.98% of sqlite.db) over ~8 months. Any query touching that bucket made aw-server scan/load gigabytes → 3.2GB RSS → repeated GLOBAL OOM kills (aw-server restart counter 9 in one evening; gnome-keyring also killed). Fixed: `poll_time = 300` in `platforms/common/programs/activitywatch.nix` (~1.6MB/day), history decimated to hourly samples (3,038 events, DB 13GB → 28MB), offline queue (246MB) drained + decimated. Raw pre-surgery backup: `~/backups/activitywatch-sqlite-preDecimation-13GB.db` (delete after settling). Data lives on `/mnt/pool/services/activitywatch` via `activitywatch-data-to-pool.nix` (self-neutralizing migration unit, started by deploy.sh, btrbk-pool snapshotted). **Gotcha:** aw-core 0.5.x reads ONLY the `.toml` (the sibling `.ini` is legacy, ignored); the toml is a read-only nix store symlink — runtime-only changes require replacing it with a real file (HM `backupFileExtension = "backup"` makes that collision-safe on next deploy)
- **systemd-graph (icholy/systemd-graph)** — Live D-Bus-driven systemd dependency graph at `graph.home.lan`. Go backend + React SPA (Cytoscape.js). DynamicUser, `MemoryMax=128M`, no persistent state. Port 8847 in `lib/ports.nix`. LAN-only (no auth, plain `reverse_proxy`). `pkgs/systemd-graph/` has separate `webui.nix` derivation (pnpm + Vite) injected into Go source via `runCommand` re-pack (`//go:embed dist`). Docs: `docs/services/systemd-graph.md`
- **systemd-timer-monitor (cappy-dev/systemd-timer-monitor)** — Read-only Python audit of systemd services+timers at `timers.home.lan`. Timer-driven oneshot (every 5 min), root user, writes `report.html` + `status.json` to `StateDirectory` served by Caddy `file_server`. LAN-only (no auth). **`python3` MUST be in `runtimeInputs`** — the `systemd-audit` script uses `#!/usr/bin/env python3`; under `harden {}` the service PATH doesn't include it by default (exit 127: `env: 'python3': No such file or directory`). **Exit code 1 = "issues found"** — the audit script returns non-zero when it detects failed services; `|| true` in the wrapper prevents systemd from marking the oneshot as failed. `deploy.sh` explicitly starts the service after activation (timer's `OnBootSec=2min` is boot-based, not deploy-based). Docs: `docs/services/systemd-timer-monitor.md`

### WiFi Failover (eno1 → wlan0 standby, enabled 2026-09-06)

**Module:** `modules/nixos/services/wifi-failover.nix` (`services.wifi-failover`, enabled in configuration.nix)

- **THE KERNEL DOES NOT REMOVE ROUTES ON CARRIER LOSS — only on admin down** (2026-09-06 live incident: cable pulled, Kittyspot WiFi connected on wlan0 with a metric-100 default route, yet ZERO internet — the static metric-0 `default via 192.168.1.1 dev eno1` stayed in the table and blackholed ALL traffic). The daemon (`wifi-failover-watch`, poll 1s) reads `/sys/class/net/<if>/carrier` (read error = no carrier), deletes the pinned eno1 defaults (v4+v6, never the fallback route) on carrier loss — the NM fallback route takes over instantly — and re-adds `default via ${networking.local.gateway} dev eno1` (metric 0 beats the fallback's 100 again) on carrier return. `startLimitBurst 5/300` + `onFailure` + system-health `monitoredServices`.
- **CARRIER-based, never ISP-probe-based** — dual-wan's route-health-monitor failover'd on 2×2s probe blips to 1.1.1.1 and was disabled for it; its ECMP would ALSO split packets over the metered phone hotspot. An eval-time assertion rejects enabling `dual-wan` and `wifi-failover` together (`config.services.dual-wan.enable or false` — the isolated-VM module eval lacks the dual-wan option tree). eno1 stays deliberately NM-unmanaged/static (see networking.nix).
- **`trustFallbackInterface` (default true)** adds wlan0 to `networking.firewall.trustedInterfaces` so LAN-equivalent service access survives failover (hotspot = user's own LAN, same trust model).
- **`ip route show` ANNOTATES routes of a carrier-down link with the `linkdown` keyword, and `ip route del` REJECTS it as garbage** ("either 'to' is duplicate, or 'linkdown' is garbage") — parsing a `route show` line and feeding it verbatim back to `del` silently fails exactly when the link is down (the first VM-test run caught the eviction doing nothing). The daemon strips `linkdown` tokens before deleting (`del_route_line`). DEBUG_POLL=1 env knob emits per-poll carrier/last-state journal lines for diagnosis.
- **VM test `tests/test-wifi-failover.nix` simulates cable pull via veth PEER admin-down** — peer down gives ethfail NO-CARRIER while staying admin-up with the route INTACT, the exact kernel state the bug rides (admin-down of the interface itself would let the kernel remove the route and test nothing). Asserts: missing-interface boot tolerance, daemon OBSERVED the up-transition (journal `carrier UP`) before the down test, pinned-route eviction with standby (metric 100) serving, restore to metric-0 primary, FAILOVER/RESTORE journal markers.

### Hardware Instability (post-crash, evo-x2)

- **The 2026-08-22 kernel freezes #1 (00:27) + #2 (05:49) + memory-emergency-guard (module `modules/nixos/services/memory-emergency-guard.nix` — NOT under platforms/nixos/system/ despite the instability topic; sev1-escalation + system-health + workload-admission live in the SAME dir; snapshots/btrfs-health/btrfs-rescue are the platforms/nixos/system/ ones)** — Freeze #1: boot f6448713 froze SOLID at 00:27:09 (journal stops mid-activity, no panic, no shutdown). Chain: at 22:25 zram swap hit 100% (Free swap = 0 kB of 29.5 GB) while flm's model was fully resident — **shmem pages (the mmap'd model) can ONLY evict via swap, so full zram makes the 21.6 GB model permanently UNEVICTABLE** (`unevictable:24967388kB` in the OOM dump). Kernel global OOM cascaded (flm killed 4× mid-cold-load — each retry re-read 21.6 GB — plus ollama 2×, flm start-limit-hit), then between 23:00-00:27 nightly btrbk sends + nix-gc deletes (27+ min) + zram-refault CPU burn (ClickHouse "100% busy for 600s" = decompression storm) ground the kernel to death. Gatus HAD alerted "Memory pressure CRITICAL" to Discord at 23:44 — 43 min of warning, no action.
  **Freeze #2 (05:49:56, boot 4d307ab5) happened WITH the guard deployed and tripping — three design gaps, all fixed 2026-08-22 morning:** (1) **THE FEEDBACK LOOP: the guard stopped only `fastflowlm.service`, leaving `fastflowlm.socket` accepting.** Every trip generated gatus alerts → PapDashboard ingest → the LLM insight enricher → a flm connection → a fresh 21.6 GB cold load into a zram-full machine → re-trip ~10 min later (flm restart-backoff cadence). The guard tripped 7× (03:32–04:53, MemAvail 5.4–9.7%, zram stuck 98.6%, Free swap = 64 kB); the kernel OOM-killed flm-real (22.2 GiB shmem) + ollama at 04:58, alert resolved 05:26, flm re-activated (an enricher insight logged at 05:34 proves it served), pressure rebuilt, froze at 05:49:56. The guard now stops the SOCKET on trip (clients get instant ECONNREFUSED — the enricher degrades gracefully instead of cold-loading) and auto-restores it once MemAvailable ≥15% + PSI some avg10 <5% + PSI avg60 <10% + episode bucket <4 + cooldown elapsed — **zram fill is deliberately NOT a restore gate (2026-09-02 live lockout): stopping the sacrifice CANNOT drain zram (its 28 GiB of swapped pages belong to OTHER processes and only fault back lazily), so the old zram<92% requirement kept the socket down for HOURS on a machine with 53% MemAvailable while the sev1 overlay re-armed on the stale trip. Relapse protection is the trip zones themselves (every zram zone combines zram with pressure), not the restore gate.** (2) **THE PSI BLIND SPOT: the final freeze was refault-thrash — PSI some avg10 >50% (gatus fired 05:49:39) while MemAvailable stayed ≥10%** (51 GiB page cache gave reclaim headroom), so NO trip zone fired at the 05:48:58 guard run; the kernel died of zram decompression CPU burn 17 s later, 2 s before the next tick. Zone 3 now trips on PSI some avg10 ≥40% AND zram ≥80% regardless of MemAvailable; interval is 30 s. (3) The guard itself was dead-on-arrival 01:27–01:52 (`mv ...prom.tmp: Operation not permitted` every minute — the sticky-bit 1777 textfile dir + `harden {}`'s empty CapabilityBoundingSet blocks rename-over-foreign-owned-file EVEN FOR ROOT; sticky-bit semantics require CAP_FOWNER. Fixed by `CapabilityBoundingSet = "CAP_FOWNER CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE"`, commit 9a14a8e1). Contributing load that night: ~a dozen concurrent crush agent sessions (52 crush + 50 bun procs), 10 nix builds, 72 compile + 22 golangci-lint procs, 12 qemu VMs (NixOS VM tests incl. 8 cross-arch aarch64), 78 helium procs — against flm's 22 GiB unevictable + zram full. flm also crashed twice on its own (01:39 core-dump, 02:37 signal) — the v1.0.2 heap bug recurring under pressure. General rules: **zram-full is the real cliff on this box — nothing reclaimable remains once anon+shmem fill it; act at ≥92%. A socket-activated sacrifice is NOT sacrificed until its SOCKET is down — alert-driven consumers (the PapDashboard enricher) re-wake it and re-pay the 21.6 GB cold load. MemAvailable can look healthy (>10%) while the kernel dies of PSI refault thrash — trip on PSI, not just free memory. Don't run full `nix flake check` (builds VM tests) or parallel agent-session storms while flm is resident and zram is filling**
- **The 2026-08-31 16:34 freeze #3 — the episodic-stall class (zram/MemAvailable/OOM/averages ALL blind)** — the box froze with zram EMPTY (~0%), MemAvailable healthy, and ZERO oomd kills: every guard zone was blind. Driver: memory-PSI stall from FOUR stacked full-disk readers on the QLC NVMe — the btrbk-data 9-day-outage FULL re-send, Persistent boot catch-up of weekly autoScrub on `/` AND `/data`, and flm cold-loading 21.6 GB ×4 (v1.0.2 prefill core-dump every attempt; loads stretched 2-5 min → 27-43 min under contention). By 16:33 even 64M oneshots (guard, PSI collector) stopped completing cycles; journal cut mid-entry 16:34:42; hard reset 16:36. **CALIBRATION (SigNoz, boot -1): `node_psi_memory_some_avg60` NEVER exceeded 3.93%** — the pressure was EPISODIC avg10 spikes (>50%, gatus CRITICAL flapping ~2 h) with recovery gaps; the terminal collapse was minutes-fast and unobserved. NO time-average can see this class. Fixes (2026-08-31): guard **Zone 5** = episodic leaky bucket (+1 per 30s run with avg10 ≥40, −1 per clean run, trip at 8; VM-tested accumulation AND decay) — the zone actually calibrated against the incident; Zone 4 (avg60 ≥50, slow-burn variant) retained with a calibration WARNING; sev1 "MEMORY STALL SUSTAINED" pages the desktop at avg60 ≥45 OR episode bucket ≥4; scrub ExecStart became a deferral guard comparing `systemctl show -p ActiveState` against `active|activating` — `is-active --quiet` returns NON-ZERO for a Type=oneshot mid-send ("activating"), the naive check would have missed exactly the streaming case (self-review catch); flm bumped to v1.0.3. General rules: a full-disk read at idle BFQ priority still contributes full BYTES to NAND saturation — serialize full-device readers; never judge freeze risk by MemAvailable/zram/averages alone — the EPISODE PATTERN of avg10 is the pre-freeze signal; and CALIBRATE trip thresholds against the actual incident telemetry before trusting a zone (the first Zone 4 draft was phantom protection for the very freeze that motivated it). Forensics: `docs/status/archived/2026-08-31_16-50_crash-forensics-freeze-incident-3.md`
- **Freeze #4 (2026-09-14 13:32, hard reset; kernel 7.2.5) — IO-PSI livelock again, now with the Zone 6 era live** — the first-real-reboot boot (10:34) stacked: the 20260911 llama.cpp mid-load spin (2× ~94% single-thread), project-discovery-daemon sustained ~7 MB/s scans, 5+ concurrent crush sessions churning QLC-root `.crush/` DBs, plus a stray `find /home/lars/projects` owner-check — io PSI some avg60 pinned 40-60% for HOURS with memory pristine; guard Zone 6 tripped 14+ times (each trip stopping flm mid-cold-load, restore churn burning the daily budget), the 13:32 deploy entered during a PSI dip and the box died at 13:32:16 mid-activation (journal cut, no panic, NO vmcore — the livelock class produces no kdump capture; wtmp + journal cut are the discriminators). Forensics: `docs/status/archived/2026-09-14_13-54_freeze-4-diagnosis-llama-rearm-io-storm.md`. Rules: `systemctl stop` is NEVER containment on this host (stc re-arms stopped units — mask/disable or config-disable); Route future repo-tree scans through `heavy-job`; the crush-DBs-off-QLC migration (TODO P1) is the structural fix.
- **Freeze #5 (2026-09-18 15:27, hard reset; kernel 7.2.6) — all-day QLC IO storm + llama-rag spin on the PINNED build, death 9 s into a deploy's activation** — btrbk-root/data were guard-killed mid-send the night before (23:04/23:30), and Zone-6 IO PSI ran continuously from midnight (avg60 62-71%, QLC random ~29KB reads, disk busy up to 100%; trips #443-465, flm restore-capped by 10:34) — drivers: the crush-session-DBs-on-QLC class + project-discovery du-walks. gen-784 (11:28) re-enabled llama-rag on the pin-back's direct-run verification → both servers spun ~94% CPU ×2 on the PINNED 0.3.0 build until death (2h33min CPU over 2h43min wall, 8.6G written per lifecycle each). FOUR deploys landed into the storm (10:22, 11:28, 14:09, 15:27); the last one's switch began 15:27:40 and the journal cut 9 s later (no panic, no vmcore, no shutdown record — the livelock discriminators). Post-freeze boot replayed it (llama spin from 15:34) — contained live: SIGSTOP both spinners, `llama-rag.enable = false` (escape condition), crush-hot-db FIRST migration left to finish (the base-storm structural fix; moved the multi-GB session DBs to the Samsung from 15:41). No reboot-revert: current-system == profile == system-784; the 15:27 deploy (`d6wdfygv`) never activated — its owner must re-run. Forensics: `docs/status/archived/2026-09-18_15-50_freeze-5-llama-spin-io-storm-mid-deploy.md`. Rules: (1) a shell direct-run green is NOT re-enable evidence for a unit-context bug — soak under the REAL units first; (2) the guard's sacrifice list cannot stop an IO storm whose drivers aren't flm/churn units — the crush-DB migration is the actual fix; (3) the deploy pressure gate checks avg10 at entry only — a switch phase entering during a PSI dip still dies; when Zone 6 tripped within the last hour, queue the deploy instead of racing dips.
- **Freeze #6 (2026-09-18 19:28:11, hard reset; boot 15:32→19:28) — the crash-RECOVERY window is itself an IO storm: crush-hot-db FIRST migration (23.2G QLC read in its first 60-min run) + discordsync-db-heal 11G integrity check at every boot (timed out at 10 min, restarted next boot) + cold page cache + ~27 concurrent crush sessions** — 16 Zone-6 trips (#466→#481) sacrificed flm+btrbk churn and changed nothing; the guard's own log at death: avg60=42.95%, disk busy 97.5%, MemAvail 61.4% (the crash-3 class). The actual readers (`crush-hot-db-migrate`, `discordsync-db-heal`, ad-hoc compsize) sit OUTSIDE `ioChurnUnits` — Zone 6 is structurally blind to the recovery-reader class. Post-crash boot 0 kept storming (avg60 up to 79%, trip #482) with the migration ~2/3 done (resumes across reboots; converges) and db-heal re-scanning — a crash-loop AMPLIFIER: every crash re-runs the heal into a cold cache. Deploy state clean (system-785 anchored). Rules: (a) after a freeze, STOP the resumable readers at the next boot (`systemctl stop crush-hot-db-migrate discordsync-db-heal` — both next-run-converges) and let the box settle instead of crash-looping through recovery; (b) extend `ioChurnUnits` with those two units; (c) BFQ ionice never reduces total bytes — a 60-min 23G read at BE/6 saturates QLC exactly like BE/4; serialize recovery readers (heavy-job) on a busy box; (d) cap concurrent agent sessions during guard-active windows. Forensics: `docs/status/2026-09-18_19-45_freeze-6-io-livelock-crash-recovery-readers-crush-migration-db-heal.md`
- **NIC falls off the PCIe bus after a hard crash — warm reboot does NOT retrain it (2026-08-22)** — After the freeze + power-button reset, the next boot had NO wired networking at all: PCI enumeration showed the RTL8125 (`10ec:8125`, normally `c1:00.0`) completely ABSENT, its root port `00:02.1` gone, and the SDHCI reader (`17a0:9755`, normally `c2:00.0`) shifted into its slot with every later bus renumbered. r8125 loaded but had nothing to probe; `networking.interfaces.eno1` (static 192.168.1.150) never ran → no IP, SSH dead. The SECOND (clean) reboot brought the NIC back. Detection: `system-health` emits `system_lan_nic_present` (fail-closed) + the `lan-nic-watchdog` unit (every 10 min, first at 90s) fails loudly with power-cycle instructions; Gatus "LAN NIC Present" alerts. Remediation: power-cycle (shut down, ~10 s, power on) — do not trust a warm reset after any hard freeze
- **DAS USB link (8-1) drops entirely, live, with data loss (2026-08-22)** — 11 min into the recovery boot `usb 8-1: USB disconnect` fired with no reconnect: ALL external disks (pool Toshiba #1, both SanDisks, buildcache SSD) went offline simultaneously mid-write (ext4 "lost async page write" + journal abort on sda1). The second pool Toshiba never enumerated at boot (smartd died on it: `open() failed: No such device`), so `/mnt/pool` never mounted (fstab waits on the missing by-id member; BTRFS RAID1 also refuses one-member mounts without `degraded`). Pool-dependent SERVICE units (atticd, immich, paperless, bank-sync, btrfs-verify-pool-backups) fail as designed; `atticd-bootstrap` (fixed 2026-08-24) instead SKIPS cleanly via `ConditionPathIsDirectory = <storagePath>` (the dir exists only while the pool is mounted) — before that it hard-failed with `connection refused` at every activation and blocked every `nh os switch`/deploy with exit 4 during DAS outages. deploy.sh restarts it post-switch (converges after DAS recovery); with the pool mounted but atticd wedged it still exits 1 loudly via its readiness probe. Test-trap: `systemctl show -p Result` retains `success` from a pre-restart run — assert on `ActiveState=inactive` + `ConditionResult=no` + the journal `unmet condition` line instead (regression-tested in `tests/test-attic.nix` step 9). Same class as the NIC vanish: post-crash peripheral/power instability. Fix is PHYSICAL: reseat DAS USB cable + enclosure power, then reboot. If the pool must come up with one member: `mount -o degraded` is a USER decision, never automatable here. **Diagnostics runbook: `bash scripts/das-link-recovery-check.sh`** (read-only; USB tree, by-id presence + fstab-drift guard, zombie mounts, ext4 damage across current AND previous boot, shadow triage, NVMe fallback-cache regrowth, decision tree; run once with sudo for root-only shadow dirs). **Still down 2026-08-26 after 4 days + user reseats — isolation evidence:** the JMicron JMS567 bridge (`152d:0567`, "External USB 3.0", serial `20170331000C3`) disconnected Aug 22 00:59:15 and NEVER re-announced across 3 boots; reseats produced ZERO connect events on any port while hotplug provably works elsewhere (Canon printer enumerated instantly on usb1) → kernel/udev/systemd all healthy (that part held — the "failure is electrical" verdict did NOT; retracted 2026-08-29, see ROOT CAUSE below). Two remaining fault domains: (A) c7:00.4 (usb7/usb8) wake-from-D3cold broken — controller is runtime-suspended (`power_state=D3cold`, `power/control=auto`) and a replug generates no PME wake; (B) the enclosure/bridge/PSU itself dead. Isolation order (each verified via `journalctl -kf` + script rerun): (1) plug a known-good USB stick into the SAME machine port — enumerates ⇒ port fine ⇒ domain B; (2) force-wake bypass: `sudo sh -c 'echo on > /sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:c7:00.4/power/control; echo on > /sys/bus/usb/devices/usb7/power/control; echo on > /sys/bus/usb/devices/usb8/power/control'` then replug the DAS — enumerates ⇒ D3cold-wake bug ⇒ permanent fix = udev rule pinning `power/control=on` on c7:00.4; (3) full AC drain (shutdown, unplug ≥60 s, hold power button) — the only reboot that resets controller silicon (warm reboots never re-enumerate, same class as the NIC vanish); (4) enclosure side: unpower ≥60 s (hung JMS567 bridges need cap discharge — a quick flip does NOT reset them), listen for Toshiba spin-up, check PSU. Red herring: `usb 1-1` churn at 05:58/06:11 was the Canon MG2500 printer, not the DAS. Diagnosis trap: `journalctl -k --since <date>` silently shows ONLY the current boot — use `journalctl -g 'pattern'` (no `-k`) for cross-boot "last seen" forensics. **Topology CONFIRMED + software exonerated (2026-08-27 git/sysfs audit):** the DAS is a port-multiplier enclosure — ONE JMS567 bridge presents 4 SCSI targets `External USB3.0 DISK00/01/02/04` (bay 03 empty): sdb+sdd = pool BTRFS (label `pool` devid 1+2), sda = buildcache ext4, sdc = spare SanDisk. It disconnected ABRUPTLY at 00:59:15 with ZERO preceding kernel errors (no xhci reset, no uas failure) — read at the time as "instant bridge/PSU death, not a port failure"; that reading is RETRACTED (2026-08-29, see ROOT CAUSE below). Software cause ruled out: nixpkgs pin `26.11.20260816.e5bdc4a` + kernel 7.1.8 IDENTICAL across the worked→dead window (generations 716→726), no commits touch USB/udev-power semantics, sysfs authorization fully open on all buses, `usbcore.autosuspend=2` stock. The 01:50:50 PCI remove/rescan of c7:00.4 (buses 7/8 re-registered) was a post-mortem intervention during the freeze-night firefight — the bridge was already silent (wedged, never proven dead). Degradation history: Aug 16 enclosure storm (11 re-enumerations in 3.5 h), Aug 18 boot attached only 3 of 4 targets. If a replug on another port stays silent AND a known-good device enumerates on that port, the one untested component is the USB CABLE (a dead cable produces exactly this signature: zero events anywhere) — swap it before declaring the enclosure dead. **ROOT CAUSE FOUND (2026-08-29) — the "software exonerated" audit was WRONG:** the 2026-08-12 commit `d57c1210` added `ACTION=="add|change", KERNEL=="sd[ab]" → hdparm -S 120 -B 127` (spindown for a retired ZFS pool, matched by FRAGILE KERNEL LETTERS). When the DAS disks inherited sda/sdb on the 00:48:05 boot, the rule fired APM/spindown commands THROUGH the flaky JMS567 — 11 min later (00:59:15) the bridge wedged mid-handshake and dropped off the bus. It never recovered because (1) the rule stayed live until 2026-08-27 (re-abusing any successful reattach), and (2) **the JMS567 runs off USB VBUS from the host — pulling only the enclosure power NEVER power-cycles the bridge**; every "60s unpower" with the cable attached was a fake power cycle (the blue LEDs stayed on through enclosure-off, fed from the host). Fixes deployed 2026-08-29: rule class is now eval-time BLOCKED (`udev-block-letter-audit.nix` assertion on `services.udev.extraRules` — any `KERNEL=="sd[...]"` line carrying `RUN+=` throws; attribute sets on whole-class matches like the bfq scheduler rule stay allowed); xHCI+USB4 controllers pinned awake via udev (`ATTR{class}=="0x0c0330|0x0c0340"` → `power/control=on`, closes the D3cold-swallowed-hotplug domain); `uas`+`usb-storage` pre-loaded in `boot.kernelModules` (this exact bridge VANISHES when uas is unavailable at attach — private-cloud 2025-11-24 `ZFS_RECOVERY_UAS_MYSTERY.md`: quirks=152d:0567:u causes TOTAL enumeration failure, uas-missing-at-boot causes boot-time vanish; NEVER apply that quirk here). Every boot after 2026-08-22 ran with uas UNLOADED — no device ever attached to autoload it (verified 2026-08-29 pre-deploy via lsmod) — so every reseat attempt also failed the enclosure's documented uas-at-attach precondition; the user's "you're just missing uas" read this recovery blocker correctly. **Front USB4-C ports: UNTESTED — do NOT call them dead.** This bullet originally claimed "dead-for-data under this kernel" — the SAME circular trap as the "software exonerated" audit: the only device ever probed on them was the wedged bridge, and an EMPTY `/sys/class/typec` is NORMAL on hardware-managed Type-C (no typec class driver ≠ dead port); the user reports all front ports work. **2026-08-30: front USB-C mass-storage PROVEN** — a Samsung 970 EVO Plus 1TB NVMe behind a Realtek RTL9210B-CG bridge (`0bda:9210`) enumerated on usb 6-1 (xHCI `c7:00.3`) at SuperSpeed Plus Gen 2x1 (10 Gbps, uas). Note: the RTL9210B does NOT pass TRIM/discard over UAS (`lsblk -D` = 0B) — `blkdiscard` cannot work through this bridge. Never write a port off after testing it only with a broken device. Recovery procedure (runbook decision tree): pull the USB cable (VBUS!) AND enclosure power, 60+s, replug — FRONT USB4-C is the user-preferred port, rear Type-A only as a fallback diagnostic; controllers pinned awake. Whether the bridge recovers at all (wedged vs dead) is decided by that replug — RECORD THE OUTCOME here. Cross-boot forensics trap that cost hours: `journalctl -k --since` shows only the CURRENT boot — use `journalctl -g` without `-k`. **OUTCOME RECORDED (2026-08-31): the bridge RECOVERED** — after the 2026-08-29 fixes (udev letter-rule block, xHCI pinned awake, uas preloaded) + user replug, the 14:30 boot enumerated ALL FOUR targets again; `/mnt/pool` mounted by-label with BOTH RAID1 members (zero device errors), buildcache + spare SanDisk attached. 9-day outage: pool backups stalled Aug 21→31 (backup-coordination ages ~250h, all red until the nightly timers catch up); watch for recurrence of the wedge class. **Runtime replug self-heal (2026-08-31, `services.pool-recovery`):** the pool is no longer the manual-recovery laggard — udev rules keyed to BOTH Toshiba `ID_SERIAL`s (never sd-letters) start `pool-usb-recovery.service` (also run by deploy.sh post-switch): grace-settle for members → `btrfs device scan` → health gate = real-IO probe (`timeout ls -A`) + mounted-fs UUID vs member UUID (findmnt's MAJ:MIN is the btrfs ANONYMOUS superblock devt `0:x`, NEVER comparable to member devts — the naive devt match would reap EVERY healthy mount; caught by the VM test) → remount via fstab **never `-o degraded`** (partial re-enumeration fails LOUDLY; degraded stays a user decision) → is-failed-gated restarts of pool consumers → `pool_usb_recovery_*` Prometheus metrics + Gatus "Pool RAID1 Membership" (fires on exactly-ONE-member present). Zero members at boot exits CLEANLY (DAS-link check owns whole-link-down alerting). VM-tested in `tests/test-pool-recovery.nix` (healthy no-op, foreign-mount UUID reaping, partial→loud-fail, absent→clean-exit, fail-closed metrics). **Consumer convergence now covers the INACTIVE class (2026-09-14 boot-dropout incident):** the 13:35 boot's `mnt-pool.mount` start job was CANCELLED (mount helper SIGTERM'd, "Failed with result 'signal'", same second — job superseded during coldplug churn); pool-usb-recovery remounted the pool at 13:36:56 (worked as designed), but bank-sync/immich-server/paperless-web — all enabled, Requires-ing the mount — were never started by anyone for the WHOLE boot: the mount job's failure left them INACTIVE (not failed), so the is-failed-only consumer gate matched nothing, no "Dependency failed" lines ever hit the journal, zero pending jobs remained, and Gatus paged Bank-Sync down from 13:42 while the pre-deploy §10 gate hard-blocked every deploy on the absent `bank_sync_*` metrics. The healthy-mount path `exit 0`ing BEFORE the consumer step meant even deploy.sh's post-switch recovery run converged nothing. Fixes: (1) `converge_consumers()` in `pool-recovery.nix` now runs in BOTH paths (healthy + recovered) and starts enabled-but-inactive consumers (only explicit `disabled`/`masked` states respected — every restartUnits entry is an always-on daemon, Gatus owns stop-alerting; systemd merges duplicate start jobs, so racing the boot transaction is safe); (2) §10 gained the `BANKSYNC_UP`/`BANKSYNC_METRICS` endpoint-down WARN branch (see the pre-deploy §10 bullet); (3) deploy.sh restarts a FAILED `discordsync-db-heal` failed-gated + `--no-block` (indirect unit the provisioner loop skips; its 10-min budget died in this boot's IO storm — multi-user.target took 15 MINUTES to reach). Diagnosis traps: `InactiveExitTimestampMonotonic=0` from busctl on an inactive unit is NOT evidence it never started — querying re-instantiates a GC'd unit object with zeroed properties (use the journal). VM-fixture limits: the guest `/etc/systemd/system` is a READ-ONLY store tree, so neither `systemctl disable` nor any mask (persistent OR runtime) can establish a disabled/masked state in a VM test — the disabled-guard skip branch is code-reviewed only; the VM test covers convergence via the enabled-inactive fixture plus a listed-but-nonexistent unit (start fails non-fatal, list continues).
- **forgejo-oidc-setup needed the full OIDC gate, not just DNS (fixed 2026-08-22)** — on the crash-recovery boot it failed 39 s in with `dial tcp 192.168.1.150:443: connection refused`: DNS resolved (dnsblockd up) but Caddy hadn't bound :443 yet. Its old `mkDnsGate` only proves name resolution. Now also carries `mkOidcGate` (curl-polls the discovery endpoint up to 120 s), matching gatus/oauth2-proxy/browser-history. Symptom of the missing gate on any boot: `forgejo-oidc-setup` failed + Forgejo logins fall back to password until next deploy re-runs it
- **Stability defense-in-depth stack (2026-08-22 evening plan, 5 user decisions)** — layered AFTER the guard, because the guard is a last-resort sacrifice, not a prevention layer. Plan: `docs/planning/2026-08-22_07-01_STABILITY-DEFENSE-IN-DEPTH-PARETO-PLAN.html`. Components:
  - **The freezes were scheduler LIVELOCKS, not lockups**: `softlockup_panic=1`, `hung_task_panic=1` and the 30 s hardware WDT were ALL armed and NONE fired — a livelock (CPUs burning in zram refault with IRQs enabled) pets the WDT "eventually", never trips the soft-lockup detector, and starves khungtaskd. Nothing in-kernel catches that class; prevention must act BEFORE the cliff
  - **`workload-admission.nix`** — `heavy-job` wrapper: slot-counting flock queue (held-fd pattern, `exec 9>lock; flock -n 9; exec "$@"`), default 3 concurrent heavy jobs (`MAX_CONCURRENT`, user decision), waits never fails. Wrap NixOS VM tests + long builds in it. Build MEMORY is bounded separately: **`nix-daemon.serviceConfig.MemoryHigh = "32G"`** (networking.nix) — ALL daemon builds run in that one cgroup regardless of invoking user; MemoryHigh throttles (reclaim/swap), never kills. Crush sessions are monitor-only: `system_crush_sessions{,_over_threshold}` metrics + Gatus alert >6
