  - **`sev1-escalation.nix`** — the human-loop layer (Discord fired 43 min pre-freeze with nobody watching): `sev1-bridge` (root, 10 s) evaluates guard-trip / guard-DEAD / monitoring-stale / DAS-link / LAN-NIC / btrfs-critical / zram-critical from the local textfile proms → rewrites `/run/systemnix/sev1/alert` (title/detail/generated-at epoch/**severity line 4**) + ONE deduped DMS notification via `systemd-run --machine=lars@.host --user notify-send` (the machined bus-proxy pattern; best-effort, no session = no-op). **Severity TIERS (2026-08-31 movie-night user decision: "stale system_health monitoring is still not something you need to spam my entire screen with"; extended 2026-09-02 after the MEMORY EMERGENCY all-day overlay spam; HARD RULE 2026-09-02 late evening — "High memory should NOT flash my entire screen while I watch a movie, only shutdowns and ACTUALLY-impacted-soon events may page"; refined same night — "if it's NOT gonna kill it, do a yellow non-flashing one once"):** `page` (red pulsing fullscreen + persistent critical notification) is **RESERVED — no current emitter** (true drop-everything emergencies only); `warn` (**infra hardware criticals**: DAS link, LAN NIC, btrfs critical) = **static amber TOP-STRIP banner (non-fullscreen, ~12% screen height) shown ONCE per alert set** (the bridge writes `warn` on the first run of a NEW alert set and downgrades every same-set refresh to `warn-seen`, which the overlay ignores — the downgrade lives in the BRIDGE so "once" survives quickshell restarts and is VM-testable; a CHANGED alert set re-arms the banner) + one cooldown-gated notification. **NO memory condition may EVER overlay** — guard-trip, sustained memory stall, and guard-dead were ALL demoted to notify 2026-09-02: the guard exists to CONTAIN memory emergencies automatically (the user cannot act mid-movie), and the trip alert still tracks the EMERGENCY — alerting while the sacrifice is actually DOWN, clearing the moment the guard restores the sockets, NOT on the 30-min `last_trip_recent` metric window; `notify` (ALL memory conditions; SYSTEM MONITORING STALE; ZRAM SWAP CRITICAL — combined-gated zram ≥90% + degraded PSI/avail; FLM RESTORE CAPPED) = ONE self-expiring normal-urgency notification + Gatus/Discord, NO overlay, cooldown-gated 30 min so a flapping condition cannot re-notify either (the live steady state of 97% fill at 53% avail never notifies). The overlay's severity gate fails LOUD (missing line 4 = page). `sev1_bridge_page_alerts_active` distinguishes tiers at the metrics level. `sev1-overlay` (user quickshell unit, shutdown-overlay pattern) shows a fullscreen banner while the file is FRESH and page-tier — **the alert file SELF-EXPIRES (overlay TTL 120 s vs 10 s refresh)**, so a dead bridge can never stick an overlay. Module-presence gates (`systemctl is-enabled <timer>`): hosts without the guard/system-health modules must NOT page DEAD/STALE forever (regression-tested). PSI warning tier (`node_psi_memory_warning`, some avg60 ≥20%) is Discord-only by decision — NO overlay, NO automated action. **Smarter-alerting additions (2026-09-02):** notify cooldown is PER-KEY (one epoch file per alert-set key under the state dir, pruned >2× cooldown — the old single epoch let a ZRAM notify cross-suppress an unrelated STALE notify); `sev1_bridge_page_active_seconds`/`sev1_bridge_page_last_duration_seconds` measure page duration (alert-fatigue visibility — the incident's core complaint was duration and nothing measured it); trip alerts append TRIP CHURN context when `memory_emergency_guard_trips_last_hour ≥ 2`; **FLM RESTORE CAPPED** (notify tier) fires when the guard's daily restore budget is spent and the socket is still down — without it flm would silently stay unusable after the trip alert clears. **Guard-side:** `maxRestoresPerDay` (default 3, 0 = unlimited) caps restores/day because every restore re-arms the 21.6 GB cold load and the 2026-09-02 re-wake loop (trip → restore → enricher/PMA reconnect → re-trip within ~40 min) made unlimited self-healing an I/O churn engine; past the cap the socket stays DOWN (`restore_capped 1`) until a human runs `systemctl start fastflowlm.socket`. Per-zone trip counters (`memory_emergency_guard_zone1..5_trips_total`) + `restored_total` + `trips_last_hour` make forensics a prom read instead of a journal dig. **Zone 6 (IO-PSI, added 2026-09-14, crash3 §c6/§f.15):** trips on SUSTAINED io-pressure some avg60 ≥40% ONLY when real disk activity corroborates (max per-disk `/proc/diskstats` io_ticks delta vs the previous guard run ≥20% — the phantom filter: D-state on dead automounts saturates io PSI with IDLE disks; unknown first-run = corroborated, fail-safe). On ANY trip the guard additionally stops the resumable churn units (`ioChurnUnits`: btrbk-root/data/pool + btrfs-balance-{metadata,data} + the three btrfs-scrub units) and RE-ARMS them (systemctl start, best-effort) on the first run where sustained io avg60 drains under the trip threshold — without the re-arm a mid-receive stop pushed the nightly btrbk send a full +24h (timer already fired; a second storm in the 3-day freshness window FAILed the backup check, 2026-09-16 23:00 trip #299 class); each unit's own pre-start guards decide whether it is safe to run, a relapse re-trips within one 30 s tick, and interrupted btrbk receives are healed by btrbk-pool-clean. Metrics: `memory_emergency_guard_{io_psi_some_avg60_percent,io_disk_busy_percent_max,zone6_trips_total,churn_rearms_total}` + the 2026-09-15 additions: `churn_units_stopped{unit=…}`/`churn_stopped_timestamp_seconds` (WHICH units the guard actually stopped and WHEN — emitted only while the churn window is open, cleared on the re-arm run; the stopped-list persists in `stateDir/churn-stopped`) and `last_run_timestamp_seconds` (freshness stamp). Guard-death is detected by TWO layers (node_exporter serves a frozen textfile forever, so the guard check's presence pats CANNOT see a dead guard): sev1 `MEMORY GUARD DEAD` (desktop) + the "Memory Guard Collector Fresh" Gatus check (`system_memory_guard_metrics_fresh` composite from the .prom mtime, threshold = sev1 staleGuardSeconds 300). btrfs-health consumes the churn window to separate a guard-DEFERRED scrub from a silently WEDGED one (`btrfs_scrub_deferred_by_guard`; the old single "BTRFS Scrub Health" check was split into "BTRFS Scrub Errors" + "BTRFS Scrub Incomplete" — the old error_free composite red on every RUNNING scrub and on guard-deferred interruptions). Runbook (Zone 6 semantics, post-storm churn re-arm, calibration evidence from the first 99 real trips): `docs/services/memory-emergency-guard.md`
  - **kdump** (`boot.crashDump.enable`, verified in the pinned fork): any future panic now lands a vmcore in `/var/crash` — postmortems become a 10-min read. `kernel.panic` 30→10 s (kdump captures first). Retention BOUNDED by `kdump-retention.service` (keep 2 newest, 20 G cap — root fs is tight; boot + weekly timer)
  - **Deploy pressure gate** (deploy.sh, exit 12): blocks `nh os switch` when PSI some avg10 ≥20% or zram ≥90% or MemAvailable <10% (reads /proc directly, no curl); escape hatch `DEPLOY_FORCE_PRESSURE=1` — deploying the FIX under pressure is sometimes exactly right
  - **Census metrics** (system-health): `system_crush_sessions`, and top-8 cgroups by `memory.current` with anon/shmem/unevictable breakdown (`system_cgroup_mem_{,anon_,shmem_,unevictable_}bytes{cgroup=…}`) — "who holds the 30 GiB" is now a dashboard glance, not OOM-dump archaeology
  - VM regression test: `tests/test-sev1-escalation.nix` (9 scenarios incl. the module-absent gate + alert-file format/severity tiers + dedup + notify-tier cooldown flap suppression)

### Shell & DevTools

- **curl ≥8.2x advertises `Accept-Encoding` by default — body-parsing curls MUST pass `--compressed`** (2026-08-16 nixpkgs bump). Servers that honor it (crush-daily API middleware, node_exporter `/metrics`, anything behind Caddy `encode`) return `Content-Encoding: gzip`, which plain `curl -s` does NOT decode: greps match nothing, jq fails, and bash warns "ignored null byte in input" (gzip streams contain NULs). Status-code-only checks (`-o /dev/null -w %{http_code}`) are unaffected. Bit the smoke checks twice under different disguises: first DiscordSync `/api/stats` (misdiagnosed as "null bytes in JSON", "fixed" with `grep -a`), then Crush Daily `/api/reports` ("unexpected response" forever against a healthy API). `--compressed` is a no-op on identity responses — safe on every body-parsing curl
- **`rsync -a --files-from=<list>` does NOT recurse into listed directories — the `-a`-implied `-r` is silently DROPPED in files-from mode (2026-08-31)** — pass `--recursive` EXPLICITLY. Symptom: rsync exits 0, "succeeds", and has copied only empty dir skeletons + single files (four benchmark runs silently measured nothing before this was caught). Related trap the same evening: `du -sk --files0-from=-` needs NUL separators (`tr '\n' '\0'`), and `btrfs filesystem du` reports LOGICAL bytes — use `compsize` or a df-free-delta for physical/compressed usage.
- **python urllib AUTO-FOLLOWS redirects; curl does NOT (no `-L` by default)** — verifying an HTTP body check with python can LIE about curl semantics (2026-08-18): paperless `/` 302-redirects to `/accounts/login/?next=/`; python `urlopen` followed it and showed the login body, while the smoke's curl saw only the empty 302 body and failed. Always validate smoke-check patterns with the SAME tool the check uses (curl), or probe the FINAL URL directly. Same class: `test -e /etc/systemd/system/<unit>.service` is the correct enable-gate for smoke checks — `systemctl is-enabled` returns rc=1 for units pulled in via `requiredBy` only (paperless-web has no own [Install] symlink despite running), and `list-unit-files` matches unit files that exist but aren't deployed-enabled (bank-sync fired its vHost check while disabled). **EXCEPTION — fstab-generated `.mount` units NEVER exist at that path**: `fileSystems` entries render to `/etc/fstab` and systemd-fstab-generator creates their units AT RUNTIME in `/run/systemd/generator/` — a `[ -e /etc/systemd/system/var-lib-clickhouse.mount ]` gate is permanently false and its check block silently skips = phantom green (post-deploy-check XFS gate, caught 2026-08-22). Gate fstab mounts on the fstab entry itself (`awk '$2 == "/path"' /etc/fstab`) or `findmnt`; `systemctl` NAME-based actions still work on generated units. Related detector: `system_current_system_profiled` (system-health) alerts when `/run/current-system` matches no numbered profile — the manual-activation signature (deploy.sh post-switch steps + boot entries missing; reboot reverts)
- **`writeShellApplication` pipefail + `|| echo 0`** — Produces multi-line output under pipefail. Use `|| true` + `''${var:-0}`.
- **`writeShellApplication` pipefail + `| sort | head` SIGPIPE** — Append `|| true` to pipeline.
- **`set -o pipefail` + `echo "$body" | grep -q` on a >64KiB body = FALSE "body lacks" FAIL (2026-08-19)** — grep -q exits at its first match, the echo writer then dies on SIGPIPE (exit 141) while still writing the rest of the body into the 64KiB-capped pipe, and pipefail fails the whole pipeline even though grep SUCCEEDED. Hit when the bank-sync templ-components dashboard grew to ~106KiB: the smoke check claimed the body lacked a string that provably sat at byte ~100. Probe bodies via `grep -q "pat" <<<"$body"` (herestring, no pipe) or the `check()` helper's `curl -o /tmp/.smoke-body` + file grep — never an echo|grep -q pipeline on a large captured body. Diagnosis trick: dump the captured var with printf in the failure branch and grep THAT — if it matches, the pipeline is the bug, not the server

- **`comm` MUST share the collation of the sorts feeding it (2026-09-17 smoke baseline diff)** — the fail-set baseline compared `LC_ALL=C sort`ed files with a comm running under the ambient `en_US.UTF-8` locale: byte-order vs UTF-8 collation diverge on the multibyte fail names (em dashes), comm prints all three "not in sorted order" warnings (masked by `|| true`), and a genuinely NEW fail line can be silently mis-merged out of the regression signal. Fix: run comm itself under `LC_ALL=C` whenever either input was sorted under C. Same class as the head-SIGPIPE lesson: every byte-order tool in a chain must agree on collation
- **`find -L` for Nix store symlinks** — `find` does NOT follow starting-point symlinks by default.
- **Smart direnv library** — `~/.config/direnv/lib/zz-smart-nix.sh` overrides `_nix_add_gcroot` (5.1x speedup) + `use_go_env` (auto-detects GOEXPERIMENT/GOPRIVATE).
- **Fish per-prompt direnv caching** — mtime-gated; 0.7ms cache hit vs 43ms. Per-session sentinel includes `$fish_pid`.
- **`git insteadOf` flake.lock SSH pollution** — Global rule rewrites `https://github.com/` → `git@github.com:`. Workaround: `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null nix flake update <input>`.
- **statix `repeated_keys` disabled** — False positive for NixOS modules. `statix.toml` (non-dotted) has `disabled = ["repeated_keys"]`.
- **Pre-commit statix lints STAGED `.nix` files only** — Unstaged/pre-existing debt does NOT block commits (pathspec-scoped commits stay clean), so `statix check .` repo-wide (always exit 0 in practice) is NOT a gate predictor. Run `nix fmt` or stage the files to lint them.
- **zellij copy model: OSC52 is NATIVE; `copy_command` REPLACES it (2026-08-16)** — zellij always copies via OSC52 escape sequences, which tunnel through SSH to the client terminal's clipboard (works in iTerm2 and Ghostty). `copy_command = "wl-copy"` kills SSH copying dead (no Wayland session inside SSH) — that was the original iTerm2 bug. `copy_clipboard` selects the DESTINATION (`"system"`/`"primary"`), NOT the mechanism — `"osc52"` is an INVALID value that hard-fails config parse (deployed config verified via `zellij setup --check`). Current state: Linux sets NO copy_command (native OSC52), darwin keeps `pbcopy`; `mouse_mode = false` returns native selection/scrollback/Cmd+C to iTerm2 (pane focus keyboard-only Ctrl+j/k). iTerm2 prerequisite: Settings → General → Selection → "Applications in terminal may access clipboard".

- **btop can show STALE PER-PID STATS after PID reuse — the pane is NOT frozen (live 2026-09-16, user-corrected)** — a long-running btop displayed `celery … paperless … 31G, 20 threads` for 45+ min, updating every second, while the kernel proved that PID had Threads=1 and lifetime peak VmHWM 196MB (60d/174 unit-runs of cgroup peaks maxed at 0.82G under 1–2G MemoryMax caps, zero OOMs). Mechanism: this box is a PID-reuse machine (celery forks a fresh pool child every 10 min via max-tasks-per-child, nix builds fork thousands; pid_max 4.2M wraps in weeks) — btop's per-PID cache kept the PREVIOUS owner's stats (MEM/threads/CPU-graph) under the NEW owner's identity (name/user/exe refresh, stats don't). **Proof pattern: starting a SECOND btop instance builds a fresh cache — the bogus row corrects/vanishes there while everything else matches.** Before believing ANY per-process memory alarm from a long-running monitor: cross-check `/proc/<pid>/status` (VmRSS + VmHWM — VmHWM bounds the process's whole life, killing "transient spike" theories in one shot) against a fresh monitor instance. Related trap: `ps` truncates user names to 8 chars by default (`paperless` → `paperles`) — filter with `ps -o user:16`, not a bare awk equality on the user field

- **`~/go/bin` (GOBIN, PATH slot #2) silently SHADOWS every mkLarsPackages tool (2026-09-22)** — stray `go install` binaries there beat the system-managed, lock-pinned copies: `buildflow` resolved to `version dev` (a Sep-20 `go install`) while the system shipped `7e1fbfe` and an imperative `nix profile` copy sat at `557fe59` — three divergent versions, the stray winning. Cleanup: 6 shadows trashed from `~/go/bin` (buildflow, art-dupl, cqrs-lint, golangci-lint-auto-configure, meta, templ; golines kept — no system copy exists), profile emptied (buildflow/govalid were version-identical dupes, sshpass had 0 uses, typst moved to `base.nix`). Doctrine: the user `nix profile` stays EMPTY — LarsArtmann tools come from `mkLarsPackages`, nixpkgs tools from `platforms/common/packages/base.nix`; after any session `go install`s a fleet tool, trash the stray from `~/go/bin`.

### Infrastructure Patterns

- **`backup-coordination` module** — Generic backup monitoring via Prometheus textfile. Add backups to `services.backup-coordination.backups.<name>` in `configuration.nix`.
- **`system-health` module** — Textfile collector for systemd state, memory, GPUActive, CPU%. Pre-computes boolean flags for Gatus (Gatus `pat()` can't do numeric comparison). **User-manager monitoring (2026-08-31):** `monitoredUserManagers` (default: the host's `users.primaryUser`) adds `system_user_units_failed{user}` / `system_user_manager_reachable{user}` / `system_user_units_scrape_errors{user}` via the machined bus proxy (`systemctl --machine=<u>@.host --user`, `timeout 10`-bounded) + the Gatus "User Unit Failures" check — catches USER-unit deaths the system-level checks can't see (smart-audio sat in start-limit-hit the whole 16:38 boot, nothing alerted). Logout ≠ wedge: `/run/user/<uid>` absent → reachable 0, failed 0 (legitimate logout, no alert); present-but-query-failed → scrape_errors 1 (the dnsblockd-:9090 wedge class, alerts).
- **`wrapWithMemoryLimit` helper** — `lib/default.nix` creates `-memlimit` wrappers for dev commands (go-test-memlimit, cargo-test-memlimit, etc.).
- **DynamicUser eval-time assert** — `dynamic-user-audit.nix` cross-references DynamicUser services with sops secrets at eval time. Catches ANY DynamicUser service, not just hardcoded names.
- **NixOS VM tests** — `tests/default.nix` uses `pkgs.testers.runNixOSTest`. `mock-sops.nix` + `test-helpers.nix` provide common mocks.
- **In VM tests, extra mounts MUST be declared as `virtualisation.fileSystems`, not `fileSystems`** — qemu-vm.nix replaces the WHOLE `fileSystems` option via `mkVMOverride` (priority 900, applied whenever `virtualisation.fileSystems != {}`, which is always) so a plain `fileSystems."/mnt/x"` entry silently VANISHES from the guest fstab (no eval error — the mount unit just 'could not be found'). Guest-side bootstrap units that must run before a mount: `wantedBy/before = local-fs.target` + explicit `before = [ "mnt-x.mount" ]` (a mount waiting on a by-label device whose label only exists post-mkfs is a chicken-and-egg — format BEFORE local-fs, `udevadm settle` after). **test-cv FIXED 2026-09-02**: converted to `virtualisation.fileSystems` + a REAL btrfs pool disk (pool-fmt unit, `findmnt -o FSTYPE` assertion) — before that, its `fileSystems."/mnt/pool"` never mounted and every cv-backup assertion ran against a root-fs shadow directory (green but weaker than believed).
- **Attic binary cache** — RS256 (not HS256). DynamicUser → sops owner must be root. No `/api/v1/server-info` endpoint. No `attic token` subcommand — use `atticadm make-token`. Storage on `/mnt/pool/services/atticd/storage` (since 2026-08-18): deliberately NO tmpfiles rule for it (tmpfiles can pre-date the pool mount → dir on root fs); the mount-gated `atticd-storage-dir` oneshot creates it, `RequiresMountsFor` (oneshot + atticd) fails loudly on a detached DAS, deploy.sh restarts the oneshot (oneshot+RemainAfterExit ignores restartTriggers). Related trap: hardened root oneshots that chown foreign numeric owners need `CAP_CHOWN CAP_FOWNER CAP_DAC_OVERRIDE` (+`CAP_DAC_READ_SEARCH` to read 0750 foreign trees) — harden{}'s empty CapabilityBoundingSet makes every rsync chown fail EPERM (caught 2026-08-18 by the migration's checksum verify; sources kept, retry safe)
- **Secret rotation monitoring** — `pocket-id-secret-rotation` checks client secret freshness (90d threshold). Does NOT auto-rotate.
- **Gatus `[BODY].jsonpath.X` unreliable in v5.36.0** — Use `[STATUS]` + `[BODY] == pat(*)` instead.
- **SigNoz alert `target=0` + `above_or_equal` = always true** — `mkRule` has eval-time `validateTarget` assertion. Use `target=1` for "at least one" semantics.
- **SigNoz `up{job="..."}` rules NEVER match — the phantom-green `job`-label class (fixed 2026-08-27)** — the OTel prometheus receiver stores the scrape job as the resource attribute `service.name` (and `server.port` etc.); NO series ever carries a `job` label, and PromQL label matchers CANNOT reference dotted keys (`up{service.name="x"}` → 400 parser error; `up{service_name="x"}` → empty because that label only exists on series where the scraped binary self-reports it). Six rules sat permanently phantom-green (DNS Blocker Down, Docker Daemon Down, ClickHouse Down, Telemetry Collector Down, EMEET PIXY Daemon Down, GPU Thermal) while "Niri Compositor Down" (`niri_running < 1`) false-fired CRITICAL on every headless/SSH-only period. Fixes, all live-verified against the query API: (1) process liveness → `node_systemd_unit_state{name="<unit>.service",state="active"}` below 1 (the ollama pattern — the repo had already learned this once and only converted one rule); (2) endpoint-reachability (process alive but stats API wedged, the 2026-08-27 dnsblockd :9090 case) → `count(up{service_name="<svc>"}) or vector(0)` below 1 — works ONLY for binaries whose OTel bridge self-reports `service_name` (dnsblockd, pocket-id); note the up-series label FLIP: scrape success carries the self-reported labels, scrape failure emits a bare-label up=0 series, so a plain selector goes stale mid-outage (count+vector(0) turns absence into a fireable 0); (3) graphical-session services → the session-aware gates (`niri_desktop_died`, `system_emeet_pixyd_expected_down`) mirroring the Gatus design — never raw `niri_running`; (4) GPU temp → no `node_amdgpu_*_temp` metric exists; use `max(node_hwmon_temp_celsius{chip=~".*c5:00_0"} or ClickHouseAsyncMetrics_Temperature_amdgpu_edge)` — hwmon chip labels are PCI-address-keyed (fragile to this box's post-crash bus renumbering), the ClickHouse async metric name is stable but CH-dependent; the OR covers both blind spots. **Histogram suffix metrics are DOTTED in SigNoz** (`dnsblockd_dns_resolve_duration_ms.sum`, `caddy_http_response_duration_seconds.sum` — NOT `metric_sum`): query them via the `{__name__="metric.suffix"}` selector form or the panel/rule matches nothing (the `{__name__=~...}` regex form in Telemetry Export Failures is the established pattern). Meta-limitation: `system_signoz_alert_rules_healthy` only counts rules (>15) — it can NEVER catch this class; the generic detector is comparing each rule's PromQL against the metrics store, which needs per-rule expectations (absence-based rules legitimately return empty when healthy). **The `signoz-query-lint` flake check (2026-08-27 evening) now rejects the STATIC classes at eval time** — `job=` label matchers, underscore histogram suffixes (`metric_sum`), bare `up{service_name=` selectors without the `count(...) or vector(0)` wrapper, and a maintainable dead-metric blocklist (`node_amdgpu_gpu_temp_celsius`) — over `_signoz-alerts.nix` (comments stripped) AND `dashboards/*.json`. Rule-query verification workflow: `fetch http://localhost:8080/api/v1/rules` for states, `api/v1/query?query=...` for live selector checks, ClickHouse `signoz_metrics.distributed_time_series_v4` (`SELECT count() WHERE metric_name='X'`) for phantom-metric sweeps
- **SigNoz dashboards: removing a panel requires removing its layout item too** — panel JSON lives in `spec.panboards.<uuid>`, but `spec.layouts[0].spec.items[]` references panels by `$ref` path; deleting only the panel makes the v2 API reject the whole dashboard (`references unknown panel "<uuid>"`, HTTP 400) and the provisioner HARD-FAILS the deploy (by design). The 2026-08-27 GPU dashboard panel removal hit exactly this — second deploy caught it
- **SigNoz dashboard LAYOUT OVERLAP is the sibling validator failure (2026-09-17)** — a hand-added panel at `x=2,y=19` while two 6-wide panels already occupied the row made the v2 API reject the update with `spec.layouts[0].spec.items[12] and items[13] overlap` (HTTP 400) and the provisioner unit HARD-FAILED; the script's convergence verifier still printed "OK 7 dashboards" because it checks the name SET, not per-panel success — read the FAILED lines, never the final count alone. Recovery without a deploy: the provisioner's update is just `PUT /api/v2/dashboards/<id>` with the whole file, so fixing the JSON in-repo and PUTting it directly converges the live dashboard and the next provisioner run becomes "Unchanged: skipping". The `signoz-query-lint` flake check now rejects ANY pairwise grid-rectangle intersection across all `dashboards/*.json` (heredoc'd python trap; NOTE: heredoc terminators inside a runCommand `''` string must sit at the block's MINIMAL nix-indent level — nix strips the common indent and an indented terminator never matches, eating the rest of the script with bash "unexpected EOF"); negative-tested via `scripts/negative-test-lints.sh` case `signoz/dashboard-overlap`
- **SigNoz journald logs pipeline (2026-08-16 overhaul)** — the OTel journald receiver (`all=true`, `priority=info`, `start_at=end` — NEVER "beginning", no persistent cursor = full-journal re-ingestion on restart) ingests the WHOLE journal; the `transform/journald` OTTL processor extracts `body=MESSAGE`, `severity_*` from `PRIORITY` (string "0".."7"), and `resource.service.name` (precedence: SYSLOG_IDENTIFIER < `_SYSTEMD_UNIT` < `CONTAINER_NAME` — docker's journald log-driver makes container logs flow with the container name as service). All statements guard on `IsMap(body) and body["PRIORITY"] != nil` so OTLP logs pass untouched; `set(body, body["MESSAGE"])` MUST be the last statement (after it, field accesses return nil). One OTTL statement per list element — the parser does NOT split embedded newlines. The 2026-08 info-level CPU burn (96% CPU, monitor365-server at 900 entries/s) is gone — journal volume is ~5 MB/h (~6 entries/s, 500x below the burn era); journald's per-unit rate limiting (10k/30s) bounds recurrence. Docker's default log driver is `journald` (`default-services.nix`) — existing containers keep json-file until recreated
- **SigNoz's Services page is TRACE-driven — and the signoz-coverage module is the rail that keeps registration honest (2026-08-31)** — a service appears at `/services` ONLY if its binary actively pushes OTLP spans; logs (80+ journald service names) and the 9 prometheus scrape jobs are invisible there. For a month only 6 services ever sent spans (cv-application, crush-daily, browser-history, discordsync, file-and-image-renamer, gotenberg) while overview/PMA initialized a TracerProvider with ZERO span sites ("OTel tracing enabled" in the journal, then silence), papdashboard has OTel metrics but no trace SDK, and fastflowlm carried a pure-noop env var (removed). `modules/nixos/services/signoz-coverage.nix` closes the class: (1) a registry (`services.signoz-coverage.expected`, keyed by systemd unit, `wiring = "env" | "config" | "upstream"`) with eval-time assertions BOTH ways — wiring="env" entries must carry `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` on their unit, and ANY unit setting that var must be a registry key (`untrackedOtelUnits` escape hatch); (2) a 5-min textfile collector reading ClickHouse DIRECTLY (`signoz_traces.distributed_signoz_index_v3` for last-seen spans — `serviceName` column, `toUnixTimestamp64Milli(timestamp)`; logs_v2's `timestamp` is UInt64 NANOSECONDS, `intDiv(max(ts),1e6)`, NOT DateTime64) emitting `signoz_traces_reporting/_missing/_upstream_gaps`, `signoz_logs_pipeline_stale`, `signoz_coverage_scrape_errors`; (3) Gatus "SigNoz Traces Coverage"/"Logs Pipeline Fresh" + three SigNoz self-watch rules. Fail-closed: query failure writes `scrape_errors 1` + `missing = enforced_total` (an `exit` inside the `{ } > "$TMP"` redirect block SKIPS the trailing mv — the file never lands). Upstream gaps stay visible via `signoz_traces_upstream_gaps` without paging; flip wiring "upstream"→enforced when instrumentation lands. **First catch within minutes of shipping**: enabling dnsblockd's `otlp_endpoint` (config key added in dns-blocker.nix) exposed a LATENT upstream bug — its exporter omitted `WithInsecure()`, so every export died `https://localhost:4318 … server gave HTTP response to HTTPS client` (the Go SDK defaults to TLS; the feature had never been enabled by anyone before). Scheme-aware fix RESOLVED 2026-09-13 (task queue): shipped upstream (dnsblockd `70ca2ea`, 2026-09-01, pushed via `8aa005f`), the lock carried it from `94c9cb93` (2026-09-06), the deployed `dnsblockd-94c9cb9` emits spans (live-verified `signoz_traces_reporting{service="dnsblockd"} 1`), and the registry flipped to wiring `"config"` with `maxUpstreamGaps` ratcheted 5→4; bank-sync had already been flipped to `"env"` 2026-08-31 (upstream `901978e`). Remaining gaps (overview, projects-management-automation, papdashboard, hermes) were re-verified never-emitted (`last_span_age -1`) and need real upstream instrumentation first (TODO_LIST tracks them). Event-driven emitters (renamer, gotenberg) get `maxAgeHours = 720` — their spans only exist when work happens; a 26h budget would page on quiet weeks. The `-health` sister unit shares the main unit's serviceName (same binary). **`wiring = "event"` added 2026-09-17 (gotenberg)**: a fourth wiring value for env-wired emitters whose work cadence is UNPREDICTABLE — gotenberg had 0 spans ALL-TIME in the retention window (ClickHouse-verified) purely because no office/email conversion ran in 30d+, and `last_span_age -1` counts as missing REGARDLESS of budget, so any finite budget is a standing false page for a never-seen event-driven service. Event entries keep the registry + reverse-assertion contract (still key members, expected/reporting gauges still emitted), are EXCLUDED from `signoz_traces_missing`, and do NOT inflate `signoz_traces_upstream_gaps` (that count now selects `wiring == "upstream"` explicitly); availability stays owned by the service's own Gatus health check. Flip back to `env` only when the workload cadence becomes regular.
- **SigNoz provisioners must CONVERGE, never delete+recreate** (`_signoz-scripts.nix` v7 pattern) — recreating a rule mints a fresh ruleId, and SigNoz emits a fake RESOLVED (old id) + FIRING (new id) pair per rule per deploy; an `|| true` on DELETE additionally let zombie duplicates accumulate (3 live at diagnosis). The provisioner compares a canonical projection (jq filter normalizing away API-injected fields like `disabled`/`stats`/`legend` in query specs) of desired vs live: unchanged → skip; changed → `PUT /api/v1/rules/{id}` (preserves ruleId — the v1 API supports PUT even though the UI uses v2); duplicates → delete ALL copies by name then create one; removed-from-nix → delete. Every state mutation checks its HTTP status; a final convergence assertion (exact name set, zero dupes, counts match — diff on failure) must pass or the unit fails. Same pattern for the notification channel via `PUT /api/v1/channels/{id}` (create-only-if-absent is not enough when templates change) and for ROUTE POLICIES (see next bullet): one policy per desired ruleId (create-if-absent), delete orphans+duplicates FILTERED BY the `systemnix` tag (never touch untagged — they may be user-created), assert exactly-one-per-rule. DASHBOARDS converge the same way via `/api/v2/dashboards`: native v2 (Perses `schemaVersion: "v6"`) JSONs with a stable slug in `.name` + tag `owner=systemnix`; the v2 GET returns the spec byte-identical to the file (verified), so `jq -S .spec` comparison detects drift exactly; zombie copies = same `spec.display.name` but different slug (the pre-v2 provisioner POSTed a fresh copy per deploy — **251 accumulated** and the new API's strict `DisallowUnknownFields` rejected the old format with HTTP 400 "unknown field title" on EVERY deploy, silently, because dashboard failures were warnings). Dashboard failures are now HARD failures
- **SigNoz Discord alert rendering** — Default alertmanager templates (`discord.default.title`/`.message`, referenced when channel `discord_configs[]` omit `title`/`message`) are label dumps — that is where `prometheusreceiver dev Unspecified…` spam came from. Custom inline Go templates on the channel config are supported and are what we ship. Fired alerts carry labels `alertname`, `ruleId`, `ruleSource` (= GeneratorURL) + rule labels (`severity`); annotations come ONLY from the rule payload's `annotations` field (the top-level `description` is UI-only) — `mkRule` in `_signoz-alerts.nix` sets `annotations.description` with `{{$value}}` (prometheus-style, expanded at rule-eval time). **`{{$value}}`/`{{$threshold}}` MUST be written with ZERO spaces inside the braces**: SigNoz's `preprocessTemplate` (`pkg/types/ruletypes/templates.go`) only special-cases the EXACT strings `{{$value}}`/`{{$threshold}}` — `{{ $value }}` gets rewritten to `{{index .Labels "value"}}` → renders empty. Successful Discord notifies are JOURNAL-SILENT (only warnings log); the cheapest positive delivery probe is a >256-rune alertname, which makes the notifier log a `Truncated title` WARN carrying `receiver` + `ruleId`.
- **SigNoz routes alerts EXCLUSIVELY via route policies — and API-created policies do NOT survive signoz restarts** (2026-08-16 regression: every alert silently dropped for ~30 min) — the custom dispatcher (`pkg/alertmanager/alertmanagerserver/dispatcher.go` → `nfmanager/rulebasednotification/provider.go Match()` → `GetAllByName(orgID, ruleId)`) resolves receivers ONLY through route policies whose NAME equals the ruleId, evaluating an expr-lang expression over alert labels. `preferredChannels` on a rule is NOT a route (it only feeds legacy startup-built matchers keyed to the ruleId AT PROVISION TIME — rule recreation breaks them); `receivers: ["default-receiver"]` in `/api/v1/alerts` is display-only. v1-API rules NEVER get policies auto-created (that path only runs for rules carrying `notificationSettings` from the UI), and — proven live 2026-08-16 — a plain signoz RESTART silently wipes all API-created policies (2026-04:47 deploy recreated 20/20 within 13s via the v6 provisioner). Policy API: `POST /api/v1/route_policies {expression: 'ruleId == "<uuid>"', kind: "policy", channels: ["Discord Alerts"], name: <ruleId>, tags: ["systemnix","auto-provisioned"]}` → 201; `GET /api/v1/route_policies` → `{data: [...]}` (list includes API-created); `DELETE /api/v1/route_policies/{id}` → 204. Rule DELETE does NOT clean name-matched policies (orphans accumulate — the provisioner's tag-filtered orphan pass handles this).
- **SigNoz alertmanager external URL** — `alertmanager.signoz.external_url` in signoz.yaml (factory section `alertmanager:` → `signoz:` → squashed `external_url`). Default is hardcoded `http://localhost:8080` — without this key every ruleSource link Discord shows is localhost. The rules engine bakes external_url into every rule object AT CONSTRUCTION (startup), so the key is useless without a restart: `systemd.services.signoz` carries `restartTriggers = [ signoz.yaml source, web dist source ]`. The web UI IS shipped (`web.enabled = true`, pnpm-built SPA at `/etc/signoz/web` — see docs/status/2026-08-16_21-25), so ruleSource links resolve in-browser
- **SigNoz reads EVERY config at startup only — restartTriggers are mandatory** — `signoz.yaml` + web dist (query service), `collector.yaml` (collector), and `clickhouse-server/config.d/*` (the nixpkgs module has NO restartTriggers for extraServerConfig) all deploy silently inert without them. All three now carry `restartTriggers` (signoz.nix). The 2026-08-16 collector.yaml deploy without a trigger left the OLD config running — the log enrichment "didn't work" until the trigger was added and redeployed
- **SigNoz schema drift: the migrator's squash logic SKIPS gap migrations forever** — traces migration 1010 (widen `span_attributes_keys.tagType` Enum8 to include 'scope') was never applied (no row at all) while 1011-1014 ran after it; `migrate sync up` treats everything below the high-water mark as done and will NEVER apply 1010. Symptom: `clickhousetracesexporter` logs `Could not write a batch of spans to tag/tagKey tables: (tagType Enum8('tag'=1,'resource'=2)) unknown element "scope"` every few minutes — errors are deliberately swallowed by the exporter ("don't want to block the exporter"), so traces still flow but attribute-key registration silently rots. Fix applied manually (2026-08-16): `ALTER TABLE signoz_traces.span_attributes_keys MODIFY COLUMN tagType Enum8('tag'=1,'resource'=2,'scope'=3)` + same for the distributed table + INSERT a `finished` row for 1010. The `Telemetry Export Failures` alert + collector self-scrape (job=signoz-collector, :8888) now surface this class
- **SigNoz scrape coverage (2026-08-16)** — the collector scrapes: node-exporter, cadvisor, caddy (:2019 admin, needs `Host: localhost:2019` — bare `localhost` gets 403), pocket-id, dnsblockd, emeet-pixyd, signoz-collector (self, :8888), clickhouse (:9363 — `<prometheus>` block in extraServerConfig; nixpkgs does NOT enable it by default), docker-engine (:9390, `metrics-addr` in default-services.nix). `up{job="ollama"}` is a phantom (ollama has no /metrics — 404); the Ollama Down rule uses `node_systemd_unit_state{name="ollama.service",state="active"}` instead. Docker Daemon Down watches `up{job="docker-engine"}` (direct signal; the old `up{job="cadvisor"}` proxy kept serving while dockerd idled). Dashboards live in `modules/nixos/services/dashboards/*.json` (generated by /tmp/gen_dashboards.py pattern — deterministic uuid5 panel IDs, one PromQL query per panel — the v2 API requires EXACTLY one query per panel — a TWO-query panel passes nix eval (nothing lints dashboard JSON) and only detonates at provision time: HTTP 400 `panel must have one query, found 2` fails the whole provisioner unit and the deploy (2026-09-16, systemnix-overview panel). Multi-series panels use ONE query with the `{__name__=~"a|b"}` selector form; pre-verify PromQL against the live query API (`http://localhost:8080/api/v1/query?query=…`, no auth, python urllib works) BEFORE committing dashboard JSON)
- **SigNoz DROPS scraped Prometheus exemplars — metric→trace drilldown is manual** (verified live 2026-09-14, evidence: the dnsblockd REPO's `docs/research/2026-09-14_signoz-exemplar-chain-verification.md` (~/projects/dnsblockd — NOT this repo)) — upstream OTel contrib's prometheus receiver demonstrably parses OpenMetrics exemplars (verified in contrib source; the pinned `signoz-otel-collector` fork rev was not independently re-verified — moot either way), but SigNoz's ClickHouse metrics schema (`signoz_metrics.*`) has ZERO exemplar columns (checked `system.columns`), so scraped exemplar trace_ids are silently discarded at export and the UI cannot render dots for ANY scraped metric. Spike→trace drilldown on this stack = Traces page → `service=<name>` + the spike's time window (dnsblockd's spans already flow via `otlp_endpoint localhost:4318`, coverage-enforced). Revisit only when upstream SigNoz ships exemplar persistence; note the deployed dnsblockd (`3be58aa`) predates its OpenMetrics wire fix, so even exemplar-bearing scrapes need a flake update + redeploy first.
- **nvme-cli 2.16 JSON key names** — `nvme smart-log -o json` emits `avail_spare`/`spare_thresh`/`percent_used`/`temperature` (Kelvin!) — NOT `available_spare`/`percentage_used`. The string `available_spare` appears in the binary only as a bit-name inside the `critical_warning` sub-object. A `jq '.[$key] // 0'` fallback turned this rename into weeks of phantom `0` values feeding a false "Spare Blocks Low" alert; collectors must error+skip on missing keys (see `node_nvme_collector_keys_missing` + the Gatus "NVMe Collector Key Integrity" check).
- **Hardcoded `/dev/nvme0n1` in smartd + nvme-health-monitor ENUMERATION-SHIFT trap (fixed 2026-08-31)** — inserting the Samsung 970 EVO Plus internally made IT nvme0 and slid the Lexar system disk to nvme1; `services.smartd.devices = [{ device = "/dev/nvme0n1"; }]` and `services.nvme-health-monitor.device` (default `/dev/nvme0n1`) then silently monitored the BRAND-NEW disk while the QLC system disk (root of everything) lost ALL SMART/nvme telemetry. Both now use `/dev/disk/by-id/nvme-<model>_<serial>` (slot-independent; smartd 7.5 parses by-id for nvme — onecheck-verified). Same class as the sd-letter udev trap: NEVER reference nvme/sd devices by kernel-enumerated names in configs.
- **`journalctl | grep -c` is an IO trap** — Serializes every journal entry through a pipe. Use `journalctl --grep "pattern" --output cat | wc -l` instead — filters inside journalctl. Add `-n N` for early termination once N matches are found. Affected: monitor365-server-watchdog, niri-health-metrics (every 30s!), niri-health.sh, niri-drm-healthcheck.sh.
- **`journalctl --grep` WITHOUT `--since` is STILL an IO trap under a small MemoryMax (2026-08-31 sev1 page)** — `system-health-metrics`' oomd counter ran `journalctl -u systemd-oomd --grep "Marked.*for killing" --output cat | wc -l` with NO time bound: it walked the ENTIRE 7.2G journal with page cache charged to the unit's 128M cgroup — reclaim thrash made one run take 11-28 MIN against a 2min timer, the textfile went stale most of the time, and sev1 paged "system_health metrics missing/stale" on the desktop all afternoon. EVERY journal counting query needs BOTH: a `--since` window sized to its semantics (24h ≈ 10s here) AND `timeout N` as the hard ceiling (fail closed via a `*_scrape_errors` gauge, totals held at last-known — `system_oomd_kills_scrape_errors` is the pattern, Gatus asserts it 0). **`--since` WITHOUT `timeout` is the same trap (2026-08-31 evening recurrence, 23:23-23:40):** the forgejo mirror-error scan HAD `-30 min` but NO timeout — under I/O pressure (movie playback off the same DAS USB link) four consecutive collector runs hit the 3min unit timeout, wrote NO textfile, and the sev1 bridge fullscreen-paged SYSTEM MONITORING STALE on every flap cycle mid-movie. Now `timeout 30` + fail-visible scrape_errors; the docker block (`docker info/ps/inspect` can hang on a wedged daemon) got `timeout 15`/`timeout 10` the same way. When a bounded-section collector still hits its unit TimeoutStartSec, the WHOLE textfile write is lost — every IO-capable section's timeout must sum comfortably under the unit ceiling. Same lesson in `btrfs-health.nix`: `btrfs scrub status /` hung 1h+ on a kernel-side ioctl wedge right after boot — ALL btrfs ioctls (`filesystem usage`, `scrub status`, chunk-check) are `timeout 30`-wrapped now, and the gc-guard timeout fails CLOSED (UNALLOC defaults to 0 → abort GC). **journalctl exit-code trap (same incident):** journalctl exits **1 when NO entries match the filter** (0 when entries shown, ≥2 real error) — under `set -o pipefail` that 1 propagates through `… | wc -l` and reads as a scrape failure. Treat 0 AND 1 as valid counts, only ≥2/timeout as failure: `out=$(timeout 60 journalctl … | wc -l) || status=$?` then `[ "$status" -le 1 ]`. Also: `| sort -rn | head -8` needs `|| true` — head's early close SIGPIPEs sort (exit 2, "fflush failed") and pipefail+errexit kills the whole collector unit into OnFailure (census section, live 17:13).
- **gawk treats an unopenable input file as a FATAL error — `awk '… END {…}' /proc/[0-9]*/stat` dies SILENTLY and emits NOTHING when a pid vanishes between glob expansion and open (2026-09-11 phantom-metric deploy block)** — END never runs, stdout stays EMPTY (not 0), exit 2, and the trailing `2>/dev/null || true` hides the evidence completely. The system-health `STUCK_DSTATE` scan hit exactly this: whenever a process exited mid-scan, `system_stuck_dstate_processes` went ABSENT for that 2-min collector cycle (the `[ -n "$VAR" ]` fail-closed emission gate suppresses the line), and pre-deploy §10 failed intermittently — the 05:41 gate probe saw absence, the 05:43 collector run saw `0`. gawk-verified: `fatal: cannot open file` aborts before END (nixpkgs awk = gawk 5.4.1). Fix: feed the glob through `cat … 2>/dev/null | awk …` — cat absorbs vanished entries as warnings and awk always reaches END, making the emission deterministic. Regression: `tests/test-scripts.nix` `awk-vanished-input` (dangling-symlink fixture reproduces the race deterministically + a static tripwire on the module source). Rule: NEVER pass a shell glob over churn-prone paths (`/proc/*`, `/sys/*`, `/tmp/*`) directly to awk as file arguments.
- **The global `DefaultTimeoutStartSec=3min` (timeout-audit.nix) IS live — the 2026-08-31 "PHANTOM" diagnosis checked the WRONG PATH (root-caused same evening)** — nixpkgs renders `systemd.settings.Manager` into `/etc/systemd/system.conf` (the WHOLE file), NOT into a `system.conf.d/` dropin; the original diagnosis looked at `/run/current-system/etc/systemd/system.conf.d/` (absent by design) and concluded the global was not live. Verified live: `grep DefaultTimeoutStartSec /etc/systemd/system.conf` → `3min`, and the RUNNING manager reports it (`systemctl show -p DefaultTimeoutStartUSec` → `3min`). Keep explicit per-unit `TimeoutStartSec` anyway for collectors/guards — 3min is still a long time for a textfile collector to sit wedged, and explicit ceilings document the expected runtime.
- **Post-deploy smoke test** — `nix run .#post-deploy-check` verifies functional outcomes, not just liveness. Hard FAILs on silent-zero regressions.
- **Prebuilt ELF binaries in Go modules break FOD purity** — Use `buildGoModule` with `subPackages` or vendor via `fetchurl`.
- **`*_templ.go` files MUST be committed** — Templ-generated Go files are required by Nix builds that vendor source without running `templ generate`. The global `~/.config/git/ignore` previously had `*_templ.go` (removed in `platforms/common/programs/git.nix`). Per-repo `.gitignore` patterns must also not ignore them. If a templ project fails to compile in Nix with "undefined: someFragment", check that the `*_templ.go` file is tracked by git. **Enforced at three layers:** `scripts/check-templ-committed.sh` (pre-commit + CI here), go-nix-helpers `checks.templ-committed` (eval-time throw in EVERY go-standard consumer's `nix flake check` — the flake source contains only tracked files)
- **`dontConfigure = true` silently skips `pnpmConfigHook`** — `pnpmConfigHook` is registered in `postConfigureHooks`, not `buildPhase`. Setting `dontConfigure = true` skips the entire configure phase, so the hook NEVER runs. The build proceeds with empty `node_modules` and fails with `ERR_PNPM_META_FETCH_FAIL` (DNS blocked in sandbox). Fix: remove `dontConfigure = true` and let `pnpmConfigHook` handle `pnpm install --offline --frozen-lockfile` automatically. Build phase should only run `pnpm build`
- **`buildGoModule` names binaries after the source dir, not `pname`** — `subPackages = ["cmd/server"]` produces `$out/bin/server`, not `$out/bin/systemd-graph`. `meta.mainProgram` is metadata only — it doesn't rename the binary. Always `ls $out/bin/` after building and `postInstall = 'mv $out/bin/server $out/bin/<pname>'` if the name doesn't match what `lib.getExe` expects
- **`fetchPnpmDeps` doesn't honor `sourceRoot`** — For pnpm in a subdirectory (e.g., `webui/` inside a Go repo), pass `src = "${finalAttrs.src}/webui"` to `fetchPnpmDeps`, NOT `sourceRoot`. `fetchPnpmDeps` runs `yq` on `pnpm-lock.yaml` from the `src` it's given
- **Python scripts with `#!/usr/bin/env python3` need `python3` in `runtimeInputs`** — Under `harden {}`, the service PATH only includes what's in the wrapper's `runtimeInputs` plus the system PATH. If `python3` is not explicitly added, `shutil.which("python3")` or `env python3` fails with exit 127. Always add `pkgs.python3` to `runtimeInputs` for any shell-wrapped Python script

---

## Build & Deploy

```bash
nix flake check --no-build  # Validate syntax (fast)
nix run .#deploy            # Build + deploy via nh
nix run .#pre-reboot-check  # Boot-chain audit BEFORE any planned reboot (self-elevates)
nix fmt                     # treefmt + alejandra
```

**Run `nix run .#pre-reboot-check` before every planned reboot** — it statically audits the whole boot chain (loader default entry → kernel/initrd on ESP → `init=` on the LIVE store → profile anchoring → initrd-required devices → btrfs health → zombie mounts → GC anchoring §10 → **boot-mirror §11**) and BLOCKS on the 2026-09-07 stuck-boot class (default entry whose store paths live only on a retired store). Exit 0 = safe; advisories never block.

**Samsung 2nd boot disk (2026-09-18, `platforms/nixos/system/boot-mirror.nix`)**: `/boot-mirror` mounts the Samsung p1 ESP (`SAMSUNG-EFI`, 4G, nofail — mirror is additive, QLC chain stays primary-managed) and `boot-mirror-sync.service` (oneshot, deploy.sh provisioner-restarted + multi-user-pulled) keeps it a VERIFIED byte-mirror of the QLC `/boot`: `bootctl --esp-path=/boot-mirror --variables=no install` (loader binary + EFI/BOOT fallback + per-ESP random seed; NEVER touches NVRAM) + rsync `--delete` (`/loader/random-seed` stays per-ESP — receiver-side exclude protects it) + a `diff -r` gate that FAILS the unit on any tree drift. `/nix` already lives on the Samsung, so after `nix run .#boot-mirror-activate` (idempotent: creates the `Linux Boot Manager (Samsung)` EFI entry and orders it FIRST; QLC entries stay fallback) the firmware boots loader+kernel+initrd+store entirely off the QLC — only root `@` remains on it. pre-reboot-check §11 grades mirror issues FAIL once the mirror is first in BootOrder, WARN before activation. Rollback: firmware boot menu (F8/F11/F12) or `efibootmgr -o` with the QLC `Linux Boot Manager` id first. The seconds-wide window between a switch and the post-switch sync restart can boot the PREVIOUS generation from the mirror — safe (its store paths live ≥3d). nixpkgs `mirroredBoots` is grub/extlinux-ONLY (verified 2026-09-18) — do not "simplify" the unit away. Stale mirror: `systemctl restart boot-mirror-sync`.

`nix flake check` skips `aarch64-darwin` by default (the "incompatible systems" warning is EXPECTED — the dms-shell/quickshell input is Linux-only and fails Darwin eval intentionally). Do not add `--all-systems` to CI.

For contributor style, module templates, and verification commands, see [docs/CONTRIBUTING.md](./docs/CONTRIBUTING.md).

---

## Platform Constraints

**Darwin:** 256GB SSD 90-95% full, 24GB RAM. `nix-collect-garbage` hangs; clear caches before builds. Never add packages that build from source >10min. HM config is minimal — no terminal/editor/theme parity with NixOS.

**Samsung 970 EVO Plus 1TB (internal, BLANK since 2026-08-31, role PENDING):** inserted internally as nvme0 (PCIe 3.0 x4 = its max), slid the Lexar to nvme1 (see the by-id smartd trap). Measured (raw, `scripts/bench-disk.sh`): 4K QD1 randread 35.3k IOPS / 27.8 µs, randwrite 79.2k / 12.2 µs, QD32 304k IOPS / 1.19 GB/s, fsync 0.78 ms, seq 2.4/2.7 GB/s — vs the QLC root under the same load: 620/295 IOPS QD1, fsync ~200 ms. Design doc: `docs/planning/archived/2026-08-31_samsung-role-assignment-first-principles.md` (proposed: 64 G XFS hot-DBs + ~860 G BTRFS `/nix`; models/Steam stay on QLC `/data` — 589 G of AI models are bandwidth-bound, not latency-bound). `/data` composition (2026-09-06): ai 291 G + models 210 G + Steam 106 G (incl. 2.5 G `steamapps/compatdata` — 3 Proton prefixes; the ONLY stateful Steam data, NFS Hot Pursuit Remastered confirmed local save paths under `pfx/.../Documents` + `Saved Games`) + llamacpp 92 G; `/data/docker` IS the live Docker data-root (verified 2026-08-31 via `docker info`: overlay2 on btrfs; ~15.5G per `docker system df` 2026-09-25 — images 9.1G + volumes 4.3G + build cache 2.1G, ~64% reclaimable — see the Snapshot-pinning doctrine table) — the earlier "docker lives in `/var/lib/docker` on root; /data/docker EMPTY" note was STALE, do not repeat it. **/nix filesystem DECIDED (2026-08-31, deep research + on-target benchmarks `scripts/bench-nix-fs.sh`): BTRFS `noatime,compress=zstd`.** Performance is a wash between ext4/XFS/BTRFS for nix workloads (load variance ±40% swamped all inter-fs deltas; fsync ~equal ≈3 ms under load); the separator is measured compression: **1.89×** on this box's real store content (compsize: 129 G → ~68 G) — matching danieldk's 1.88× discourse report; nix disabled `preallocate-contents` by default precisely to enable btrfs compression (PR #4094). NOT compress-force (36% of store data is correctly heuristic-skipped as incompressible). ZFS excluded (5 s sqlite txg-sync stalls), f2fs excluded (power-loss eats open files).

**GPU (NixOS) — GTT-first (2026-09-02):** the BIOS UMA carveout is **1 GiB — the BIOS FLOOR, no lower value is settable on this board** (user-confirmed 2026-09-05 boot 13:16; the 2026-09-02 "512 MiB" target was never achievable — 1 GiB is canonical, MemTotal = 124.3 GiB verified, carveout ~89% in use. BIOS-only setting — reboot required to take effect; the 2026-08-19 "18 GiB" flip had silently reverted to 32 GiB before this; "Auto" ALSO resolves to the full 32 GiB — verified 2026-09-05 boot 12:54: VRAM total 32768 MiB, MemTotal 93.8 GiB, GTT total = whole visible RAM while pages_limit 120G sat ABOVE it, inverting the TTM safety margin — ALWAYS set an explicit value, never Auto). ALL real GPU memory is GTT (= shared system RAM, ~124 GiB visible post-flip): HIP/ROCm allocations fall back to GTT automatically, so model weights land in system RAM and the tiny carveout only holds display scanout. Kernel knobs: `amdgpu.gttsize` is GONE in kernel 7.x — `ttm.pages_limit` is the only GTT ceiling (120 GiB in `boot.nix`; mechanics VERIFIED from the running 7.2.2 source 2026-09-05: kernel default is totalram/2 — ttm_device.c:96 — and our 120G is a deliberate ~96% oversubscription for GTT-first; amdgpu_ttm.c:2156 sets the GTT DOMAIN SIZE = pages_limit, clamped to physical RAM on APUs — which is why GTT total read 96058 MiB (min(120G,93.8G)) on the 32 GiB-carveout boot and 122880 now; ttm_tt.c:388 enforces it as a SOFT eviction trigger — forced `ttm_global_swapout` while over the limit, then the allocation PROCEEDS anyway when nothing is evictable, i.e. it is NOT a hard cap and reserves nothing. Real effect: GPU clients' accounting (KFD/ROCm) tops out below physical RAM so they fail with clean driver-OOM instead of eating the last pages. It has never fired in any incident here — the freezes were shmem/PSI/zram class) and `ttm.page_pool_size` MUST stay 24 GiB (pool = ceiling is what re-created the GPUActive black hole, 2026-07-12). `OLLAMA_GPU_OVERHEAD=1073741824` (1 GiB) biases ollama's model accounting — on this APU hipMemGetInfo reports the GTT pool (≈ all system RAM); the hard bound is the service cgroup MemoryMax=32G. Memory fractions are per-service, not system-wide. `HSA_OVERRIDE_GFX_VERSION=11.5.1` + `HSA_ENABLE_SDMA=0` are in `environment.sessionVariables` (via `rocmEnv` in `ai-stack.nix`) so ANY ROCm application launched from an interactive shell detects the gfx1150 GPU. Without `HSA_OVERRIDE_GFX_VERSION`, ROCm does not officially support gfx1150 (RDNA 3.5 / Strix Halo) and silently falls back to CPU — the GTT-backed GPU stays unused. `llama-server-rocm` is a wrapper that additionally bakes in `LD_LIBRARY_PATH` for ROCm runtime libs; use it instead of bare `llama-server` for standalone llama.cpp inference. The bare `llama-server` binary (from `llama-cpp-rocwmma`) also works via session vars since Nix RPATH resolves its ROCm deps, but `llama-server-rocm` is belt-and-suspenders for JIT-loaded (dlopen) ROCm libs. Monitoring: SigNoz "GPU GTT Critical (>85%)" replaced the VRAM-% rule — a VRAM-% rule pins near 100% forever on the 1 GiB carveout (false-fire class); the amdgpu textfile collector now emits `node_amdgpu_mem_info_gtt_*` alongside the vram pair.
